The Vajra Peak, the Tantra of the Great Secret Yoga (Vajraśikharamahāguhyayogatantra)

The Vajra Peak, the Tantra of the Great Secret Yoga, known in Sanskrit as the Vajraśikhara Mahāguhyayoga Tantra and in Tibetan as Sangwa Naljor Chenpo'i Gyü Dorjé Tsemo, is Toh 480 in the Kangyur, where it belongs to the Tantra section among the scriptures of the yoga tantra class. Opening with homage to Samantabhadra, to all the tathāgatas and to the Tathāgata Vairocana, it presents the buddhas supplicating Vajrasattva, the lord of the vajra family, to explain the great way of yoga and the maṇḍalas of the buddha families from which the various rites arise. Over its chapters it sets out the secret practices of the families of the tathāgatas, the maṇḍalas, mudrās and rituals associated with them, and the subjugation of the three worlds, and it closes with a hymn of one hundred and eight names praising Vajrasattva in his wrathful form. It is closely related to the cycle of the Compendium of the Reality of All Tathāgatas, and according to its colophon it was translated into Tibetan by the Indian preceptor Karmavajra together with the translator monk Shönu Tsultrim.

In the language of India: vajra śikhar mahā gutya yoga tantra

In the language of Tibet: The Vajra Peak, the Tantra of the Great Secret Yoga.

oṃ Homage to Samantabhadra. Homage to all the tathāgatas. Homage to the Tathāgata Vairocana.

Then King Vajradhara,
supreme over all, lord of existence,
having examined that wheel,
the yogin remained without speaking.
All the buddhas, the great protectors,
the sages, supreme beings without exception,
made supplication to Vajrasattva,
the great king, the lord of sages:

"Accomplishing the great way of yoga,
from which the various rites truly arise,
source of the practice of the family maṇḍalas:
please explain this supreme wheel.
Protector, you are of great fortune;
you attend to the aim of what is to be done.
You know the secret and know the intended meaning;
you are proclaimed supreme among those who know yoga.
Possessing reality and the reality that follows it,
very self of yoga, great yogin,
great vajra, mind of awakening:
speak, speak, O great way!

The wheel of the family maṇḍalas,
the manifold self of vajra yoga,
the secret essence, the great maṇḍala,
that which accomplishes the way of the Dharma:
of phenomena that are free of conceptual thought,
tell what this meaning of dharma is.
What is the ritual procedure here?
What is the meaning of accomplishment?
Why is this the later tantra?
Please tell what the meaning of tantra is.
Why is it tantra, and why the later one?
How did the meaning of tantra arise?

What is this fierce Vajradhara?
How did the meaning of fierce arise?
What is this peaceful vajra?
For what reason is it called peaceful?
Why is he Vajrapāṇi?
And what, too, is Vajracaṇḍa?
For what reason is he called ferocious?
For what reason is he called wrathful?
That which is spoken of as vajra, vajra:
why is it called vajra?
What is this that is called Vajradhara?
What is his vajra like?
How many are the vajra holders?
To whom do all the vajra families belong?
In what abode does the great one dwell?
Tell how many kinds of family there are.
For what reason are they called families?
What is this, the nature of the family lord?
For what reason is he called a sattva?
How many kinds of perfect awakening are there?
Why is he Vajrasattva?

Tell what Vajrarāja is.
What is the meaning of this Vajrarāga?
Tell what Vajrasādhu is.
Why is there Vajraratna?
Tell what Vajrateja is.
Vajraketu and the great Vajrahāsa:
speak! Tell what they are.
For what reason is there Vajradharma?
Tell what Vajratīkṣṇa is.
Vajrahetu, the great wheel,
tell what Vajrabhāṣa is.
Vajrakarma and the great yakṣa,
Vajrayakṣa, the great fierce one,
Vajrarakṣa and the great fist:
speak! Tell what they are.

From what did these sattvas arise?
Likewise, who are the father and the mother?
What is the first, and what the occasion?
What is the objective condition?
From what did this secret arise?
From where did these all-pervading lords arise?
What is this without beginning or end,
of which the great Lord of Secrets has spoken?
Utter joy itself, utter excellence itself:
what is the lord of the vajra heart like?
For what reason is he Samantabhadra,
and likewise vajra, and Vajradhara?
How did the first family arise?
Since all phenomena are unborn,
how can arising be possible?

Tell what a bodhisattva is.
Why should one be called a buddha?
Tell what a tathāgata is.
Tell what the conquering in Blessed One means.
Likewise, what is the teaching of the Teacher?
For what reason is it called gnosis?
Tell what wisdom and gnosis are.
Why should it be called wisdom?
Likewise the perfection of wisdom:
what is its way, and what its samādhi?
What are meditative concentration, yoga, and the yogin?
What is the meaning of the three existences and the three worlds?
And for what reason the three realms?
What is the meaning of saṃsāra?
Tell also what nirvāṇa is,
permanence and destruction, and likewise
tell what the Great Vehicle is.
And why is there space?
Tell, too, what earth itself is.
From what do the great elements arise?
What is cessation, and by what is it brought about?

For what reason is it called mudrā?
Why should it be called mantra?
For what reason is it called vidyā-mantra,
and likewise the dhāraṇī-mantras?
Tell the characteristics of mudrā;
the characteristics of mantra should be told;
tell the characteristics of vidyā-mantra,
and likewise of the dhāraṇī-mantras.
What is the basis of the root mudrā?
How is it to be bound, great sage?
When the mudrās are bound,
how are all the vajra holders pleased?
How should the vidyā-mantras be recited?
Great Sage, tell this!
When the vidyā-mantras are recited,
how is the vajra recited with the mind?"

This is the brief presentation of the set of questions.

"What is oṃ explained to be?
Tell what the syllable hūṃ is.
For what reason the syllable phaṭ?
Tell what svāhā is.
Why should it be spoken in offering?
Likewise, the knowing of the samaya too:
for what reason is it a maṇḍala?
What is the rite of the maṇḍala like?
For a man or else a woman
who first enters the maṇḍala,
what are the tasks to be done?
Speak, great Lord of Secrets!
To one who has entered the maṇḍala of the Sage,
how should the vow be given?
What is it that he must not abandon
even for the sake of his life?
How should the act of bathing be done?
Likewise, binding the directions,
together with binding the obstructors,
how should the vināyakas be expelled?
With what great joy,
with deep reverence, should prostrations be made?
Incense and flowers, and likewise
lamps, offerings and the rest: how are they to be?
How is the maṇḍala to be examined?
And how is one to be led in?
What is the samaya explained to be?
What is the rite of the vajra disciple?
How should the preliminary approach be done?
What is the meaning of accomplishment?
What is the secret yoga like?
What kind of absorption is it?

What is the meaning of summoning into one's presence?
What, too, is the attachment here?
What is the act of binding?
How do the guides become pleased?
How, here, is the request to depart made?
How should the supreme maṇḍala be taken up?
How, too, should the work for beings be done?
How should the tasks of the rite of activities be done?
What is the dense virtue, the great secret?
How are the various activities to be done?
How are the mudrā and the answering mudrā performed?
How are offering and the return of offering received?
How does the wheel of the maṇḍala arise?
How many varieties of wheel are there?
How are entity and nonentity abandoned?
What are the supreme one born of the secret, and the secret of light rays?

Tell how many kinds of fire offering there are.
What is the hearth like?
What is the condensed fire offering?
As to how the activities arise,
tell how many kinds of activity there are.
How many kinds of secret are there?
How is the supreme secret spoken of?
What is reality like?
What are the mudrā and the secondary mudrā?
How is the offering mudrā?
Through the yoga of the family deity,
how is what is to be accomplished thoroughly accomplished?
How is the subtle vajra spoken of?
How should obstructors be removed?
In brief, what are the vidyādhara
and the master like?
Tell how many kinds of wheel there are
endowed with maṇḍala and wheel.
What are the various ritual procedures
that accomplish the marks of siddhi?
The lines, and permanence itself:
how are the siddhis described?
What is the collection of siddhis like?
Speak the compendium of all secrets.
What is the rite to be performed
for conferring empowerment on oneself?
Or else, upon others,
how is empowerment conferred?
How should the mind be generated?
What kind of samādhi is it?
This knowing of reality at all times:
what is vajra gnosis like?
Further, what is the intention like?
What is the conduct like?
Arising from any sign whatever,
the mark of knowing name and namelessness:
from what is it taught that there is an agent
without action and result, and a producer?
The body that arises like space:
from what should it be said to arise?
For what reason is it called liberation?
Tell what the meaning of yogin is.
What is the yoga of the subtle vajra
that generates the vajra sphere?
Further, from one's own families,
how is the observance of yoga undertaken?
In brief, what are bringing under control
and the secret of reality?
The secret of the condensed Great Vehicle,
taught in the great ritual procedure:
with its many intentions,
speak the secret, O great intellect!
Tell how many kinds of yoga there are.
How many arise from vajra yoga?
How is it called the root tantra?
What is the arising of the secret family like?
It is self-arisen itself:
the three realms, the worlds beneath the earth,
upon the earth and above the earth, nirvāṇa,
and the other secrets as they have been explained.
The vajra bearer himself is Viṣṇu himself,
the Sun, the Moon, Yama, and Rudra himself;
he himself is Prajāpati, he is father and mother.
How can such contradictory words be valid?"

This is the brief presentation of the second set of questions.

"Brahmā, the great lord Maheśvara whose body is the three worlds,
the six-faced one born of the host, the maṇḍalas of the divine families,
and further, whatever planets, hosts and the rest there may be:
how can it be valid to say that I am their very self?
'I am desire and liberation;
I am the hell-being of the hells;
I am the self of pretas and of animals':
how can those words be valid?

Then, further, those whose minds are attached to desire,
holding views, full of words, attached to the views they grasp,
the tīrthikas, the Lesser Vehicle, those who delight in existence, the families of creatures,
understand the meanings just as they please;
the various family maṇḍalas as they have been explained,
the source of pure dharma, they do not know as intended;
by their own wishes they conceive wrong extremes.
For their sake, great Lord of Creatures, speak out of love!
Great one worthy of offering, abode endowed with the supreme,
supreme lord of the secret, please analyze the compendium.

Before the secret maṇḍala of the Lord of Creatures,
how should one offer with samaya offerings?
That one who sports with the five sense pleasures
will attain the great, supreme collection of siddhis,
that the siddhi of the family arises from base desire:
how can such words of delight be valid?
Likewise, how is the bhaga, and what are its signs like?
And how, too, does one enter it?
How are those substances fully prepared?
How are they base within the three worlds?
Then, that siddhi is attained by clinging to desire
because desire is pure: how can that be valid?
That freedom from attachment is evil, and that for one free of attachment
the teaching of the secret becomes actual evil: how can that be?
How is supreme merit attained through the torma?
How is there merit in killing creatures?
That by taking what is not given there comes merit:
how can such contradictory words be valid?
That by relying on a woman of great joy
supreme buddhahood is accomplished in this very life,
and that by relying on impure desire buddhahood
and Vajrasattva arise: how can that be?
That here there is no merit equal to lying:
how can lying be called meritorious?
That here one should kill other beings and eat them:
how can such contradictory words be valid?
That even renouncing saṃsāra becomes non-dharma,
that devotion to nirvāṇa is not dharma,
that following the yoga of the buddhas
becomes non-dharma: how can that be valid?

Endowed with arrogance, avarice and a hating mind,
practicing non-dharma and attached to false words,
one accomplishes yoga by fierce attachment to the three existences,
and by desire's craving one is supremely victorious:
how is it accomplished through the yoga of the family?
One who desires the siddhi of the unsurpassed state
should take up with devotion the pure and wondrous yoga.
By drinking handfuls of urine and eating excrement
one seeks the pure and supreme siddhi of yoga:
by eating and drinking impure excrement and urine,
how is the longed-for siddhi to be gained as siddhi?
Practicing dharma together with a woman,
both joined as one, one makes pure and clean offering:
how does one worship the maṇḍala together with a woman,
and how does it become the supreme, excellent siddhi?

Further, those born of the vajra family, complete in all,
who perform supremely unbearable, fierce deeds,
are pure, exceedingly lovely, and go on high:
how can that contradiction be valid?
Further, those devoted to beating and killing,
who perform expulsion and fierce deeds,
are pure, exceedingly lovely, and go on high:
how can that contradiction be valid?
Further, one devoted to eating and drinking,
who seeks purity by tearing out hearts,
is pure and lovely and goes on high:
how is that contradiction valid?
Further, by stealing precious jewels
one is lovely and steadfast, pure and goes on high;
and further, by devotion to pride and avarice
one becomes pure: that is a contradiction.
Further, one in whom the knowledge of action has arisen
should do all nonvirtuous actions:
in doing the many kinds of deeds,
sexual union and the rest, how does no fault arise?
Further, by false words and likewise by sexual union,
those who seek purity, though not noble, desiring
the great power of the Victor by means of the secret, like nectar,
are lovely and go on high: how can that be valid?

O Protector, you are the mass of pure dharma; you are ever in yoga.
O Protector, you who teach the nectar of great bliss are the source of the secret of the true Dharma.
O Victor, like the stainless sun you thoroughly cleanse all unknowing,
all-pervading one whose nature is the Victor's, great guide of the three worlds, bodhisattva, feast of joy!"

This is the summary.

Supreme over all, the knower of all,
the omniscient one who has abandoned the three stains,
bodhisattva, buddha, great king,
that very Tathāgata
arose from his meditative absorption
and gazed in every direction.
The Sage bowed deeply to the protectors
and spoke thus to all the buddhas:

"Oh, the embodiment of pure wisdom!
Oh, buddhahood attained!
Oh, this wheel, the dancer,
inquires into its own wisdom!
I shall explain the supreme secret
that gathers together all the rites.
I shall open the lid of the precious casket,
sublime and very hard to find.
I shall raise the sublime banner
of unsurpassed wisdom.
Having opened the precious casket,
I shall illuminate it with wisdom.
Then I shall truly explain
the sublime, the supreme secret:

the great means of accomplishing activity,
the compendium of all rites,
which distinguishes the varieties of the secret
and unravels the intent of yoga;
the compendium of all rites
that makes known sublime reality,
the rite of vajra yoga
and the rite of the yoga of the wheel;
the compendium of all rites
of that which is called the Supreme Secret;
endowed with activity pervading the three times,
the source of all secret yoga,
the compendium of all rites
of the great means for every activity;
endowed with the rite of buddha yoga,
fully entering Vajradharma,
the compendium of all rites
of the great secret called the Three Bodies;
endowed with the rite of vajra yoga,
taming all the savage ones,
the compendium of all rites
called Trailokyavijaya, Victory over the Three Realms;
endowed with the rite of dharma yoga,
giving rise to the lotus dancer,
the compendium of all rites
called the Taming of Beings;
fully endowed with the four kinds of giving,
giving rise to the jewel wheel,
inspiring confidence in bodhisattvas,
the compendium of all rites;
the great means of all the buddhas,
giving rise to the dancer of many forms,
the compendium of all rites
of the rite of karma yoga;
knowing reality and knowing the intent,
making known the supreme great way,
the Supreme First, the supreme buddha:
the compendium of all rites.

Entering them in due order,
it alone performs the work of beings.
Great activity, supreme and peaceful,
through the supreme Dharma and enjoyment:
this wheel of the buddha dancer,
supremely wondrous,
I shall tell how, according to fortune and according to the secret,
it comes to tame sentient beings.

Long ago, in times past,
having taken up the mind of awakening,
when he had become a bodhisattva
endowed with method and wisdom,
with great enthusiasm he practiced
immeasurable austerities of great wonder.
In due order he
duly attained everything up to empowerment.
Having reached the far shore through great wisdom
and having obtained the empowerment of the cloth,
the one of great intelligence came to dwell
in delightful Ghana Akaniṣṭha.

The yogin thought,
'I shall become a supreme buddha.
Having brought my work to completion,
I shall attain what brings about the welfare of beings.'
Contemplating that work, he thought,
'Before and after there is no great bliss;
I shall practice the austerities
of the great marvel, the joy of Dharma.'
Seated at the foot of the tree of awakening,
he took on every hardship.
He stopped the breath going out and coming in;
without thought and without movement,
his body did not stir at all
and he did not move his limbs.
And so, in the unmoving state,
he remained for a long time.

The lords of sages of the ten directions,
all the buddhas, considered:
'This is the time of perfect awakening;
it is not fitting to practice suffering.
Therefore let us all go together
to rouse him.'
With this thought they all set out
and came to Ghana.
Having come, all the sages
made the supreme exhortation.
With the vidyā oṃ vajra uttiṣṭha
they snapped their fingers
and roused the bodhisattva completely.
When he looked in every direction,
he saw an ocean of buddhas.
The Sage bowed to the protectors
and requested, 'Please give me instruction.'
All the buddhas, the great protectors,
declared with a single voice:
'Examine your own mind closely:
what is the domain of mind like?'

Having heard the words of all the buddhas,
he examined his own mind,
and when he had remained so for a long time
and had not seen any form of mind,
he bowed deeply with all his limbs
and asked, 'Blessed Tathāgatas,
I do not see any form of mind.
What is the characteristic of mind?'
Then all the buddhas said,
'The domain of mind is hard to know.
We shall give you the heart mantra;
practice its subtle recitation.'
oṃ citta prati vedhaṃ karomi
Having taken up the pure heart mantra,
as he examined his own mind
and remained in that for a moment,
he saw it as the maṇḍala of the Moon.

Then he asked, 'What is this mind?
Is it one that bears the seeds of affliction?
Is it a virtuous or a nonvirtuous mind,
or a mind that bears the seeds of the ground of all?'
'Having realized what is concordant with the cause of the Dharma,
relying on the six perfections,
this is the purity of sentient beings,
not an abode of thorough affliction.
For a long time the great accumulations
of merit and wisdom have been gathered.
Just as this full Moon
is pure, clear and free of turbidity,
so, because the accumulations are complete,
the mind is like the full Moon.
It is no thing, neither existent nor nonexistent:
how could it be the same as the Moon?
Yet the completion of great merit and wisdom
is taught as being like the Moon.'

Endowed with the accumulations of good deeds,
having seen the maṇḍala of the Moon that is his own mind,
joy and delight arose in him,
and once again he spoke these words:
'Blessed protectors, I have seen
the maṇḍala of the Moon that is my own mind,
completely purified of the stains of affliction
and free of grasped and grasper.'
All the buddhas said,
'This mind that has first arisen is excellent.
In the beginning, the adventitious afflictions
are purified by the mind of awakening.
With this pure heart mantra,
which entirely abandons all things,
contemplate the mind of awakening
in the center of the maṇḍala of the Moon.'
oṃ bodhi citta utbādayāmi
Having taken up the pure heart mantra,
when he contemplated in the center of the mind,
in the center of the Moon he saw
yet a second maṇḍala of the Moon,

and he spoke these words to the sages:
"I see the second maṇḍala."
Then all the buddhas said,
"The nature of the mind of awakening is firm.
In order to make it firm,
accomplish it well.
We shall give you the heart mantra;
recite it and contemplate it as firm."
oṃ tiṣṭha vajra
Contemplating the great vajra
that abides in the center of the second maṇḍala,
and examining it closely for a moment,
he saw that it was utterly firm,
and said, "Perfect buddhas,
I see a vajra in the center of the maṇḍala.
I see the center of the second
maṇḍala of the Moon endowed with a vajra."
Then all the buddhas said,
"Great vajra of all the deities,
recite this utterly firm mantra
that gives rise to the vajra sphere (Vajradhātu)."
oṃ vajra ātmako 'haṃ
Having taken up the pure heart mantra,
he meditated on the vajra body.
Through that, he obtained the vajra body,
solid and firm, without any hollow,
and said, "Perfect buddhas,
I see myself as a vajra body."
Then all the buddhas said,
"Meditate on yourself in the form of a buddha."
oṃ yathā sarva tathāgatāstathā ahaṃ
Having taken up the pure heart mantra,
by meditating in the form of a buddha
he attained omniscient wisdom
endowed with the supreme of all aspects.
Bowing deeply to all the buddhas,
he said, "Blessed Tathāgatas,
protectors, bless me
and make my awakening firm."
All the buddhas of great renown,
having heard the words of the Vajradhātu,
the protectors, entered his heart
and proclaimed the yoga of the vajra:
oṃ sarva tathāgata abhisamabodhi dṛḍha vajra banadha
When all the buddhas of great renown
uttered the great vidyā-mantra,
the Vajradhātu became utterly firm,
and he fully awakened to the wisdom of reality.

Then all the buddhas
came forth from the vajra heart,
and with the four empowerments of the Dharma
the Sage was then empowered.
The empowerment of the mind of awakening
conferred by Vajrasattva:
whatever possesses the mind of awakening as its fruit,
that is the empowerment of Vajrasattva.
The giving of three countless eons,
the excellent fruits of giving,
and the obtaining of jewels equal to space:
that is the empowerment of the precious jewel.
The austerities practiced for others
throughout three countless eons,
and the fruit accomplished through diligence:
that is the empowerment of Vajrakarma.
The essence of the Tathāgatas,
the great way of the sphere of reality,
unsullied by the mud of affliction:
that is the empowerment of the vajra lotus.
These four empowerments of the vajra yoga of the perfected mind of awakening are the sphere of reality.
Then, further, there are the secret empowerments,
the assembly of sublime qualities that is the nature of a buddha.
Through the empowerment of Vajrasattva,
it is held to be the essential body.
Through the empowerment of Vajradharma,
it is held to be the body of perfect enjoyment.
Through the empowerment of Vajraratna,
likewise, come the major and minor marks.
The great wisdom of all the buddhas,
equal to space, with the nature of reality as its essence,
through the empowerment of Vajrakarma
is held to be the emanation body.
Through the empowerment of the four secrets
one attains the awakening of a perfect buddha.
Then, through the empowerment of the name,
that Sage became Mahāvairocana.

He also truly realized the nature of reality;
Dharma and enjoyment are to be understood as complete.
All dharmas are without substance,
without signs and without manifestation,
without characteristics and like space,
free of any object of reference,
without intrinsic nature and inexpressible;
all dharmas are not the domain of words.
Yet it is examined further as a secret,
and therefore here it is called realization.
Of dharmas that are free of conception,
what is this meaning of the Dharma? It is said:
selfless, they do not exist as things;
dharmas are nonexistent and free of conception.
Yet the nature of reality is truly realized,
and Dharma and enjoyment are realized as complete.
Realization is said to be experience,
and here it is realized as a wonder.
All dharmas are without form,
yet here they are also shown as having form.
All dharmas are not the domain of words,
yet here they are also shown as sound.
Therefore, in this wondrous Dharma,
the rite is declared to be a rite.
Why is this a wondrous Dharma?
Because it accomplishes that secret reality:
the six actions,
the powers of embodied words,
and the wonder of the vows for the sake of the fruit.
Therefore this is a wondrous Dharma,
and therefore this is called a rite.
Therefore, in this wondrous Dharma,
the rite alone is the rite.
And what is the rite here?
For the sake of all sentient beings,
having generated the mind set on awakening,
to rely on the practice of the six perfections:
that itself is the rite.
And what is the meaning of accomplishment?
To accomplish the ground of Perfect Joy,
with the selflessness of persons and of phenomena,
which abandons grasped and grasper:
that is the meaning of accomplishment.

Why is this the later tantra?
"Tantra" means continuity,
and saṃsāra is held to be a continuum.
"Later" means what lies beyond,
and so the tantra is called "later."
Tantra means continuity:
the later arises from the earlier.
That secret reality is said to be hidden,
and so the tantra is called "later."
Why is it "tantra" and "later"?
The supreme Victor who has reached the end of the grounds
has crossed beyond the prison of existence;
or else, whoever has crossed over,
that is explained as the later tantra.

How did the meaning of tantra arise?
For the benefit of all sentient beings,
the Dharma and enjoyment of the buddhas,
urged on by the compassion of the buddhas:
that is the true arising of the meaning of tantra.
The Dharma and enjoyment
that arose from the minds of the buddhas,
the great secret of all the buddhas:
that is the true arising of the meaning of tantra.

What is this fierce Vajradhara?
For the sake of the hosts of vicious beings
who are not tamed by the form of a buddha,
one's own wisdom, the mind of awakening,
truly arises as the fierce vajra.
How does the meaning of "fierce" arise?
The fierce one, with his fierce eyes,
is shown as the fierce wrathful one.
Those who engage in fierce deeds
are to be corrected and turned back.
What is this peaceful vajra?
Having tamed all those vicious ones,
he truly arises once again as peaceful.
That very king, Vajradhara,
teaches the means for the welfare of beings.
The Blessed Vairocana
is shown as the lord of the wrathful ones.
He is both peaceful and fierce,
Vajrapāṇi of great miraculous power.
Why is he called peaceful?
The great yogin of manifold forms,
solely in order to ripen beings,
regards with love those made vicious by affliction,
and so the meaning of "peaceful" truly arises.

Why is he Vajrapāṇi?
In order to crush the evils of vicious affliction,
he holds in his hand the vajra,
the excellent nature of the mind of awakening;
therefore he is called Vajrapāṇi, Vajra in Hand.
Likewise, the māras of the three times
and all adverse forces cannot destroy
the vajra that is the nature of the mind of awakening,
the assembled teaching of all the buddhas.
Then, endowed with all the buddhas,
he always holds such a great secret vajra in his hand,
and therefore he is called Vajrapāṇi.

What is the meaning of Vajracaṇḍa?
Vajracaṇḍa is the great yakṣa;
Vajrayakṣa, utterly firm,
devours the noose of pride and affliction,
the self-arisen bodhisattva.
Why is he called ferocious?
"The great ferocious, vicious and fierce one
is I alone, the self-arisen,
who cut the noose of existence."
The meaning of "fierce" is "ferocious."
What is vajra wrath, it is asked?
Killing and driving away are fierce deeds,
and that is said to be the meaning of wrath.
"I blaze like vajra fire
and burn up all the forces of affliction."

"Vajra, vajra," it is said:
why is it to be called vajra?
It is solid, with a core, without hollowness;
it cannot be cut or destroyed;
it cannot be burned, for it is indestructible.
So emptiness is to be called vajra.
The mind of awakening that abandons
the wheel of the six destinies
and tears evil apart is the vajra;
it clears away all afflictions.

What is this that is called Vajradhara?
Whatever is the wisdom of all the buddhas
and the firm mind of awakening,
that is the king Vajradhara,
the very self of all the Tathāgatas.
What is his vajra like?
Free of all conceptual thought,
having abandoned all grasping at dharmas,
the suchness of all dharmas,
emptiness, is called vajra.
Whatever is the nature of dharmas such as form,
the unchanging suchness,
the firm dharmadhātu:
such is this vajra.

What is the meaning of the vajra family?
True and meaningful suchness,
unchanging and unborn,
unceasing reality:
such is the vajra.
That which has become a family
of such meaningful unchangingness
is called the bodhisattva family;
it is called the vajra family.
The Dharma without beginning or end
is called the bodhisattva.
The mind of awakening that is true and undeceiving:
that is the family.

How many are the vajra holders?
The wise bodhisattvas
who pervade all directions everywhere
are, like the dharmadhātu, without end:
that is how many vajra holders there are.
To whom do all the vajra families belong?
To the unsurpassed lord of the Dharma,
Vairocana, the perfect buddha.
What that protector has fully embraced
is said to be his maṇḍala.

Who is the teacher of these?
Who is the one who instructs after him?
What are his conduct and his activity?
It is said in the Compendium of All Secrets:
"Its teacher is the mind of awakening;
the one who instructs after is wisdom and means;
the mudrās are its conduct;
the mantras are its activity."
What is the family of Vajradhara?
The family of all those vajra holders
is the mind of awakening,
the bodhisattva of excellent discipline.
That itself is also the domain of the buddhas.
In what great abode does it abide?
In all dharmas there is no abode;
it is the domain of the rootless mind.

All dharmas are without form;
they are the dharmadhātu, with no domain of activity.
How many kinds of family are there, it is asked?
The permanent, the Imperturbable, the Lord of Jewels,
Amitābha, and the accomplishments:
the families are taught to be of a hundred kinds,
and gathered in brief they are of five kinds:
buddha, vajra, jewel,
Dharma and karma, the five kinds.
Why are they called families?
By the distinction of supreme and lesser,
what are called families are of two kinds.
The family of beings who have entered bad paths
is said to be the lesser.
To abandon the beings of the bad paths
and enter the mind of awakening is Dharma.
Therefore the vajra family is spoken of,
and that family too is the fifth.

What is the nature of the lord of the family?
The mind of awakening is the great king,
Vajrasattva, who is without fear,
the great protector, lord of the family;
that family too is the fifth.

Why is he called a sattva, a being?
What are called beings are of three kinds:
the childish, likewise the wise,
and likewise the bodhisattvas:
beings are to be spoken of as of three kinds.
It is also said:
Why is one called a being?
What is a being? The childish is spoken of.
Why is there the wise being?
What is a bodhisattva, it is asked?
Whatever state one is to be spoken of as being in,
one is called a being of that.
Toward Dharma or what is not Dharma,
toward good deeds and bad deeds,
one is obscured by the darkness of unknowing,
and grasps dharmas as affliction and as self;
one becomes of a lesser and deluded family.
Such a one is called childish.
Attached to all the afflictions,
and likewise to existence and the three lower realms,
attached to the darkness of unknowing:
that one here is to be called childish.
Who is the wise being?
One who has passed beyond stains and abandons affliction,
endowed with yoga and of great discipline,
who destroys the cataract of unknowing:
that one is called a wise being.
Here, who is the bodhisattva?
One who has realized the reality of the Dharma,
has awakened from the abode of saṃsāra,
and awakens sentient beings:
that one is called a bodhisattva.

How many kinds of perfect awakening are there?
Awakening is of three kinds:
lesser, supreme and middling.
The lesser is the mind of the śrāvakas;
the middling is the awakening of the pratyekabuddhas;
the supreme is the mind of awakening,
and that is the unchanging vajra.
Whatever establishes body, speech and mind
is to be called supreme awakening.

Why is he Vajrasattva?
Whatever is the wisdom of supreme awakening,
that is definitively called vajra.
That which arises from the vajra of wisdom
is called Vajrasattva.
The king of all the vajra holders,
why is he called a being?
Vajrasattva, the great protector,
that great joy: is he attached, or what?
In order to free all sentient beings
he is attached to sentient beings;
therefore that king Vajradhara
is proclaimed as Vajrasattva.
Whatever is the fruit of the mind of awakening,
the unsurpassed wisdom of a buddha,
that is the nature of the vajra mind:
Dharma, enjoyment and emanation.

What is Vajrarāja, it is asked?
He is the king of all the vajra families,
and is called the vajra hook.
The vajra of summoning and of bringing down
is called Vajrarāja.
The four means of attraction
that the Blessed One practiced
are called the means of summoning beings,
and truly arose as Vajrarāja.

What is the meaning of Vajrarāga?
Pleasing all the buddhas,
he is called passionate devotion.
Vajrarāga, the great māra,
is called Vajrarāga.
When, to ripen sentient beings,
the Blessed One practiced the conduct of awakening,
the passionate devotion to the conduct of awakening
truly arises as Vajrarāga.

What is Vajrasādhu, it is asked?
Pleasing all the buddhas,
he bestows the cry "Excellent!"
and delights all the bodhisattvas;
that is called Vajrasādhu.
He delights pure beings
and accomplishes the vajra holders;
he gladdens wise beings
and satisfies the holders of the secret.
For the sake of the body of Dharma and perfect enjoyment,
the six dharmas of this Blessed One,
which he accomplished in former times,
truly arose as Vajrasādhu.

Why is there Vajraratna?
The giving of a bodhisattva
is like a jewel equal to space;
Vajraratna, of great fortune,
is called Vajraratna.
It is the empowerment of the great being,
the empowerment of all beings;
by the empowerment of the essence of awakening
he upholds the heirs of the Victor.
Whatever is the giving of all the buddhas
truly arose as a vajra equal to space.
That is the jewel that is meaningful,
and it is called Vajraratna.

What is Vajrateja, it is asked?
He destroys the cataract of ignorance,
the darkness of unknowing of saṃsāra;
the light of liberation, of great splendor:
that is called Vajrateja.
Why is there Vajraketu?
The banner is the nature of all the buddhas;
going on the path of liberation is the banner;
the banner of the Dharma is the great banner:
that is called Vajraketu.
What is Vajrahāsa, it is asked?
The great yogin, Vajrahāsa,
makes all the buddhas laugh
and brings relief to all the bodhisattvas:
that is called Vajrahāsa.

Why is there Vajradharma?
The great yogin, the Dharma of the buddhas,
which makes buddhas and sentient beings realize,
the pure essence of the Tathāgatas:
that is called Vajradharma.
What is Vajratīkṣṇa, it is asked?
Sharp wisdom, great primordial wisdom,
cuts the most painful afflictions,
and since it also cuts the obscuration of the knowable,
it is called Vajratīkṣṇa.
What is the meaning of Vajrahetu?
Through the dance of the buddha wheel
the wheel of Dharma is firmly established;
it arises from the essence of the taste of the true Dharma:
that is called Vajrahetu.
What is Vajrabhāṣa, it is asked?
The great Dharma of all the buddhas
is equal to space and free of words;
it truly arises as the word from the wordless:
that is called Vajrabhāṣa.

Why is there Vajrakarma?
Whatever is the activity of all the buddhas
for ripening and freeing beings,
and the activity of the buddhas that is selfless:
that is called Vajrakarma.
What is Vajrarakṣa, it is asked?
It is the protection of the bodhisattvas,
which abandons the Lesser Vehicle
and protects against falling from the peak:
that is called Vajrarakṣa.

What is the meaning of this Vajrayakṣa?
He brings all evil to an end
and decisively cuts off all afflictions;
the great yakṣa who devours existence:
that is called Vajrayakṣa.
What is Vajramuṣṭi, it is asked?
True suchness, not other,
whatever is unchanging reality,
the wisdom of the suchness of the buddhas:
that is called Vajramuṣṭi.

Whatever formerly became the mind of awakening
truly arose as Vajrasattva.
The four means of attraction
truly arose as Vajrarāja.
Ripening beings is Rāga;
the conduct of awakening is Sādhu.
These, called reality, the great samaya,
truly arose from the mind of awakening.
Whatever was the former practice of giving
is called Vajraratna.
Giving fearlessness to all beings
is called Vajrateja.
Loving-kindness toward all bhūtas
truly arose as Vajraketu.
The gift of the Dharma is Hāsa.
These are taught as the four empowerments.
Whatever is the meditative absorption of a perfect buddha,
from that Vajradharma arose.
The great vajra that cuts off afflictions
truly arose as Vajratīkṣṇa.
The higher perceptions, the powers and so forth
are called Vajrahetu.
The accomplishment of speech and the rest
truly arose as Vajrabhāṣa.
Great wisdom, great Dharma,
the great joy that is called samaya,
the pure, manifest realization of wisdom:
these truly arose from Vajradharma.
The king, the perfection of diligence,
truly arose as Vajrakarma.
Because he abandons the Lesser Vehicle,
he is called Vajrarakṣa.
Against the great graha of afflicted existence
Vajrayakṣa truly arose.
That manifest awakening to the wisdom of reality
is called Vajramuṣṭi.
These are the four samayas of action.
The great-souled being of action,
Vajrakarma, who arises from the wheel of action,
arose from that.

Further, from the generating of the mind of awakening
Vajrasattva truly arose.
That mind whose nature is vajra
arose in four forms:
freeing, being freed,
consoling, and establishing in nirvāṇa.
Sattva, Rāja, the great Rāga,
and Vajrasādhu truly arose.
From the accumulations of merit and wisdom
Vajraratna is proclaimed.
That jewel whose nature is merit
is of four kinds, just as was explained.
What is spoken of as the accumulation of wisdom
is called Vajradharma.
That Dharma whose nature is wisdom
also truly arose in four forms.
The perfection of diligence itself
is called Vajrakarma.
That action whose nature is diligence
also truly arose in four forms:
what are called undertaking and application,
discernment and accomplishment.
These four actions of diligence
arose from it in sequence.

From what did these beings arise?
Vajrasattva and the rest likewise
truly arose from the mind of awakening.
The vajra bearers are held to have arisen
from the practice of the six perfections.
Likewise, who are the father and the mother?
The mother is the perfection of wisdom;
the father is skill in means.
From the lotus come the heirs of the buddhas.
From what did the vajra bearers arise?
Vajrarāja and likewise Rāga,
Vajraratna and Ketu,
Vajradharma and likewise Tīkṣṇa:
these are taught as chiefly wisdom.
Vajrasattva, Vajrasādhu,
Teja and likewise Hāsa,
Hetu and Bhāṣa, the great beings:
these arose from means.
Vajrakarma and Vajrarakṣa,
Yakṣa and likewise Muṣṭi,
are known as wisdom and means,
and are explained as the assembly of the great steadfast ones.

The chief of all the vajra families,
Vajrāṅkuśa, is explained as the mother;
Vajraratna, the supreme,
and Vajrasattva likewise are the father.
The father is Vajrarāja, the cause;
the mother is Vajratīkṣṇa.
From them arose the lotus holder
of the great and excellent lotus family.
Vajrateja is of the jewel,
and Ketu is shown to be the mother.
From them arose the jewels
of the excellent precious gem.
The father is the king, Vajrakarma;
the mother is Yakṣa, the protector.
From them arose the actions
of the excellent family of action.

What is first, if it is asked?
The first of all things is the seed;
the first of all the vajra bearers is mind.
Aspiration, mindfulness and meditative absorption
are explained as the first of all the families.
And what is the occasion, if it is asked?
It truly arose from the four families:
the great compassion of those whose nature is compassion,
endowed with the great abodes of Brahmā,
is the occasion.
What is the object of reference, if it is asked?
It is without conceptual thought and without object of reference.
The six supreme perfections
of the mind of awakening that first arose
are explained to be its object of reference.
What is the condition, if it is asked?
Having seen sentient beings,
who always delight in the three existences,
fall into suffering,
one is urged on by the mind of awakening
endowed with compassion.

Where did this secret arise?
In delightful Ghana Akaniṣṭha,
the fearless Densely Arrayed buddha field:
from there arose this mode
of fully enjoying the Dharma of the buddhas.
From where did these all-pervading lords arise?
From the lovely celestial palace
of great and supreme vajra jewels,
endowed with the Dharma of the buddhas:
from there these all-pervading lords arose.
What is this that is without beginning or end?
The great Lord of Secrets spoke:
The awareness of all sentient beings,
buddha-nature,
and the great vajra of the dharmadhātu
are explained to be without beginning or end.
What is this Perfect Joy itself?
For the sake of fully accomplishing great bliss,
the unsurpassed awakening of the buddhas,
Vajrasattva, the great king,
is explained to be Perfect Joy itself.
What is Utter Excellence itself like?
The vajra, supreme among all things,
is called the mind of awakening.
The vajra of the excellent one who gives birth to buddhas
is explained to be Utter Excellence itself.
What is the Lord of the Vajra Essence like?
Those to be trained, the supreme noble persons,
are called the vajra essence.
The king who is their lord
is explained to be the Lord of the Vajra Essence.
Why is he Samantabhadra, the All-Good?
Whatever births in existence there are for beings
who possess the state of a bodhisattva,
since all of them are always good,
he is to be called Samantabhadra.
What are the vajra and the holder of the vajra?
Whatever is the mind of awakening, that is the vajra.
Whatever is the vajra, that is the vajra holder.
Vajrasattva, the great king,
is called the vajra and Vajradhara.
How did the first family arise?
The one of great renown who was perfectly awakened
through the awakening of all the buddhas:
that is the arising of the first family,
and it is from the family of suchness.
Or else, the very learned who,
having passed through incalculable eons,
attain the ground of Perfect Joy:
that is the arising of the first family.
All phenomena are unborn,
so how could arising be possible?
By realizing and knowing just this,
all phenomena are free of conceptual thought.
From the selflessness of phenomena and of persons
that family is held to arise.
No phenomena whatsoever are born;
phenomena do not come to cease.
The complete pacification of conceptual thought:
that itself is the great buddha family.
What is a bodhisattva? Tell it.
One who realizes all phenomena,
who has awakened from the state of saṃsāra,
and who brings sentient beings to realization:
that one is called a bodhisattva.
Why is one to be called a buddha?
Suchness is true and utterly stable.
That which, having realized reality,
is called the realization of all phenomena:
because of that realization one is called a buddha.
What is a Tathāgata? Tell it.
One who abides correctly in the reality of phenomena,
who never again changes into anything else,
and who teaches the path of that reality:
that one is called a Tathāgata.
What is the Blessed One (the Buddha)? Tell it.
Karma and afflictions, likewise birth,
likewise the afflictive and cognitive obscurations,
whatever phenomena are adverse factors:
because he has destroyed these, he is called the Blessed One, the Destroyer.
What is his destruction? Tell it.
The Blessed One is likewise said to have destroyed.
Cutting off the attachment of desire,
crushing and vanquishing the afflictions:
that is called his destruction.
Likewise, what is the Teacher of teachers?
One who cuts off afflictions, māras and the rest,
and by great skillful means protects
all beings without exception from existence in the lower destinies,
is called the Teacher of teachers.
Why is it called primordial wisdom?
Knowing reality is primordial wisdom;
it does not again conceive of it as a phenomenon.
It abandons permanence and annihilation,
and likewise primordial wisdom is deathless.
What is wisdom-gnosis? Tell it.
It knows many aspects,
and through realization it frees from suffering.
That which abandons the phenomena of complete affliction
is called wisdom-gnosis.
Why is it to be called wisdom (prajñā)?
Joined with the prefix pra, it becomes knowing,
and it realizes in a special way.
The wisdom that knows truth and suchness
is explained as wisdom, as knowing.
What is the meaning of the perfection of wisdom?
It is said to be higher than the wisdom
of śrāvakas, pratyekabuddhas and outsiders.
Directed toward suchness, gone beyond:
it is explained as the perfection of wisdom.
What does the word mode (naya) mean? Tell it.
Na has the great meaning of negation.
Since it is neither nonentity nor the absence of entity,
that in which there has come to be no stain
is called its mode.
What is the meaning of this, meditative absorption? Tell it.
Abiding thoroughly in the equality
of all phenomena being without entity,
relied upon above all by those who have wisdom,
it is to be called focusing on the mind.
Why is it called meditative concentration?
It abandons all suffering
and utterly crushes the net of afflictions.
Thinking that is joined with wisdom
is the supreme destroyer of existence.
What is the meaning of yoga? Tell it.
Through that Dharma which is vast and sublime
and supremely profound,
the union endowed with knowing all and understanding all
is to be called yoga.
What is a yogin? Tell it.
One who thinks by means of wisdom,
who contemplates yoga and the aim of yoga,
and who thinks of the welfare of all sentient beings:
that is said to be the meaning of yogin.
I am the great king of all,
knowing all and granting all,
the first great Vajradhara.
By this cause of bliss,
likewise I am the three worlds,
the supreme knower of the yoga of the three realms;
likewise saṃsāra and nirvāṇa,
the higher destinies and the lower destinies,
and I am the one who enjoys the five objects of desire.
What is the meaning of this, the three existences?
Whatever is the mind of all sentient beings
is to be called the three existences:
of desire, of form and of the formless.
Thus, O supreme knower of all,
there is no self, nor is there any agent,
and phenomena too do not exist;
yet all is obscured by the habitual tendencies
of doing virtue and nonvirtue.
Phenomena are of beginningless time,
and there is no agent at all.
Through possessing a mind impaired by conceptual thought,
one comes to see cause and result.
The all-ground (ālaya) containing all seeds
gives rise to all phenomena.
Imprinted and endowed with habitual tendencies,
that is called the three existences.
As the cause of the three realms born of the karma of desire,
that very all-ground appears;
it is also the basis of all the phenomena
of the beings of form and the formless.
Because all phenomena are completely pure,
they are free of every fault.
The essence of Dharma, arisen without beginning,
becomes afflicted by adventitious stains.
I alone am the great Dharma;
I am explained to be the essence of Dharma.
Therefore I am the great king;
I am proclaimed to be the three existences.
What are the three worlds? Tell it.
The world of desire, likewise of form,

and the beings of the formless:
these are called the three worlds,
and they are just I myself, the self-arisen.
What is called desire is the great Dharma.
In order to seek purity,
one discerns, in the light of suchness,
what are called the phenomena of apprehended and apprehender.
Because I am inseparable from the dharmadhātu,
I purify those phenomena.
Therefore I am the great king,
shown to be the lord of the three worlds.
As with desire, so with form,
and likewise with the formless, it is proclaimed.
Inseparable from emptiness,
I am explained to be the lord of the essence.
What is the meaning of the three realms?
The desire realm, likewise the form realm,
and the formless itself:
these excellent three realms
are just I myself, the self-arisen.
This is the realm, the cause and the meaning.
The cause of the three realms is intention.
Emptiness and signlessness are explained,
and wishlessness is shown to be I myself.
What is the meaning of saṃsāra?
The childish arise everywhere,
and likewise they circle again and again.
Saṃsāra is pure in its nature,
and it is just I myself, the self-arisen.
A phenomenon in which saṃsāra exists
does not exist even as an atom.
Because it is unreal and imputed,
it is without beginning or end.
Whatever is nonexistent is empty,
and whatever is emptiness is I myself.
Therefore saṃsāra itself
is proclaimed to be I myself, Vajradhara the king.
What is nirvāṇa? Tell it.
That which is suchness, permanent and empty,
the excellent nature of the four truths,
which has entirely abandoned characteristics, entities and form,
and signs as well,
which cannot be grasped by words or sound,
and in which there is no conceptual thought:
therefore, by the word nirvāṇa,
I alone am to be fully known.
What is the meaning of its permanence?
Permanence itself, the source of the permanent,
that which is free of conceptual thought:
just that permanence
is proclaimed by the word nirvāṇa.
And what is extinction? Tell it.
That which does not enter saṃsāra,
and which stops the impermanence of all things
and the arising of suffering:
that extinction is the word nirvāṇa.
And what is purity? Tell it.
It is neither existent nor nonexistent,
neither an entity nor a nonentity,
neither what is expressed nor what expresses it.
Purity is nirvāṇa itself.
What is the Great Vehicle? Tell it.
That by which all the buddhas have gone,
which is certainly victorious over all vehicles,
the sentient beings who have become genuine:
this is taught to be the Great Vehicle.
The entry into powers and activities,
the gatherings, and the wonders of yoga:
what is called vast is the Great Vehicle,
the supreme wonder of all the buddhas.
What is the meaning of space?
And what is the earth? Tell it.
That which has no elements and no substance,
no birth and no death,
the supreme state free of the things of the three times,
is taught by the word space.
Being born again and again,
and likewise the nature of all the afflictions,
free of the countless and the immeasurable:
that is to be called the earth.
From what do the great elements arise?
What is their cessation, and by what is it brought about?
From ignorance, the afflictions and the rest,
the great elements truly arise.
The all-ground, which contains all seeds,
is held to be the nature of outer and inner.
The transformation of the basis,
in which the seeds of the all-ground no longer arise,
and which is free of all conceptions:
that is the cessation of the great elements.

From the Vajra Peak, the great yoga tantra, the supremely secret and hidden king of great rites, the compilation of all the rites of the dense assembly of the utterly secret, this completes the first of the explanations, the explanation called the Explanation of the Sixteen Sattvas, from the sixth.

Why is it called a mudrā?
Whoever has bound a mudrā,
by that one all harmful creatures
and all sinful beings
will always be sealed.
Why is it to be called a mantra?
The secret mantras summon
and drive out all the harmful.
Mantras crush the afflictions,
and they are the invitation of the Dharma of the buddhas.
If one asks why it is called a vidyā-mantra:
it tames the darkness of attachment,
it thoroughly tames the afflictions as well,
and it turns back ignorance.
So I have rightly called it vidyā-mantra.
What are dhāraṇī-mantras? Tell it.
They serve as the support of the teachings,
they utterly crush the side of existence,
they bring the attainment of the qualities of the buddhas,
and they also give a support for virtue.
The characteristic of a mudrā is stated:
it signifies the conduct
of all the buddhas, of the bodhisattvas
who keep their vows,
and of all the vajra holders.
The characteristic of mantras, too:
they are the mind of all the buddhas,
they accomplish the essence of the Dharma,
and they are truly endowed with the dharmadhātu.
This is said to be the characteristic of mantra.
The characteristic of vidyā-mantra was spoken:
that which pacifies the suffering experienced in saṃsāra
and the mass of faults such as attachment,
together with their kinds,
is said to be the characteristic of vidyā-mantra.
Likewise, of the dhāraṇī-mantra:
that which holds the qualities of the buddhas,
which holds the teachings,
and which holds what is called virtue
is said to be the characteristic of dhāraṇī-mantra.
What is the basis of the root mudrā?
How is it to be bound, great sage?
When the mudrās are bound,
how are all the vajra holders pleased?
First of all one should bathe,
and likewise bind the directions;
one should also bind the vināyakas
and put on clean clothes.
In villages and districts,
in cities and market towns,
in their midst one should
never bind the mudrās.
In a temple, or a hermitage,
or in mountain caves,
in a clean place, secluded in various ways
and free of bustle,
there one should sit cross-legged.
One should take the auspicious cross-legged posture,
just as the bodhisattva
took it at the seat of awakening.
There one should close the eyes
and set teeth upon teeth.
The two lips too should be made even.
Having made mindfulness one-pointed,
one should recite the mantra that joins the unjoined
and bind the great mudrā.
Through binding a mudrā of that kind,
all the vajra holders are pleased.
How should the vidyā-mantra be recited?
Maheśvara, the Secret One, spoke:
When reciting the vidyā-mantras,
just as one recites the vajra with the mind,
one should take the cross-legged posture as before.
With mindfulness and mind undistracted,
one should recite the vidyā-mantras
and also contemplate the divine body.
Not with the lips, nor with any one of them,

not with the two palates either,
not with the tongue and throat,
nor ever at the place of the heart.
With an extremely subtle mind
one should draw the mind within the nature of reality.
When recitation is done in this way,
it is the vajra recitation done with the mind.
What is oṃ explained to be?
It is supreme, it gives wealth, glory and prosperity,
it is endowed with the aspects of good fortune;
it has the meaning of a pledge and of auspiciousness,
and it is the essence of the jewel holder.
What is the syllable hūṃ? Tell it.
It cures and crushes suffering,
it cuts the noose of existence,
it terrifies and slays the harmful.
hūṃ is to be remembered as supreme.
What is the syllable phaṭ? Tell it.
It crushes the harmful and suffering,
and it draws bliss to oneself.
phaṭ is the sound of crushing:
it crushes the foe together with his allies.
What is the word svāhā? Tell it.
It pacifies all wrongdoing,
it increases the store of virtue,
it carries off misdeeds,
and it is the supreme crusher of all suffering.
Why is it to be called offering?
It is offering because one makes offerings.
Where one is fearless about what is to be kept secret,
one's offerings become manifold.
It is not an outer offering:
this is offered by all the buddhas,
and likewise it is supreme for liberation.
Those secret offerings
of the fearless children of the Victors,
the nature of all the buddhas,
the incomparable secret of accomplishing the rite
by their own sons,
are ever supreme for the vidyādharas.
Likewise, knowing the pledge:
whatever one has entered,
that is knowing the pledge.
Habituating oneself to the pledge
is also said to be knowing the pledge.
One becomes equal to the mind of awakening
that has passed through incalculable eons.
One who has entered the secret pledge
is said to know its pledge.
Why is it a maṇḍala?
Because the essence, the quintessence of the sublime Dharma,
arises from it, it is called a maṇḍala.
The great maṇḍala is the great secret,
the great Dharma thoroughly taught.
What is the rite of the maṇḍala like?
The rite is Dharma and yoga;
from it secret liberation truly arises.
It is supreme for accomplishing Perfect Joy
in this very life.
What is to be done
by a man or a woman
who has entered the maṇḍala?
Speak, great Lord of Secrets!
Going for refuge to the Three Jewels,
generating the great mind of awakening,
and likewise the supreme practice:
these are to be made utterly firm.
To one who has entered the maṇḍala of the Sage,
how are the vows to be given?
You must not kill living beings.
You must not take what is not given.
You must not engage in sexual misconduct out of desire.
You must not speak falsehood.
You must give up drinking liquor,
the root of all faults.
Discard all that is not to be done,
and above all tame sentient beings.
Serve the holy ones
and honor the yogins.
In order to accomplish the knowledge of yoga,
attend upon the learned.
The three kinds of bodily action,
the four kinds of speech,
and the three kinds of mind also,
guard as best you can.
Do not despise the master:
the guru is equal to all the buddhas.
Mantras and mudrās, too,
you must never despise.
Whatever symbolic seals there are,
the wheel, vajra, lotus and the rest,
you must not step over them,
nor must you eat them.
Likewise, the shadow of the goddesses and deities
you must not tread on with your feet;
you must not sit upon it,
nor set anything down on it again.
The excellent emblems of the deities
and the symbols of the deities' mounts
you must not step over or despise.
Do not harm sentient beings.
What is it that one must not abandon
even for the sake of one's life?
Even for the sake of the life by which one lives,
do not abandon the mind of awakening.
The vajra, the bell and the mudrā
you must never abandon.
Your faith in the Three Jewels, too,
you must in no way give up.
You must not give up the vows.
You must not torment yourself.
How is the act of bathing to be done?
Having abandoned the ten nonvirtues,
relying on the ten virtues,
and wishing to benefit beings:
this is held to be the inner bathing of oneself.
In order to purify oneself completely
and to seal oneself with the mudrā,
one correctly binds all the mudrās:
this is called the bathing of the yogin.
Accepting the five secret baths,
the bath of water is to be performed.
This act, the bath of conceptual thought,
is to be done by the vajra masters.
Or else the bathing done by the rite,
supremely wondrous, shall be explained.
First one puts on one's armor,
and in order to wash oneself
one should also call to mind the wrathful one.
Accomplishing every aim,
the two syllables are the excellent armor.
Vajrayakṣa, the great fierce one,
and the wrathful Amṛtakuṇḍalin.
oṃ vajra kīli kīli vajra hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ ṭuṃ: this is the armor's.
oṃ hūṃ ā: this is Vajrayakṣa's.
oṃ amṛte hūṃ phaṭ: this is Amṛtakuṇḍalin's.
With this secret vajra mantra
and the mudrā of the blazing vajra,
by the blazing vajra, hard to withstand,
one should make oneself blaze.
oṃ dhupi dhupi kāya dhupi prajālani svāhā
Entering into the water
and pouring out three handfuls:
for the families of all the buddhas
this vidyā-mantra is prescribed.
The three heaps of earth
over which the vidyā-mantra has been recited
are supreme for purifying
my upper, lower and middle parts.
oṃ bhūra jala hūṃ
Recollecting all the buddhas,
one should take up with all the mudrās
the water over which all the vidyā-mantras have been recited.
This vidyā-mantra is held to be secret.
How are the directions bound?
Agni, the great king, in the east,
Yama in the southern direction,
Vāyu in the west,
and Varuṇa in the north are to be subdued.
The yogin binds the four forms
firmly in the four directions.
Then, above, below and in the intermediate directions,
one cuts off firmly with Vajrakālī.
How are the obstructors bound?
With the blazing Vajramuṣṭi,
the vajra blazing like the sun,
and the mudrā of the Vajra Eye,
the binding of the obstructors becomes firm.
What is the great joy like?
One should prostrate with great reverence.
With the four mudrās in the four directions,
and with the fifth, the stacked mudrā,
above, below and in the intermediate directions,
one should prostrate together with praise.

By the greatly fortunate vajra master,
the sublime one of the Supreme Secret,
it is to be bound in the same way
in the maṇḍala of the stacked mudrā.
If one asks what the offering is like:
the offering of reciting the vidyā-mantras,
devotion to binding the mudrās,
the offering of meditating on yoga,
and the offering of the practices.
What is the rite of the fire offering like?
The three kinds of bodily action,
the four kinds of speech,
and the three kinds of mind:
these, to be burned, are the supreme fire offering.
It arises from vajra yoga.
Then I shall explain the reality,
utterly hidden, supreme among the sublime,
the heart of the vajra vidyādhara
which gathers all the secrets.
The vajra master is endowed with great conduct;
he has fully entered the secret maṇḍala
and received empowerment from a master;
he knows all the secrets and intentions;
he is brave, steadfast and of great diligence,
upright of mind, retentive and clear;
gentle, honest and possessed of spiritual friends.
Such a one should be the vajra master.
First, until the accumulation is complete,
for a month or half a month,
or else for as long as a year,
one should practice the observance of knowledge.
If it is of the buddhas,
the vidyādhara practice is threefold.
If it is of the vajra holder,
it is definitely threefold.
For the excellent jewel family
one should perform the four yogas.
If it is for the pure lotus-born,
one fully accomplishes the four yogas.
If it is the excellent nature of action,
from the family of all actions
four applications truly arise.
Thus they come to eighteen kinds.
In the morning, the afternoon and at midday,
in the first and last parts of the night and at midnight,
one should do all the great tasks,
likewise for the buddha and vajra families.
First of all, the great yogin
should prostrate to all the directions.
By the rite of vajra yoga
one becomes the vajra master.
With great wisdom one should recollect
all the buddhas again and again.
The great being should urge
all the buddhas with the vajra mantra.
oṃ sarva tathāgata pūjepasvānāya ātmānāṃ niryātayāmi sarva tathāgata vajrasatva adhitiṣṭha sva māṃ
Having gone to the eastern direction,
one prostrates with all the limbs,
and with this secret vajra mantra
one offers oneself.
oṃ sarva tathāgata pūjā abhiṣekāya ātmānāṃ niryātayāmi sarva tathāgata vajraratna abhiṣiñca māṃ
With this secret vajra mantra
one prostrates to the southern direction
and offers oneself
to all the buddhas.
Then, toward the western direction,
one prostrates with this mantra:
oṃ sarva tathāgata pūjā pravartanāya ātmānāṃ niryātayāmi sarva tathāgata vajra dharma pravartayāmi
The great master of pure Dharma
prostrates to the western direction,
urges with this vajra mantra,
and fully offers himself.
oṃ sarva tathāgata pūjā karmaṇe ātmānāṃ niryātayāmi sarva tathāgata vajra karma kuru māṃ
Endowed with the acts of offering and empowerment,
and through firm application to the Dharma in the north,
one prostrates to the northern direction,
and oneself becomes vajra action.
In the east is the act of offering,
in the south empowerment,
in the west the great Dharma,
and in the north firm application to the Dharma.
This is the sun of all the sages.
Having bowed down to the great protector,
in all the directions, with this mantra,
one should earnestly make prostration.
oṃ sarva tathāgata kāya vāk citta praṇamena vajra vandanaṃ karomi
Then one makes prostration as explained.
With a mind focused on the buddhas,
the mudrās made with deep reverence
are the swift practice of approach.
After prostrating, one rises
and performs the acts oneself.
With the excellent offering of oneself,
one should offer to all the buddhas:
"Supreme ones dwelling in every direction without exception,
buddhas, please consider me!
Thus may I, who am pacified,
be made to become vajra itself."
To all the buddhas together with their children,
supreme in the vajra family,
always and in every way,
one should offer oneself.
By that secret vajra mantra
oneself comes to be offered.
Recollecting again and again
all the buddhas together with their children,
one should confess all the afflictions, wrongdoing and the rest,
so that they are extinguished.
Having entirely abandoned all wrongdoing
of this life or of others,
one should rejoice in the accumulations of merit and wisdom
generated by all the buddhas together with their children,
and likewise in those of all sentient beings.
One should request all the buddhas
not to pass from awakening into nirvāṇa.
With the great means of the mind of awakening,
one should recollect sentient beings and the buddhas.
Utterly wondrous offerings
should be accomplished as best one can.
Or else, to nirvāṇa and the rest,
and to the virtues of yoga and the rest,
with wondrous offerings
one should offer clouds of offerings.
Flowers grown in water and on dry land,
in this place or in others,
not claimed as their own by gods and others,
one offers to the great means, all the buddhas,
and, from the maṇḍala of their light,
to the lords of the sages.
With this vajra yoga
one worships fully with the offering of flowers,
and with mudrā and yoga
one emanates them firmly in all directions.
oṃ sarva tathāgata puṣpa pūjā megha samudrā spharaṇa samye hūṃ
Whatever excellent incense trees there are,
one offers them to the lords of the sages.
Marvelous divine incense and the rest
one should emanate in all directions.
oṃ sarva tathāgata dhūpa pūjā megha samudrā spharaṇa samaye hūṃ
Excellent fields of fragrance
one offers to the lords of the sages.
Having urged them on with the mudrā,
one should emanate them in all directions.
oṃ sarva tathāgata gandha pūjā megha samudrā spharaṇa samaye hūṃ
Oceans whose nature is precious jewels,
supreme mountains of jewels and the rest:
by emanating clouds of offerings
one should offer them in all directions.

oṃ sarva tathāgata bodhyaṅga ratna alaṃkāra pūjā megha samudrā spharaṇa samye hūṃ
Play, merriment, bliss and great joy,
a host of laughter, grace and the rest:
in all directions, from every side,
one should emanate the supreme of joy.
oṃ sarva tathāgata hāsya lāsya rati krīḍā saukhya anuttara pūjā megha samudrā spharaṇa samaye hūṃ
That from which clothing, ornaments and the like arise,
and likewise excellent wish-fulfilling trees:
one should recite the vajra mantra
and offer them to all the buddhas.
oṃ sarva tathāgata anuttara bodhi alaṃkāra vastra pūjā megha samudrā spharaṇa samaye hūṃ
Excellent jewels blazing greatly,
what arises from trees of light,
and the light of the moon, the sun and the rest:
one should emanate them in all directions.
oṃ sarva tathāgatāloka pūjā megha samudrā spharaṇa samye hūṃ
Recollecting again and again
the great suffering of all sentient beings,
through the practice of generating compassion
one should generate the mind of awakening.
Having generated the mind set on awakening,
for the sake of benefiting all sentient beings,
one should offer it to all the buddhas
and emanate it in all directions.
oṃ sarva tathāgata vajra bodhi citta pūjā megha samudrā spharaṇa samaye hūṃ
Well-being for all sentient beings,
loving-kindness toward all spirits,
the gift of fearlessness to all animals,
the wish that all the lower destinies be stilled:
loving-kindness, compassion, equanimity and joy,
the yoga of the abodes of Brahmā, has arisen.
One should recite the vajra mantra
and offer it to all the buddhas.
oṃ sarva tathāgata anuttara mahā catur brahma vihāra pūjā megha samudrā spharaṇa samaye hūṃ
Through practicing the perfection of generosity:
"May all sentient beings attain
glory and wealth!
May they never lack happiness!"
With these words one should offer and emanate.
oṃ sarva tathāgata mahā vajrodbhava dāna pāramitā pūjā megha samudrā spharaṇa samaye hūṃ
"May all sentient beings abide fully
in awakening and in the vows!"
One should emanate everywhere
the stilling of great faults such as pride.
oṃ sarva tathāgata anuttara mahā bodhi hara śīla pāramitā pūjā megha samudrā spharaṇa samaye huṃ
"From suffering such as fear and enmity,
may all fears and the rest be stilled!"
Through the yoga endowed with loving-kindness
one should accomplish the selflessness of phenomena.
oṃ sarva tathāgata anuttara mahā dharma ava bodhi kṣānati pāramitā pājā megha samudrā spharaṇa samaye hūṃ
"Until saṃsāra comes to its end,
may I not become a buddha!
And likewise may all sentient beings
accomplish that very aim!"
oṃ sarva tathāgata saṃsāra parityāga anuttara vīrya pāramitā pūjā megha samudrā spharaṇa samye hūṃ
"May the great afflictions of all living beings,
attachment and the rest, be stilled!
May all sentient beings be undistracted
and possess the four meditative concentrations!"
oṃ sarva tathāgata anuttara mahā saukhya vihāra dhyāna pāramitā pūjā megha samudrā spharaṇa samye hūṃ
"The primordial wisdom and great wisdom of the buddhas
is the unsurpassed cutter of suffering.
Through the application of wisdom that purifies,
may sentient beings become pure buddhas!"
oṃ sarva tathāgata anuttara kleśaccheda sarva dharma samanta jñāna mahā prajñā pāramitā pūjā megha samudrā spharaṇa samye hūṃ
One imagines oneself
as many as the atoms of a great trichiliocosm,
and, having so imagined for the sake of offering,
one should offer to all the buddhas.
oṃ sarva tathāgata kāya niryātan pūjā megha samudrā spharaṇa samaye hūṃ
One imagines oneself
as many as the atoms of a great trichiliocosm,
each possessing as many tongues,
and one should utter the praises of the buddhas.
oṃ sarva tathāgata vāka niryātan pūjā megha samudrā spharaṇa samaye hūṃ
Then, gathering together the offerings
that arise from the body, speech and mind
of that very yoga,
one should offer them to all the buddhas.
oṃ sarva tathāgata citta niryātan pūjā megha samudrā spharaṇa samaye hūṃ
Having made offerings to all the buddhas
endowed with great renown, without exception,
and having gathered together the virtues,
one should dedicate them to the benefit of sentient beings.
Recollecting all the buddhas,
one should take the cross-legged posture of the sattva.
The great yogas of loving-kindness and the rest
one should emanate in all directions.
With the two syllables of the supreme armor
the intelligent one arms himself,
and whatever vidyā-mantra he truly desires,
that vidyā-mantra he should recite.
In this great secret tantra
the recitation of the mantra of great yoga
is explained to be one hundred and ten times,
or else twenty-one times.
For the middling application of the rite,
five kinds are explained in common.
The secret recitation, the great yoga,
by which even the sinful attain accomplishment:
for the supreme family of the Tathāgata,
this recitation is supreme.
Having taken the auspicious cross-legged posture,
with the mind focused on the buddhas,
the intelligent one should always imagine
himself as the omniscient Buddha.
Imagining my body endowed
with the excellent marks, the uṣṇīṣa and the rest,
with the essence of the Buddha dwelling within,
one should think, "I am the Buddha."
Arising from the yoga of the Buddha,
by which the wheel is emanated,
one should turn the wheel of Dharma
of great, manifest awakening.
The Dharma enjoyed by the assembly of those to be trained,
the enjoyment, and the emanation are also accomplished.
Through the transformation of the basis,
mind does not arise from the all-ground.
Because merit and wisdom are fully perfected,
one imagines a full moon.
The stainless full moon
one should meditate upon at the heart.
In the center of the disc of the moon
one should meditate on the syllables of the vidyā.
By such a supreme method
one attains accomplishment in half a month.
Even one who has committed the five inexpiable deeds
attains accomplishment within one year.
For as long as he does not grow weary,
the great yogin should recite.
When the body has become tired,
he should begin binding the mudrās.
When reciting for the family of the Tathāgata,
he should then bind the supreme awakening,
or else the one called the source of phenomena,
or the utterly wondrous śrīvatsa.

Then, when reciting for the vajra family,
one's own self is Vajrapāṇi.
Abiding at the seat of awakening,
oneself is shown as Vajrapāṇi.
In the center of the disc of the moon
one should meditate on the form of a vajra.
In the center of the great secret vajra
one should meditate on the syllables of the vidyā.
Then, when reciting for the lotus family,
one's own self is Padmasattva.
In the center of the moon is a great lotus;
in the center of the lotus are the syllables.
Likewise, when reciting for the jewel family,
one's own self is the Jewel King.
In the center of the moon is a great jewel;
in the center of the jewel, too, are the syllables.
Then, for the excellent action family,
for the yoga that accomplishes every aim,
one's own self is the heart of the buddhas:
one should meditate on oneself as Vajrakarma.
In the center of the disc of the moon
one should meditate on a jewel vajra,
a great vajra with jeweled tips;
in its center, too, are the syllables.
This recitation of the five families
should be declared the secret recitation.

Then I shall explain yet another,
the line of recitation that holds the supreme secret.
When reciting for the Tathāgata family,
oneself the Buddha, holder of auspiciousness,
should meditate on this world of a billion worlds
as the form of the Buddha.
In the center of that Buddha's heart
one should meditate on the syllables of the vidyā.
One's own body is without self,
and phenomena and the person are emptiness;
the form of those buddhas
one should contemplate as without self.
Though the body is spoken of as the Buddha,
that Buddha, too, does not exist.
In the aggregates, the sense sources and the elements,
in what are called phenomena, there is no buddha.
As the person itself there is no buddha;
yet neither is there no buddha.
The six perfections are the seed,
and wisdom purifies the seed.
The fortunate one who knows what is possible and what is not
is the very one who holds awakening, selflessness.
If in this way one recites
for the great secret Tathāgata family,
the great master will swiftly
accomplish Perfect Joy.

Then, when reciting for the vajra family,
one's own self is Vajrasattva.
One should meditate on this world of a billion worlds
as the form of a vajra.
In the center of the hub of that vajra
one should meditate on the syllables of the vidyā.
That vajra is called the absence of entity;
it is what the fruit of awakening is.
That which is explained as vajra,
that which is reality itself, unchanging,
is called the reality of the body;
that which is empty of phenomena and the nature of reality.
The great phenomena, form and the rest,
do not bear the name of great elements.
The body is spoken of as form,
yet there is no such thing as form.
Analyzing that form as well,
one should meditate on the perception of the subtle.
When it is analyzed into parts,
there is no such thing as form.
When what is named a part is analyzed,
there is no such thing as a part.
Form is explained to be empty;
this is taught as what is called vajra.
All phenomena, form and the rest,
one should meditate on as emptiness.
By such a yoga as this
one should accomplish Perfect Joy.

Then, when reciting for the jewel family,
one's own self is Vajraratna, lord of the family.
One should meditate on this world of a billion worlds
as emptiness, as space.
That is the yoga of phenomena in space:
with conceptual thought, one contemplates the syllables.
That all phenomena are without entity
is called emptiness and suchness.
What is called the absence of entity has no form;
like space, it is the domain of the buddhas.
Where form does not exist,
tell me, what is the thing called the eye?
And of whom is that eye
said to be the eye?
Who is this lord, this king?
And what is its arising like?
From the gathering of the four great elements
the faculty of the eye arises.
In that eye, too, there is no form;
what does it do? It does not even exist.
Just as with the eye,
everything is free of grasped and grasper.
By this practice of recitation
one will be empowered with the great Dharma.

Then, when reciting for the lotus family,
one's own self is Padmasattva.
One should meditate on this world of a billion worlds
as the form of a lotus.
On that utterly pure lotus
one should meditate on the syllables of the vidyā.
With great wisdom one should contemplate
that lotus as the essence of the Dharma.
The lotus is not stained by water,
nor is it stained by the faults of the mud;
just so are all sentient beings pure.
This very Tathāgatahood,
in which phenomena are unborn from the very beginning,
which is the emptiness of phenomena and of their nature,
is held to be the nature of reality, suchness,
of all phenomena, form and the rest.

Then, when reciting for the action family,
one should contemplate the yoga of action.
This wheel of the Buddha's emanations
is what brings attainment of the Dharma and of enjoyments.
Where there is nothing called a phenomenon,
the emanations of the Buddha appear.
How wonderful! Those buddhas appear
and accomplish their own deeds as well.
Through such a yoga as this
the vajra master of great austerity
will in this very life attain
the supreme, sublime buddhahood.

The mudrās such as the vajra heart
are taught as the lords for the vajra family.
For the jewel and the rest, there is empowerment;
the supreme of purity holds the supreme lotus.
For the Buddha's body, the great Dharma,
the vajra yoga should be definitively taught.
The supreme awakening disciplines all;
this is held to be the sublime recitation.

Then the master, the Vajradhara,
should certainly make use of a rosary.
Wherever one performs ten million recitations,
two kinds, three kinds, or even fewer, are allowed.
For the Buddha, the rosary is held with the index finger;
with the middle finger the vajra yoga arises;
upon the ring finger the jewel arises;
with the little finger the lotus opens.
Performing the action with the action rosary
is taught to be done with all the fingers.
This is the great secret recitation
of the vajra vidyādhara.
For the Buddha yoga, bodhi seeds;
for the vajra holder, rudrākṣa beads;
for the outer vajra family, indra gems;
for one whose yoga is the jewel, pearls.
A rosary of lotus seeds
is praised for all of the lotus family.
Or else, supreme above all,
are the bodhi seeds that sustain life.
Recitation with iron is twofold;
with copper it becomes fourfold;
with rudrākṣa, twenty millionfold;
with pearl it becomes a hundredfold;
with indra gems the yoga is a thousandfold;
with silver it becomes a hundred thousandfold;
