Not because one is a son or a disciple:
it should be given to one who is a vessel.
If one knows him to be a vessel,
one should give even life and all one owns.

First he is brought into the maṇḍala,
and having been admitted according to the rite,
then, with palms joined holding flowers,
bowing the body, with great joy,
he should make confession and supplication,
and rejoice in every way.
As taught in the secret lord of tantras,
he should dedicate fully, together with offerings.
Bowing in all the directions,
he should take the great vow.
Of the master he should earnestly request:
"Lord, grant me the vows.
All you buddhas without exception, suns among sages,
please consider me.
I, who am called such-and-such,
abiding under the master's authority,
shall enter the great secret,
arisen from the play of the buddhas,
endowed with the wealth of the irreversible wheel,
the supreme city of great liberation.
Into all the lofty secret families,
great master, please admit me.
The empowerment of irreversibility,
O greatly fortunate one, grant to me.
Sealed with all the major marks,
perfectly endowed with the minor marks,
the lovely body of a buddha,
great master, grant to me.
The great and marvelous empowerment,
so that, to act for the welfare of all sentient beings,
I may always become a master,
great master, grant to me.
The wheel arisen from the secret family,
together with the vajra family,
perfectly complete with its porticoes:
let it be known that I have become a master.
I myself, utterly pacified,
shall take up the awakening mind,
and into the wheel of the secret
I shall enter, together with the vows."

Then the master should speak thus:
"Great secret, pure family:
do you, great being, wish
to uphold the secret fully?"
And he should reply, "I do wish it."

Again the master should speak thus:
"Go for refuge to the Buddha, the Dharma and the Saṅgha,
the Three Jewels.
This is the firm samaya
of the pure buddha family.
The vajra, the bell and the mudrā as well,
O great-minded one, you should take up.
The awakening mind is the vajra;
wisdom is said to be the bell.
The master, too, should be taken up:
the guru is equal to all the buddhas.
This is declared to be the vow and samaya
of the pure vajra family.
In the great supreme jewel family,
three times by day and by night,
always give the four kinds of giving:
material things, fearlessness, Dharma and love.
The three vehicles, outer and secret,
the true Dharma, you should uphold.
This is declared to be the samaya and vow
of the pure lotus family.
In the great supreme karma family,
one should properly take up
possession of all the vows,
and perform acts of offering as far as one can.
The other fourteen, apart from these,
are taught to be defeats.
They are not to be given up or cast aside;
they are called root downfalls.
Three times by day and by night,
one should recite them every day.
When a yogin lets them degenerate,
it becomes a grave offense.

You should not kill living beings,
nor take what is not given.
Do not engage in sexual misconduct,
and do not speak falsely.
Alcohol, the root of all ruin,
should be completely avoided.
Except for the sake of taming beings,
avoid all that should not be done.
Rely on the holy ones,
and honor and serve the yogins.
The three kinds of bodily action,
the four kinds of speech,
and the three kinds of mind,
guard as far as you are able.
Do not desire the lesser vehicle;
do not turn your back on the welfare of beings.
Do not abandon saṃsāra,
and never be attached to nirvāṇa.
Gods, asuras and guhyakas
you should not despise,
and you should not step over
mudrās, mounts, weapons or emblems.
These are taught to be the samayas;
you should always guard them.
The concordant vows are taught at length
in the secret tantra."

He in turn should declare:
"Master, listen to me here.
As the Lord has commanded,
so I wish to act.
Just as the protectors of the three times
made themselves certain of awakening,
so I shall generate
the unsurpassed and holy awakening mind.
In the vow of the yoga of the buddhas,
the three moral disciplines:
the discipline of training,
the gathering of virtuous dharmas,
and acting for the welfare of beings,
I shall firmly uphold.
The Buddha, the Dharma and the Saṅgha,
the unsurpassed Three Jewels,
from this day onward I shall uphold.
In the great supreme vajra family,
the vajra, the bell and the mudrā as well
I shall properly take up;
the master, too, I shall take up.
For the pleasing samaya
of the great supreme jewel family,
every day, at the six times,
I shall give the four kinds of giving.
In the great pure lotus family,
arisen from great awakening,
the three vehicles, outer, inner and secret,
the holy Dharma, I shall uphold.
In the great supreme karma family,
I shall properly take up
possession of all the vows,
and perform acts of offering as far as I can.
Having generated the unsurpassed
and holy awakening mind,
for the sake of all sentient beings
I take up the vows without exception.
Those not freed I shall free;
those not ferried across I shall ferry across;
those not relieved I shall relieve,
and I shall set beings in nirvāṇa."

Then the vajra master
should confer the empowerment according to the rite.
The vajra master's empowerment
is taught in the secret tantra.
When one always practices
by a rite such as this,
one will become a master, a great Vajradhara;
of this there is no doubt.

The rite of the vajra master is complete.

Next, the act of admitting the disciple
I shall properly explain.
The vajra master should admit the disciple
into the great secret as is fitting.
Examining whether he is a vessel or not
is not to be done by the great being.

For merely by seeing this
buddhahood is held to be accomplished.
Even if he is not a vessel,
admission should be granted to him.
Or else, whoever comes seeking the goal,
for him, as is comfortable,
or whoever simply comes to be present,
the wise one should grant admission.
Because merely by seeing it
everything is accomplished, without doubt.
But for whoever is merely present,
the rites are not to be performed:
only admission should be granted,
and not all the procedures are to be done.
If he is joyful and endowed with faith,
one should examine whether he is a fit vessel;
when he has first gone for refuge to the three,
admission should be granted to him.

The great mudrā of Vajrasattva
should be given to the vajra disciple.
As the great being Vajrasattva applies it,
one should ask, "Who are you?"
With his eyes covered
by a cloth of white and red,
by the mudrā of vajra entry
the vajra disciple should be admitted.

Then the rite of the doors is performed.
First, with Vajra Hook,
one should draw him in with the vajra hook,
and utter the syllable jaḥ.
vajra aṃkuśa jaḥ
Having drawn him in with the vajra hook,
one should lead him in with Vajra Noose,
applying it at the southern door,
and utter the syllable hūṃ.
hūṃ vajra pāśa hūṃ
Having led him in with Vajra Noose,
one should bind him with the one who holds the chain;
having bound him at the western door,
one should utter the syllable vaṃ.
vajra sphoṭa vaṃ
Draw him in properly with Vajra Hook,
lead him in with Vajra Noose,
bind him with Vajra Chain,
and with Vajra Bell bring about the descent.
vajra ghanḍe aḥ
With the mudrā of vajra entry
he should be led in to worship the maṇḍala.
With the great samaya mudrā
bring down the vajra wisdom.
samaya stvaṃ
With the great samaya mudrā
the vajra mantra should be urged on.
By the great samaya being
the wisdom of the buddhas should be brought down.
samaya hūṃ

Then, with the offerings and their mudrās,
the hosts of incense, flowers and the rest,
all the mudrās should be performed
and the vajra mantra urged on.
Then, according to the rite,
the vajra disciple should be bound by oath.
vajra udka
Conferring empowerment with a vajra name,
one should empower him as one wishes.
vajra abhiṣeka
Then the unsurpassed vajra wisdom:
the descent is then given to him.
vajra āveśa a a
Ten, twenty, thirty, forty, fifty, sixty, seventy, eighty, ninety, a hundred.
With the vajra, by means of this mantra,
bring about the descent in every respect.
Then the face covering should be untied:
the unsurpassed vajra eye.
vajra akṣa
"Fortunate one of the vajra family,
look upon the maṇḍala!" it should be said.
vajra pāśya ho
Then, with the excellent vase,
the vajra master empowers him;
merely by its excellent name
one is freed from all evils.
oṃ vajra abhiṣiñca
The vessel should be properly examined
and his wisdom thoroughly purified.
If he is known to be a fit vessel,
one should rejoice in making the distinctions.
he vajra nāma
Rite, ten. Holding, twenty. Water, thirty. Vajra, forty. Jewel, fifty. Clapping, sixty. Joined palms, seventy. Hāsa, eighty. Purity, ninety. Sattva, a hundred. Buddha, a thousand. Karma, two thousand. Form, three thousand. Yoga, four thousand. Holding, five thousand. Form, six thousand. Color, seven thousand. Shape, eight thousand. Sign, nine thousand. Words, a hundred thousand.
When a hundred thousand of the syllable a have been completed,
one is perfectly endowed with the characteristics of the wheel.
This is another domain of the secret;
next I shall explain the rites.

The rite of the disciple and the samaya of the name are complete.

Great being of pure wisdom,
next I shall explain the secret,
by all of whose rites
the great wheel becomes firm.
The buddha-being, being itself,
perfectly endowed with the characteristics of the grounds,
the nature of the secret pledge,
the pure mind of the tathāgatas,
vajra body, speech and mind,
pure wisdom of great miraculous power:
having taken hold of the vajra mind,
examine the characteristics properly.
The yoga endowed with the meaning of the minor marks:
examine the characteristics one after another.
By union in accord with the vajra mind,
pure wisdom is properly grasped.
The Supreme Secret, holy and supreme,
the abiding of wisdom, is fully explained.
Then, by holy wisdom,
one should be led into what transcends the world.
Endowed with the great characteristics,
rely properly on the ground of realization.
The supreme domain of yogins,
the vajra Dharma, is delightful.
By what are called dissolution and the dharmakāya,
threatening, and the fivefold light ray,
lightning, and the moon in water,
the wisdom of the buddhas is properly attained.
By the excellent moon in water
the disk of the moon should be abandoned.
Perfectly endowed with five suns,
the family dharma called the star,
which destroys the harm of the afflictions:
how wonderful, it is taught as the great secret!
What is called the secret is the arising of vajra light;
further, it is held to be the half moon.
The maṇḍala of the messengers, the khaṭvāṅga,
arises from the union of binding, love and the three.
The blazing hook,
and the host of Caṇḍa and the Vajra Bearer:
the transformation of the seat of conceptual thought
is held to be the great vajra that brings attainment.
By the lowest of what is highest of all,
that partial one should be abandoned.

The Supreme Secret is complete.

Then, from this great wheel of play, which is the supreme secret and vast, whose domain is beyond conception, profound, vast in its manner and wise, which teaches the host of rites within the domain beyond conception, which is pervaded by the array of great joy, and which is the source of the treasury of the Dharma of the full and perfect awakening of all the tathāgatas and the rest, there comes forth what is called the Supreme Tantra of the Great Mudrā:

The supreme and vast secret
teaches inconceivable rites.
In the great wheel of play
one should hold the treasury of the Dharma.

First of all, the host of rites shall be taught, namely hūṃ.
The method of application for grasping
the maṇḍala of the wheel: one utters the syllable hūṃ.
By this, too, one becomes a king,
and the queens of mudrā become a hundred.

The second is entry into the mode of the wheel of the Dharma of the full and perfect awakening of great awakening.

Namely hūṃ.
By this, the syllable hūṃ,
which is called the mudrā, becomes firm.
By the application of the great mudrā
the queens of mudrā are of a thousand kinds.
The third: the thousands of mudrās
become hosts of hundreds.
By the syllable hūṃ of entering the wheel of the buddhas
it becomes firm.
hūṃ
The divisions of the fourth yoga
here become a million.
In what is called the wheel of knowledge,
all the queens of mudrā, together with hūṃ:
as many mudrās of the wheel as there are,
their servants become ten million.
The trident and the other weapons
are always the king of mudrās. hūṃ

Then the host of mantras arises:
ṭag, ten. maṭ, twenty. phaṭ, thirty. raṭ, forty. baṭ, fifty. riṭ, sixty. khaṭ, seventy. vaṭ, eighty. taṭ, ninety. taṭ, a hundred.
ka ta is a thousand times.
ta and what is called va,
and ḍa, become ten kinds.
The syllable ḍa is applied three times;
the syllable a, too, is of four kinds;
the syllable ba is of five kinds;
the syllable a is applied six times;
ka is properly applied seven times;
the syllable dha is applied eight times;
the syllable śa becomes half.
khiṭ becomes a hundred thousand.
From the hundred thousand the yogin
should extract the tantra of mudrās.

From the Vajraśikhara, the very secret yoga, the great king of rites of the secret of all the tathāgatas, the supreme of the great secret assembly, the sixth of the great secret yoga that gathers all the rites, the second chapter, called "Entering the Vast Mode of Awakening," is complete.

The Supreme Secret of all the vidyādharas is also complete.

Then, from this vast mode of the bodhisattvas, there arises, at the beginning of the great maṇḍala, the wisdom from which the mudrās and the secondary mudrās arise:

When one has become
a vajra master and a vajra disciple,
one binds the mudrā and the secondary mudrā,
because this is the abode of the vajra holders.
When one binds Vajrasattva,
one binds the vajra fist tightly.
When one binds Vajrarāja,
one thoroughly binds Vajrayakṣa.
When one binds Vajrarāga,
one thoroughly binds Vajrarākṣasa.
When one binds Vajrasādhu,
one thoroughly binds Vajrakarma.
When one binds Vajraratna,
one thoroughly binds Vajrabhāṣa.
When one binds Vajratejas,
one thoroughly binds Vajrahetu.
When one binds Vajraketu,
one thoroughly binds Vajratīkṣṇa.
When one binds Vajrahāsa,
one thoroughly binds Vajradharma.
By an application such as this,
properly join the mudrā's counterpart.
By the mudrā and the secondary mudrā
the vajra mudrā becomes firm.

How are the mudrās of offering?
Having drawn in with the vajra hook,
delight with the vajra arrow.
Having loosed the vajra arrow,
accomplish with the vajra fist.
Having released the mudrā as is proper,
then perform the binding.
By this rite of offering
the vajra maṇḍala is worshiped.
Likewise, dance with the left hand,
and having bound a fist with the right,
circle it around the forehead
and set it before the mouth.
Laugh with the laughter of the vajra sun,
remaining even, slightly open.
With the stainless gleam of the sword
one should sing the vajra song.
The finger of the vajra wrathful one:
accomplish it well, raising it properly.
Then, at the crown and at the mouth,
when one performs the rite and worships the maṇḍala,
through the application of the family deity,
by accomplishing it well, speech is accomplished.
First, oneself as Vajrasattva;
then likewise examine the deity itself.
By this application of the observance
one should accomplish the family deity.

Of how many kinds is the wheel declared to be?
As the family is, so is the wheel;
as it is set out, so it becomes,
just as it was spoken, according to the intent.
In brief, it is of five kinds:
vajra, jewel, and likewise dharma,
lotus, and the variegated, well taught,
and likewise buddha, the secret yoga:
the wheel is explained as being of five kinds.
Of how many kinds is the secret?
The secret is declared to be of five kinds;
in extended terms, of many kinds:
buddha, vajra, jewel,
dharma and karma, of great renown.
Of how many kinds are the rites declared to be?
In brief, they are held to be of three kinds:
in the rites of pacifying,
increasing and fierce sorcery,
arising from vajra union;
unsurpassed vajra union,
immeasurable and countless:
just as the dispositions of those to be trained are,
so are the arisings of vajra union.
How is it declared in the root tantra?
That which possesses the five secrets
is called the root tantra:
empowerment and the great mudrā,
the family maṇḍala, and the arising of yoga.
Just as it arose from the secret family,
whoever first sees the nature of reality,
endowed with attention,
the great method of the perfect buddhas,
should likewise examine the secret thoroughly.
By this, always know thatness.
How does vajra wisdom come about?
As, by meditating, the Vajra Bearer
knows that very full union,
then the mind whose nature is wisdom,
that very wisdom, becomes firm.
How is the Supreme Secret described?
That which possesses the five kinds of mudrā
is proclaimed the Supreme Secret.
Gather the mudrā and the secondary mudrā:
the offering of the gentle smile.

And what is that itself like?
Teaching the meditation of knowledge,
perfectly endowed with the gateways of samādhi,
supreme among the retinue of knowledge goddesses,
one always becomes that very thing.

How is the Subtle Vajra declared?
The wise one should contemplate the self:
now, who is it that does not find a self?
The body has the nature of the four elements;
how does it arise from the elements?
Then, through this whole body,
the yogin, starting from the crown of the head
down to the soles of the feet,
sees impure substances arise.
Or else one should imagine oneself
dwelling in the middle of a moon vajra.
Or else, in the middle of the heart,
one should meditate on a moon disk.
Or else, perfectly endowed with two moons,
meditate on a vajra in their middle.
Or else, dwelling in the middle of the vajra,
one should firmly radiate the whole body.
Or else, with a body whose nature is vajra,
free of the vajra as a substantial thing,
knowing dharmas to be equal to space,
properly take up the vajra yoga.
Placing the tongue against the palate,
one should gaze at the tip of the nose;
by the blissful touch of the subtle vajra
the mind becomes settled in equipoise.
In the blissful touch of the subtle vajra
a sign will always arise.
Radiating that sign,
radiate it fully in all directions.
spharaṇa vajra
If one wishes to radiate the mind,
radiate it fully in all directions;
until it reaches the tip of the nose,
one should draw the mind back in again.
saṃharaṇa vajra
From then on, in equipoise,
whatever little one meditates on
is the selflessness of dharmas and of persons,
and the abandonment of grasped and grasper.
vajra vajra tiṣṭha vajra
Having focused always and firmly
on loving-kindness toward sentient beings,
by the conduct of radiating the mind
radiate it fully in all directions.
mahā vajra maitri spharṇa
"These beings are caught in every suffering,
in the greatest hardship":
by the application of great compassion
radiate it fully in all directions.
mahā kāruṇi spharaṇa
Setting aside nirvāṇa in equanimity,
properly undertake the welfare of beings:
"Alas, these beings are hard to tame!"
Radiate it fully in all directions.
mahā upekṣi spharṇa
Since all things are luminous by nature,
they are, from the very beginning, pure in nature;
total affliction is no longer perceived,
and there is neither nirvāṇa nor liberation.
sarva budbha pramoda spharaṇa
In space or in the other directions,
by the application of the subtle vajra,
meditate in perfect equipoise
on the vajra at the heart, the awakening mind.
By the application of the subtle vajra,
in the proper sequence,
one should meditate on the great form of Vajrapāṇi,
the holy nature of reality.
Endowed with all the supreme aspects,
meditate in the form of a buddha.
By the application of the subtle vajra
one should recollect the Buddha.
buddha anusmṛtyā viśa
In the middle of a moon disk
one should meditate on oneself.
In the maṇḍala of one's own heart,
meditating on the moon is a marvelous great yoga.
samanta bhadra āveśa
In the middle of the moon disk,
oneself in vajra form,
endowed with all the supreme aspects,
Vajrasattva, according to the rite.
vajra satva
Oneself as Vajrasattva,
endowed with all the supreme aspects,
at the heart, in the middle of the moon,
one should meditate on the moon disk.
vajra satva samādhi jñānāveśa
Meditating on oneself in the form of a buddha,
endowed with all the supreme aspects,
at the heart, in the middle of the moon disk,
meditate on the sattva.
tathāgato haṃ
The meditation of vajra yoga,
in a hundred and ten kinds,
is explained at length, as appropriate,
in the secret tantra.

The Subtle Vajra is complete.

How do worldly people wish it?
The vajra form where treasure lies
one should meditate on at the heart.
If one also meditates on the place in the ground,
the treasures will be clearly seen.
vajra nidhi
The vajra form of the great jewel
one should meditate on in the sky;
where it is seen to fall,
one should know there is treasure.
ratna nidhi
On the tongue, the form of a lotus
one should meditate on in perfect equipoise;
having fixed it with the words "it is there,"
the vajra tongue itself will speak.
dharma nidhi
Through the great yoga, the whole body
one should meditate on in vajra form;
where it comes down everywhere and falls,
there treasure is shown to be.
karma nidhi

The knowledge of treasures is complete.

This is the arising of the vajra descent.
Meditating on water as having the nature of vajra form,
by the force of the vajra
one should walk upon the water.
vajra jala
In whatever form one wishes,
Vajrasattva and the others, as desired,
one should meditate on oneself,
and one will obtain the very form desired.
vajra rūpa
All dharmas are equal to space:
"I am space," one says of oneself.
One should meditate on that very purity,
and one will become invisible.
vajra ākāśa
Sitting on a vajra ladder,
if one meditates on climbing it,
as far as the place one climbs to,
that far one will travel through the sky.
vajra mahaṃ

The knowledge of invisibility, of traveling through the sky, of walking upon water, and of the desired form is complete.

Sitting upon the form of a moon,
meditate on Vajrasattva;
if one meditates on Vajrapāṇi,
one will become a vajra vidyādhara.
vajra dhara
Sitting upon the form of a moon,
oneself as Vajraratna,
having meditated fully on the jewel,
one will become a jewel vidyādhara.
ratna dhara
Sitting upon the form of a moon,
oneself as a vajra holder,
meditate on the hands as lotuses;
one will become a lotus vidyādhara.
padma dhara
Sitting upon the form of a moon,
firmly established as Vajrakarma,
if one meditates on karma in the hands,
one will become a karma vidyādhara.
karma ṅhara

The suchness of the accomplishment of the vidyādharas is complete.

The samādhi of all the mudrās:
having imagined everything as the expanse of space,
for as long as one wishes to be of vajra nature,
for that long, in an instant, one will soar aloft.
vajra
The samādhi of accomplishment:
having imagined everything as the expanse of space,
one obtains the five superknowledges,
and wisdom swiftly becomes self-accomplished.
śuddha śuddha
Everything having the nature of the jewel vajra:
having imagined everything as the expanse of space,
the recollection has been taught;
swiftly one becomes the equal of the jewel holder.
vajra vajra
Everything having the nature of the form of a buddha:
having set one's conviction on the expanse of space,
the samādhi of all the buddhas
serves for the sake of buddhahood itself.
buddha buddha

The supreme accomplishment is complete.

Oneself making the vajra fist,
with eyes opened wide in the delight of passion:
whatever one looks upon with them
is likewise seen as the Vajra Bearer.
oṃ vajra dṛṣṭi maṭ
Eyes that are clear and blazing,
rolling the eyeballs all around:
this is declared the blazing gaze,
which summons all beings.
dīpta dṛṣṭi aṃkuśa jaḥ
Brows knit in a wrathful frown, utterly crushing,
eyes rolling violently in wrath:
relying on the wrathful gaze,
one may destroy even the three worlds.
krodha dṛṣṭi hriḥ
Like Mount Meru and Mount Mandara,
an eye that is steady and unblinking
is called the loving gaze;
it destroys grahas, fevers and poisons.
dṛḍha tiṣṭa traṃ

The rites of the secret of all the tathāgatas, summoning, bringing under control, inflicting punishment, and full protection, are complete.

There is no other bliss at all.
The vajra master is of great austerity,
yet summoning is bliss,
and it surpasses all.
At this time, for the greatly fortunate one,
from performing the great maṇḍala,
whatever bliss there is, even without accomplishment,
surpasses every bliss.
Even if the bliss of the worlds
of gods and humans were gathered together,
it could not match a sixteenth part
of the bliss of the vajra master.
Or else, whoever obtains accomplishment
should be known as buddhahood itself.
What one has realized for oneself,
by what means could others know?
One who has entered the ground and possesses the state of the victors,
who bestows the secret accomplishment,
whatever bliss he has attained
is just as the holder of the secret.
Whoever has entered
this great assembly of the secret
should be known to have attained the ground:
an irreversible son of the victors in very nature.
Though one attains the ground of the victors' state,
one does not gain conviction:
up to the eighth ground
one does not give up the Lesser Vehicle.
The sons of the victors who have entered
this rite of the great secret
and are devoted to the Great Vehicle
do gain conviction.
The benefits of the secret
are fully known by the buddhas.
Here are its qualities in brief:
one attains the bliss of the buddhas.

The tantra concerning the qualities is complete.

There is no maker and no producer;
from what are karma and its result taught?
As to what is spoken of as "I":
I am the maker and the producer;
I am Prajāpati, the lord of creatures;
I am the lord of all that is various,
the maker, the producer, the Grandfather (Brahmā).

Again he said:
The pure essence of the tathāgatas
is said to be called the essence of beings.
I am the essence of the tathāgatas;
all sentient beings are equal to me.
Together with nirvāṇa, below the earth, upon it,
and above the earth, this world and its beings,
the abodes of all existences without remainder,
all enter into mind alone.
The mind of all sentient beings
is declared to be the maker and the producer.
I am the king, the lord of mind.
Why am I said to be the maker?
As it is taught in another secret text:
I myself arose from myself.
Together with nirvāṇa, below the earth, upon it, and above it,
the three existences and the three worlds:
the mind that is said to abide there,
which has arisen from what does not abide,
the pure essence, the tathāgata,
that very thing, self-arisen, is I.
I am also the Vajra Bearer.
Because all suffering has been abandoned
and all faults have been exhausted,
I am the vajra of the awakening mind;
I myself arose from myself.
I myself am also Viṣṇu:
the pure consciousness of suchness,
free of all that is grasped and the rest,
is without self and without affliction;
therefore I am called Viṣṇu.
Likewise I am the Sun.
Why is it said to be so?
I have realized the profound Dharma;
therefore I, the self-arisen,
am declared to be called the Sun.
I am declared to be the Moon:
I am said to have realized
all dharmas as peace;
therefore, through great peace,
I am the Moon, of great intelligence.
Likewise I am Yama:
the great wisdom of all dharmas
just as they are is especially taught;
whoever has realized that
is I, Yama, the one who holds Yama.

Indra:
What is called the undeluded Indra
has crossed saṃsāra and reached the far shore;
having gained the wealth of wisdom,
I am Indra, the one who wields power.
Prajāpati:
The aspect of all dharmas
that tears out birth by the root
fully knows the great suffering;
therefore I am Prajāpati.
I myself am the Grandfather:
having drunk the water of amṛta,
I ferry beings from the ocean of saṃsāra
to the great city of liberation;
therefore I alone am the Grandfather.
I am the world of desire:
I fully bring into being the essential body,
the body of perfect enjoyment, and the emanation body.
What is called Mahāloka
is the one who teaches the view of the world;
it frees from the terror of existence
and thoroughly abandons the great afflictions.
Explained as Bhava:
cutting the noose of existence,
I am the great king of existence.
The one of the three realms:
tantra is declared to be "realm";
it is explained as the awakening mind.
Because I hold the three vehicles,
I am said to be the one of the three realms.
Below the earth:
the great vajra of true suchness
is explained as emptiness and as suchness.
Just as the earth is the supreme upholder,
so am I the upholder of the Dharma.
Upon the earth:
upon the earth is declared to be space;
by nature I am like space.
The selflessness of dharmas and of persons:
I am declared to be that "upon the earth."
Agni:
I am from the beginning unborn and without arising;
not self, without self, I am the lord of creatures.
Beings bound by the creeper of existence are tormented;
I am the liberator who clears away the creeper.
Vāyu:
having meditated on signs,
the signless and unconditioned,
the awakening mind scatters
the creeper and the noose; thus it is Vāyu.

I am Maheśvara:
I act as the great vajra Lord of Hosts (Gaṇapati).
I am the Buddha; what need to speak of any other lord?
The great nectar, the great city of liberation:
I am the lord, the wielder of power, the great lord.
I am declared to be the observance;
I am declared to be the destroyer of afflictions.
Because I am definitively freed
from old age and death, I am declared Brahmā.
In the body of the three worlds,
the noose, aversion and delusion
are the three realms, the three worlds.
I become their guardian and their servant;
since they arise from me, I am the three realms.
Ṣaṇmukha:
Buddha, vajra, jewel,
Dharma, karma, and the holder of secrets:
these six kinds are the great wheel.
I am the great supreme Six-Faced One.
Further:
whatever planets, hosts and the like there are,
as to how it is fitting to call them "my very self":
all the grahas and the rest
are waves that surge up from the mind.
I am the king who holds the mind;
therefore I am the abode of Bhava.
I am Desire:
the great city whose nature is liberation,
the ultimate intent, is taught by me.
I long for all sentient beings;
therefore I am declared to be Desire.
I am Liberation:
I myself am liberated, and all sentient beings,
each one of them, I also set free.
Therefore I, the self-arisen,
am the king who holds liberation.
I am Hell:
I am neither self nor dharmas;
I am wholly free of attachment and aversion.
Because I teach the path of the deathless,
I am the hell-dweller of hell.
I am a Preta:
what is longed for, I obtain;
I ripen the meditation of sentient beings.
Because I have attained the abode of joy,
I am a preta, born a preta.
I am an Animal, born an animal:
because the river of saṃsāra is hard to cross,
all sentient beings remain within it.
Just as they have become ignorant and benighted,
so do I set them free.
Further, I am passion,
because I am attached to the liberation of beings;
aversion, as the one who cuts off existence;
a miser, because I hold the secret treasury;
desire, because I desire sentient beings;
and, as the one who ripens beings, I am a sentient being.
Whoever is the king who holds the secret,
he is my fearsome son.
Therefore, of the great secret
I am the holder of the treasury of Dharma,
the holder of the secret of the buddhas' awakening;
therefore I am the Lord of Secrets.
Or else: blessing,
and possessing a face proud and graceful;
joining thā upon that,
it is spoken of as secret.
da ra dha rma da ra is the secret.
I am the holder of the fives upon fives.
Of the Great Peacock and of her who has the treasury
I am declared to be the Lord of Secrets.
She who has veins and skin and breasts,
the Treasury One and the others with hanging bellies,
and he who dances upon the moon:
these are the king of secrets.
He who dances proudly upon the earth,
the vajra dancer, the destroyer of white hair,
she of the net, who seizes by the hair, the big-bellied one,
the Treasury One, and she who holds the garment,
she who utters the letter ta, the Moon,
the Supreme, and she of terrifying face,
Rāhu, the vajra sound in harmony,
she who bares her fangs, pervaded by intoxication,
likewise she who is endowed with true conduct,
Mālin, and she who destroys delusion,
she who invites all that is conditioned,
she who has a long-lasting troupe of dancers,
she who moves in the dance of the family,
the Terrifier and the Utter Terrifier,
the one called the Abode of Dance,
the dancer, she who is the vajra dancer,
she who scatters all that is excellent,
the refuge and holder of the five heaps.
With her anklets she crushes the hosts of māras;
with her anklets she makes the lord of thunder tremble;
with her anklets again and again she escorts the great lord;
trembling as she moves, she escorts the steadfast;
hands and waist move in accord;
with those outside the dance she gathers all.
The vajra Sun, the dance of the earth,
likewise na di ki si ri:
this is the secret of the dancer of the great feast;
therefore I am the Lord of Secrets.
hā hā hā thā pā tā pa kā ta sa ra ma ra hūṃ
She of great yoga who invites,
the Devoted One, and another, the Emanator,
Hārītī, the Destroyer, she of the bow,
the Heart, the Māra woman, she who roars,
the holder of the drum, and Rudra,
and further she is declared the Vajra Bearer.
She who has the six hosts of neighing horses,
she who destroys the night with masses of light,
who sounds forth the lion's roar,
she who neighs like a horse, Rāhu's lady,
and further she who bathes in the dense secret,
the plundering mistress with manifold cries of "ha ha,"
she of the fair, wide eyes who reveals the secret,
and further she who by her effort brings about joy itself,
and further she of wide eyes adorned with blue lotuses,
who destroys the clouds and destroys all the lands,
lion-faced and playful, in due order from above:
such is the retinue, endowed with the sublime.
And further she who proclaims taṃ in the secret mind,
and likewise she who step by step brings about goodness itself.
ṭu hūṃ ṭu hūṃ dha ri dha da ri ma hā śri ri śa, or else śri ya ri kaṃ ma ta sa ta kaṃ.
This completes the secret of reality.

Having entered its maṇḍala,
if the yogin delights
in the supreme five qualities of desire,
he will thereby gain accomplishment.
As taught in the Lord of Secret Sūtras,
what is the conduct of sentient beings?
He further said:
The five qualities of desire that are taught
are the full accomplishment of the secret.
First, for the Tathāgata family,
one should enjoy them as practice.
What the bhaga is shall be stated:
because it destroys, it is called bhaga.
That wherein afflictions are destroyed:
wisdom destroys the afflictions of the body;
therefore wisdom is called the bhaga.
Then what the sign is shall be stated:
the sign is explained as the mark.
O wise one, wisdom arises from the sign;
the awakening mind is the great means,
and that is proclaimed to be the sign.
Having placed the liṅga in the bhaga,
one should accomplish the accomplishment of the wheel.
Whatever samādhi and dhāraṇī there are,
the wise one should hold the two doors.
That is called "the insertion";
one becomes a great vajra holder.
Next, as taught with hidden intent,
the accomplishing of substances in the bhaga:
as spoken in the Lord of Secret Tantras,
how are these practices to be done?
Having brought forth saffron in the bhaga,
the mark too should be inserted.
Excellent substances will be obtained,
and the four kinds of rite will also be accomplished.
The bhaga is called the lotus;
the mark is explained as the vajra.

Having placed the substance upon the lotus,
one should accomplish it with the vajra churning stick.
One who is passionate, through intense attachment,
thereby fully accomplishes the secret.
How does great accomplishment come about
through the union of passionate attachment?
This is fully accomplished by the passionate one;
it is rightly taught as truth itself.
For one free of desire there is no secret;
he will not accomplish the secret.
Whoever, with the great faith of faith,
engages in practice with the attachment
that arises from the secret union,
will accomplish it beyond doubt.
The wrong of being free from attachment:
whoever becomes free of attachment toward the secret
while practicing the awakening of the buddhas
commits a great sin, a great wrong,
utterly reviled by all the buddhas.
In the Dharma there is no wrongdoing;
by it the secret will be fully accomplished.
Of every kind of affliction,
desire is the supreme destroyer.
Desire, aversion and the other afflictions:
there is no dharma equal to their realization.
How is killing all creatures meritorious?
As long as one is bound in saṃsāra,
existence will not be exhausted.
This prison of saṃsāra
is to be destroyed by all sentient beings.
Whoever steals the wealth of others
thereby fully accomplishes the secret.
Wealth is explained as saṃsāra;
those sentient beings become afflicted by it.
Whoever steals the wealth of others
fully accomplishes the awakening mind.
One who sports together with women
thereby accomplishes the secret.
With whatever goddess of one's own family,
the unsurpassed goddess of knowledge,
through playing together in yoga,
by the play alone one is accomplished.
If one wishes to accomplish the family,
one should even speak false words.
All dharmas are without entity,
inexpressible, not the domain of words.
Whatever does not exist bears false words;
the recitation of knowledge mantras is the truth.
If one wishes to accomplish the supreme accomplishment,
one should kill sentient beings and likewise eat them.
"Sentient being" is explained as attachment,
attached in every way to name and form.
If, having summoned it, one devours it,
one will gain great merit.
How abandoning saṃsāra becomes contrary to the Dharma:
whoever abandons saṃsāra
turns his back on the aims of sentient beings.
The wise one who delights in the lesser vehicle
is always at fault.
If one delights in nirvāṇa,
one turns away from the aims of sentient beings.
The wise one who delights in the lesser vehicle
is always at fault.
The miser accomplishes that family:
his moral discipline becomes pure,
and even for the sake of his life he does not give it up.
One endowed with the observance of knowledge
thereby fully accomplishes the secret.
One who has a fierce mind,
it is held, will thereby be accomplished.
In order to cut off all suffering,
he is ever fierce.
If he abandons sins and hatreds,
he thereby fully accomplishes the secret.
How conduct contrary to the Dharma is the accomplishment of supreme yoga:
one who knows the dharmas of grasped and grasper,
when he comes to abandon them,
always practices the supreme Dharma,
and thereby fully accomplishes the secret.
How yoga is accomplished through intense attachment to the three existences:
one becomes attached to the three existences;
because one realizes them as mind only,
that is the accomplishment of yoga.
Whoever is ever victorious through the passion of desire
is firmly accomplishing the great secret.
What is to be desired is desire;
it is called the passion of desire.
It is attachment to supreme joy;
the wise one's understanding is attached to that.
If one drinks a palmful of urine,
one should eat a little excrement.
Through the observance of total purity,
one quickly gains the supreme accomplishment.
Urine is explained as the vajra;
excrement is truly endowed with the lotus.
By touching the dharma of joy,
one then goes to the city of liberation.
The great yogin drinks the bhaga,
and the yoga is fully accomplished.
Whatever is the wisdom of all the buddhas
is proclaimed by the word bhaga.
Whoever drinks the drink of nectar
becomes like Vajradhara.
Together with a lovely woman
one should contemplate the great maṇḍala.
The goddess of the family, arisen from yoga,
is the lovely knowledge consort herself.
If one contemplates the secret maṇḍala,
in that very instant one is spontaneously accomplished.
Wrathful, ferocious and fierce,
one belongs to the vajra family.
Whoever is wrathful toward sufferings
and hates the karma that arises from union with saṃsāra
becomes perfect in vajra yoga.
It is the family that degenerates:
having abandoned all virtue,
one rightly practices what is contrary to the Dharma;
by the yogin who does what is not to be done,
the karma maṇḍala will be accomplished.
Having abandoned all conceptual thought,
one fully engages in virtue.
Being one who looks to the Dharma,
one should never practice what is contrary to the Dharma.
Whatever is not to be done is without entity;
that very thing one makes firm.
Through such great yoga,
one is fully accomplished by the great rite.

This completes the third explanation, called Commentary on the Intent of the Great King of Tantras, from the Vajraśikhara, the yoga of the utmost secret, the great king of realizations of the Supreme Secret of the great secret assembly, the sixth of the Compendium of All Rites.

From the great king of tantras called the Compendium of All Rites, the supreme Dharma of all the tathāgatas, the Supreme Secret, the abode of the secret Dharma, is also complete.

Homage to all the buddhas and bodhisattvas.

Having paid homage again and again
to the Thus-Gone Ones of the three times together with their heirs,
I shall explain in brief
the supremely marvelous tantra of knowledge.
hūṃ a ja kar dha ra ho dha ra sa oṃ a dra na ha ha he var dhi dha dha dhī ṅhār ṇi ma he to raṃ ka kra ṇaṃ ka ba ce hūṃ baṃ
Herein is the secret, the supreme secret,
the great nature of sublime wisdom,
the king, the Lord of Secret Sūtras,
which gathers up the whole of the Great Vehicle,
the great wise source of the families,
the source of all the buddhas,
and the rite of the vajra empowerment of all:
this most marvelous teaching I shall fully explain.
In the body of essential nature
all the buddhas and vajra bearers arise.
The marvelous great tantra of the Dharma
is the supreme teaching of liberation.
From the heart of Vairocana, the Lord of Sages,
this tantra came forth.
Samantabhadra, the lord of vajras,
Vajrapāṇi of great radiance,
Vajrasattva, the great being,
the sublime Lord of Unfailing Purpose:
the Great Vehicle, the awakening mind,
is called the Pledge of Truth.
The great Sage taught
countless thousands of heaps of Dharma.
The great supreme Vajrasattva
explained the realization of the Dharma:
the realization of discernment,
likewise complete awakening,
the supreme minor marks made manifest,
and likewise the very attainment of wisdom.
This Dharma of great wisdom
is taught as the realization of the buddhas;
it is the realization of the Great Vehicle,
the great way of the utmost secret.
The discernment of one's own mind
is the great way of the bodhisattvas,
the supreme conduct of the six perfections,
the summation of the Great Vehicle.
The ultimate ground, the great ground,
the ground of buddhahood, unsurpassed:
this is the realization of the Great Vehicle.
Realizing the attainment of wisdom,
the great awakening of the ten practices,
the ten sublime perfections,
the full realization of the ten grounds:
this is the summation of the Great Vehicle.
Great and vast awakening,
supreme omniscience,
the complete awakening of all-knowing wisdom:
this is the summation of the Great Vehicle.
The five aspects of complete awakening,
the unsurpassed wisdom of the buddhas,
the Great Vehicle, supreme among dharmas,
the secret of all the tathāgatas:
the abode of the Dharma is wholly transformed.
Buddha, Dharma, and the supreme assembly.
The maṇḍala of the accumulations is utterly pure,
the same as the expanse of space,
freed from all negative tendencies,
freed from all conceptual thought,
wholly purified of the stains of affliction:
first it is seen as the moon.
Loving compassion truly arises,
and one takes all sentient beings into one's care.
The awakening mind, of great purity,
the buddha field without defilement,
filled throughout with virtuous dharmas,
decisively destroys all afflictions.
In the center of the great full moon,
awakening is a second moon:
the awakening of three countless eons,
arisen from what was truly undertaken,
countless supreme sublime dharmas,
endowed with the supreme practice of generosity,
realizing ground after ground,
wisdom that is pure and without defilement.
In the center of the moon disk
is the vajra, utterly firm.
Whatever is the body of all the buddhas,
without appearance and without abode,
unborn, unmade and pure,
free of arising and destruction,
unburnable, uncuttable, indivisible,
the Dharma body without remainder:
the vajra self truly arises.
The vajra body is unsurpassed.
Body and secret likewise act,
fully realizing every domain of experience.
These five are all held in common,
having the nature of all the buddhas:
the five aspects of complete awakening,
the unsurpassed wisdom of the Dharma.
These five are called the secret,
and they are also spoken as heart mantras.
Because they serve to accomplish every aim,
formerly they brought all aims to accomplishment.
The Blessed One Samantabhadra,
the unsurpassed Vajrakarma,
the Vajradhātu, the lord of steadfastness,
the unsurpassed Vairocana:
these are the secret names;
through them one attains the stage of yoga.
Then the heart mantras:
oṃ citta pra ti bo dhaṃ ka ro mi oṃ bo dhi citta ut pā da yā mi oṃ tiṣṭha bajra oṃ bajra ātma ko haṃ oṃ ya thā sarba ta thā ga tā sta thā a haṃ
Then all the buddhas,
abiding in the heart of the Sage,
the speech of all the tathāgatas,
uttered this vajra knowledge mantra:
oṃ sarba ta thā ga ta a bhi saṃ bo dhi dṛ ḍha bajra tiṣṭha
When all the greatly renowned buddhas
had uttered this knowledge mantra,
Vairocana, the great Sage,
became fully awakened to the wisdom of suchness.
Then all the buddhas
came forth from his own heart.
Having bestowed the great yoga of the four dharmas,
he is empowered by the goddesses.
Truly endowed with the aims of all sentient beings,
fulfilling every wish,
truly arisen from speech equal to space,
he is empowered by the great jewel.
Whatever is the wisdom of all the buddhas,
the way of the perfection of wisdom,
omniscience, the great wisdom:
this is bestowed by the great Lokeśvara.
Unfailing in every activity,
manifesting in Tuṣita and elsewhere,
the activity of means for taming those to be tamed:
this is blessed by all the buddhas.
Whatever is the secret of all the buddhas,
the awakening mind, the great way,
completely freed from all afflictions:
this is bestowed by the great chief Buddha.
At the very moment of empowerment
the secret name truly arose:
the king, the lord of all the buddhas,
the sublime empowerment, supreme over all.

Then the heart mantra:
oṃ sarba ta thā ga ta e śva jya a bhi ṣe ka hūṃ
Then the great king of buddhas,
who had made the Dharma directly manifest,
departed from the Densely Arrayed abode
together with the buddhas, great and manifold,
great beings joined with yoga,
and went to the abode of the vajra lord.
Then, from the body of essential nature,
emerging once again, he is emanated.
The buddha of great yogic activity,
following the former ones just as they did,
came to be seated in the center of the vajra.
Just as the buddhas of the past had done,
he displays the deeds of a buddha.
In the wheel of secret play
his body is the supreme body of perfect enjoyment.
Great being who is all the buddhas,
great being of manifold yoga,
he comes to abide in all the wheels.
In all the directions and intermediate directions,
following the former ones just as they did,
truly endowed with the play of the buddhas,
buddha teachers come to be seated.
In accord with the wisdom of the three times
the yoga of the buddhas is declared:
hūṃ bajra sa tva oṃ bajra ratna hrīḥ bajra dharma aḥ bajra karma
The awakening that the Blessed One accomplished
three countless eons ago:
I shall make appear the vajra maṇḍala
with its four beings of yoga.
Great awakening, supreme in Dharma,
the awakening mind, the great way:
I shall make the nature of Vajrasattva,
and the maṇḍala of the moon appears.
The practice of the perfections
that the Blessed One practiced long ago:
through union with Vajraratna,
the maṇḍala of the moon appears.
Realizing the Great Vehicle,
fully realizing the supreme Dharma:
through union with Vajradharma,
the maṇḍala of the moon appears.
Ripening and liberating sentient beings,
fully engaging in the deeds of a buddha,
the great activity endowed with diligence:
the maṇḍala of the moon appears.
The awakening mind, the great secret,
the accumulation of merit, and wisdom,
fully applying the conduct of awakening:
the four kings fully arise.
Whatever is the application of armor,
and likewise the conduct and the realization,
the great awakening of the perfect Buddha:
the four kings arise.
Realization of the essence of awakening,
the empowerment of all the buddhas,
the turning of the wheel of Dharma:
the four kings arise.
Realization of the supreme Dharma,
the doors of samādhi and the dhāraṇīs,
the great means of all the buddhas:
the four kings arise.
The perfect awakening of the great king,
and likewise the realization of the Dharma,
the supreme Dharma and great awakening:
the four kinds fully arise.
Emanated from the heart of the Protector,
the four kings arise.
From all the directions everywhere,
having made the whole earth quake,
in all the directions everywhere
they made all bright with radiant light.
All the great protectors, the buddhas,
uttered this inspired utterance:
"How wonderful, the great secret Dharma!
How wonderful, the unsurpassed wisdom!
How wonderful, the wheel of play!
How wonderful, the great marvel of the buddhas!
How wonderful, the great means of the buddhas!
How wonderful, it is wondrous indeed!
Dharmas whose nature is not to appear
are here perceived as having appearance."
Those great kings of yoga,
illuminating all the directions,
attained the samādhi of the Dharma body,
marvelous, equal to the sky.
In the eastern quarter,
all the buddhas of great renown,
and Vajrasattva, the great king,
were truly emanated into the dharmadhātu.
Having emanated as the Dharma body,
the dharmadhātu becomes dense.
Because he truly arose in the east,
he made himself Akṣobhya.
In the south is Vajraratna,
in the west is Vajradharma,
in the north is Vajrakarma;
they come to be endowed with the Dharma body.
All the buddhas, taught supremely without remainder,
are in the four directions:
Akṣobhya, Vajra, the Lord of Jewels,
and the unsurpassed Amoghasiddhi.
The four kings in the four directions
come to be seated upon the vajra.
In the four directions and intermediate directions
they were seated, envisaging equality.
The great king of vajra yoga
truly gathers all the buddhas:
the essential nature of the steadfast dharmadhātu,
gathered so as to pervade all secrets.
In the center is the protector Vairocana,
who confers empowerment on all the buddhas;
having made firm all the buddhas
of the directions and intermediate directions, he is seated.
The victorious lord over all the wicked,
Akṣobhya, supreme being among sages,
having made firm all the buddhas,
is truly seated in the eastern direction.
The great king of empowerment,
the supreme being, the Sage of Space,
having made firm all the buddhas,
came to be seated in the southern direction.
The great king who holds the pure Dharma,
the great chief, the holder of the Dharma,
having made firm all the buddhas,
came to be seated in the western direction.
The king who holds the deeds of the buddhas,
the unsurpassed Amoghasiddhi,
having made firm all the buddhas,
came to be seated in the northern direction.
Akṣobhya is Vajrasattva;
Ākāśagarbha is Ratnasambhava;
Lokeśvara is Amitābha;
Vajrakarma is Amoghasiddhi.
Secret, utterly secret, supreme:
whatever is the steadfast dharmadhātu,
that itself is the protector Vairocana,
declared to be the lord of all the buddhas.
For all the buddhas in the four directions
the four gifts are fully taught.
Having made firm the six intermediate directions,
Vairocana is truly present.
The buddha of the east, victorious lord,
Vajrasattva, the fearless one,
the Sage Akṣobhya, that light,
speaks the four heart mantras:
hūṃ bajra satva jaḥ bajra rā ja ho bajra rā ga sā bajra sā dhu
Those called the pledge beings,
likewise Vajrasattva and the rest,
the four fearless beings,
arose from the heart of the Protector:
Vajrapāṇi, supreme and excellent,
holding the Great Vajra Hook,
Vajrarāga, the great māra,
Vajrasādhu, of great miraculous power,
and Vajrarāja: these four,
arisen from the heart of the Sage,
together making the number four,
came to be seated in the order of the wheel.
Vajraratna, of great fortune,
Ratnasambhava, of great renown,
lord of the gods, the Dharma of the buddhas,
speaks the four heart mantras:
oṃ bajra ratna oṃ bajra te jaḥ trāṃ bajra ke tu hā bajra hā sa
Vajraratna, of great power,
truly endowed with the number four.

Arisen from the King of Jewels,
arisen from the heart of the Sage:
Vajraratna, the precious,
Vajratejas, of great miraculous power,
Vajraketu, seen from far and wide,
Vajrahāsa, of great miraculous power.
And from each one of them
countless kings came forth.
To the right and the left, behind,
and in front of the maṇḍala they are seated.
The pure Dharma, the great wisdom,
the Tathāgata Amitāyus,
through the purity of the perfection of wisdom,
speaks the four heart mantras:
hrīḥ bajra dharma dhṛḥ bajra tīkṣṇa maṃ bajra he tu raṃ bajra bhā ṣa
The four kings of Dharma as well,
arisen from the heart of the Protector,
for the benefit of all sentient beings,
came to be seated in the order of the wheel.
Unfailing toward all dharmas,
the supreme master of conduct,
the great king Amoghasiddhi,
speaks the four heart mantras:
kaṃ bajra karma haṃ bajra rakṣa hūṃ bajra yakṣa baṃ bajra muṣṭi
All of them are truly endowed with the wheel,
those of supreme and pure wisdom.
The wheel of the emanations of the buddhas
is supreme in the Dharma body and the body of perfect enjoyment.
Whatever was the former awakening mind
arose as Vajrasattva.
The four means of attracting disciples
become Vajraratna.
The pure perfection of wisdom
is endowed with the supreme Vajradharma.
Through skill in means, the wheel
is emanated as Vajrakarma.
Then the Dharma, the perfect Buddha,
the support of the Dharma body and the body of perfect enjoyment,
has arisen, arises, and will arise.
The unsurpassed deeds of the buddhas,
holding supremely to the awakening mind,
taming through the pure Dharma,
the great essence of the dharmadhātu,
arose from the Dharma of the Middle Way.
Further, compassion and likewise giving,
truly endowed with wisdom and means:
those four kings of Dharma
arose from the heart of Vairocana.
Neither perception nor non-perception,
not imputed, without abiding,
not a view of self or of dharmatā,
not arisen from conceptual thought:
in the body of essential nature
the separate appearances truly arose.
Through the body of perfect enjoyment
comes such an enjoyment of Dharma as this,
free from all expression,
peaceful and subtle, hard to see.
The buddhas hold it in their intent,
and the emanations of the buddhas will be seen.
That itself has the nature of the Great Vehicle,
the supreme nature of the great treasury,
great wisdom and great means,
the summation of the Great Vehicle,
the realization of the wisdom of the buddhas.
What the Blessed One did long ago:
those four beings
are held to be the realization of the Great Vehicle.
From that mind of awakening
the Sage Akṣobhya arose.
That mind, moreover, is fourfold;
the four aspects truly arose.
Whatever is the awakening mind is the vajra;
the vajra is Vajrapāṇi.
Delivering, liberating, and reviving,
the holder of the hook is firmly present.
The being who is taught is unfailing;
that very wisdom is intensely passionate.
In order to teach once more,
it truly arose from Sādhu.
The Protector who brings forth sages,
who takes sentient beings into his care,
is called Ratnasambhava;
he too, in turn, is fourfold.
The gift of Dharma is the jewel;
fearlessness truly abides as splendor;
love truly proclaims the banner;
material gifts are truly endowed with laughter.
The king of the perfection of wisdom,
Amitāyus, is fourfold:
the discriminating knowledges, fully perfected;
the door of samādhi, possessed of the lotus;
the cutting of suffering, extremely sharp;
the wheel of Dharma, endowed with life.
Another emanation of the buddhas,
the supreme Dharma that cannot be expressed,
is the activity of Amoghasiddhi;
that means too is fourfold.
From taming sentient beings and the like
comes Vajrakarma, the great offering.
Not entering into another vehicle
is proclaimed as Vajrarakṣa.
Taming the wicked and bringing them to certainty,
not turning back, is the fist.

Then the secret names of these are Vajrasattva, Vajrarāja, Vajrarāga, Vajrasādhu, Vajraratna, Vajratejas, Vajraketu, Vajrahāsa, Vajradharma, Vajratīkṣṇa, Vajrahetu, Vajrabhāṣa, Vajrakarma, Vajrarakṣa, Vajrayakṣa, and Vajramuṣṭi. Among them, the first are Vajrasattva, Vajradharma, Vajraratna, and Vajrakarma; then their synonyms are Akṣobhya, Ratnasambhava, Amitābha, and Amoghasiddhi. Then the secret heart mantra of these is hūṃ oṃ ā ha. The heart mantra of the kings is oṃ hūṃ hrī hoḥ. The heart mantra of the sixteen is hūṃ jaḥ ho sā oṃ āṃ tvāṃ ha hrī dhi maṃ raṃ kaṃ haṃ hūṃ baṃ. That which has truly arisen as the sixteen kinds of sublime perfection of this great and utterly secret tantra is the sublime vajra perfection.

Through the power of the conduct of awakening,
one has gone to the far shore of buddhahood itself.
Therefore she who possesses perfection
is to be called Sattvavajrī.
The four means of attraction
and the discriminating understandings, fully perfected:
these four dharmas become one,
and that is called Ratnavajrī.
The wisdom of the secret Dharma
is itself the supreme perfection;
arisen from the great perfection,
it is to be called Dharmavajrī.
That which truly knows the application
is to be called Karmavajrī.
The four perfections of the kings
are the supreme vajra perfection.
Because she is supreme in playful delight,
Lāsyā is the perfection of generosity.
With adorned body, captivating the mind,
Mālā is the perfection of moral discipline.
Uttering inspired utterances,
Gītā is the perfection of patience.
Swiftly bestowing buddhahood,
Nṛtyā is the perfection of diligence.
Unsullied by the mire of desire,
Puṣpā is the perfection of meditative concentration.
Spreading evenly everywhere,
Dhūpā is the perfection of wisdom.
Scent turns away from nonvirtuous deeds;
skillful means is perfectly endowed with scent.
Illuminating all directions,
aspiration is the great light.
What is called the perfection of attainment
is the supreme perfection of power.
Destroying the darkness of ignorance,
the goddess is the perfection of primordial wisdom.
Vanquishing all the māras,
the goddess is the invincible perfection.
The great awakening of perfect awakening,
the mode of the vajra perfection,
she who turns the wheel of Dharma,
the supreme intrinsic nature of selfless phenomena:
the vajra goddess has truly arisen,
the supreme vajra perfection.

Then their secret names are Sattvavajrī, Ratnavajrī, Dharmavajrī, Karmavajrī, Vajralāsyā, Vajramālā, Vajragītā, Vajranṛtyā, Vajrapuṣpā, Vajradhūpā, Vajragandhā, Vajrālokā, Vajra Hammer Lady, Vajra Wisdom Lady, Vajrāparājitā, and Karmavajrī.

The great skillful means of all the buddhas
operates in four ways.
The great means of summoning sentient beings
is explained as Vajra Hook.
Engaging in the conduct of awakening
is proclaimed as Vajra Noose.
Having no desire for other vehicles:
that is explained as Vajra Chain.
All phenomena are unborn,
utterly luminous, at peace from the very beginning;
the Vajra Vehicle, the Great Vehicle,
is called Vajra Bell.

Then their secret names are Vajra Hook, Vajra Noose, Vajra Chain, and Vajra Bell.

Then the heart mantra of all those wheels of the means of perfection is hūṃ trāṃ hrī ho; that is of the vajra perfections. jaḥ hūṃ vaṃ hoḥ.

Abiding in the play of the wheel,
through this yoga of the buddhas
the Vajra Bearer, enjoyer of the Dharma,
becomes the unsurpassed dharmakāya.
The buddha in the center and the four buddhas,
endowed with the five wheels,
all the lords of vajra yoga,
bring about the attainment of yogic samādhi;
and each of them, moreover,
is surrounded by countless vajras.
Arisen from the Dharma and from perfect enjoyment,
it is the great mode of the pure Dharma.

Then their heart mantras are oṃ satva vajrī oṃ ratna vajrī, oṃ dharma vajrī, oṃ karma vajrī, oṃ vajra lāsye, oṃ vajra māle, oṃ vajra gīte, oṃ vajra naṃr rya, oṃ vajra puṣpe, oṃ vajra dhūpe, oṃ vajra gandhe, oṃ vajra āloke, oṃ vajra aṅkuśa, oṃ vajra pāśa, oṃ vajra sphoṭa, oṃ vajra ghaṇḍe. Then the heart mantra common to all of these is hūṃ.

The great protector Vairocana,
the four great wheel-turning monarchs,
the sixteen great beings,
the twelve goddesses of the great secret,
and the four gatekeepers:
they are no fewer than thirty-seven.
The four secret goddesses
are the later supreme perfection;
from all the secret goddesses
they truly arose right afterward.
The great-souled bodhisattva
should not count them among the thirty-seven.
From the heart of Vairocana
the supreme families of the Dharma truly arose.
Arisen entirely from the mind of awakening,
the great assembly of Akṣobhya and the rest
I declare to be the vajra family:
great-souled Vajrapāṇi.
Arisen entirely from the conduct of generosity,
Ratnasambhava and the rest
I explain to be the jewel family:
great-souled Ratnavajra.
Arisen entirely from the conduct of generosity,
arisen entirely in the pure Dharma,
Amitāyus and the rest
are explained as the lotus family:
great-souled Vajradharma.
Arisen entirely from accomplished action,
from Amoghasiddhi and the rest,
that is explained as the action family.
Gaganagañja is the great action.
As the vajra is, so too is the goddess;
as the goddess is, so, relied upon, it is alike.
As it is taught, so it is peaceful;
as it is peaceful, so it has arisen.
Vajrasattva, equal to Akṣobhya,
is seated in the eastern direction.
Vajraratna, the supreme jewel,
is seated in the southern direction.
The king, the protector Lokeśvara,
is seated in the western direction.
He who is unfailing in all deeds
will be seated in the northern direction.
In the center is the protector Vairocana,
the great yoga of omniscient wisdom,
peaceful like the full moon,
the unsurpassed vajra wheel.
Because it always goes before,
the vajra is endowed with the meaning of the mind of awakening;
from it comes conduct, from it the Dharma,
from it conduct that is hard to practice.
The unsurpassed great being
will abide in vajra yoga.
With a single voice all the buddhas
made supplication to the Protector;
Vajrasattva, Lord of Sages,
makes supplication to the Protector:
"Perfect awakening depends on you;
relying on you, the tathāgatas,
all the buddhas together with their heirs,
come into being by relying on you.
The primordial mind is the great yoga,
the mind of awakening, so very hard to find.
Alas, these sentient beings suffer;
they are not vessels for the Buddha's Dharma.
For the sake of all sentient beings,
teach the sublime maṇḍala of the wheel!"
Then King Vajradhara,
the lord of the supreme abode of all,
having heard the words of the Protector,
will teach the application of the wheel:
"I shall explain it properly.
The great wheel, the great city,
arisen from the heart of all the buddhas,
the supreme Dharma of the essence, endowed with yoga,
is the teaching of the buddha vajra.
These sentient beings are not amenable to training."

For the sake of their happiness
I shall explain vajra yoga.
The way of the vajra master's rite,
by which, having practiced yoga oneself as prescribed,
one will attain the awakening of a buddha:
that I shall explain fully first.
If all sentient beings there
enter into it with great steadfastness,
they will attain the very vajra of a buddha's awakening;
of that there is no doubt.
It comprises the Great Vehicle,
the utterly secret awakening of the buddhas,
and is taught as supreme liberation:
first one should listen to this supreme scripture.
Even if, for hundreds of eons,
one strives at it earnestly,
four kinds of person in the world,
though they practice, will not succeed:
one in whom the mind of awakening has not arisen,
one who is full of doubt,
one who does not act according to the command,
and one without faith: these will not succeed.
Just as those who have gone in the three times did,
having generated the mind set on awakening
and having faith in the Three Jewels,
to be free of doubt is supreme.
Householders and those gone forth:
both these sides should practice it.
As is fitting and appropriate,
I shall explain it fully, in order.
Having taken the mind of awakening as one's chief concern,
one should not strive after other gods.
One should have faith in the Three Jewels
and strive to be devoted to them.
Give up completely killing, stealing, sexual intercourse,
lying, and intoxicants.
Abiding in the vows of a householder,
one should then practice the king of knowledge mantras.
If one has gone forth,
one abides properly in the three vows:
the prātimokṣa, the mind of awakening,
and the vidyādhara vow, which is supreme.
For a month, or else for a year at a time,
or likewise for a single year,
if one desires the supreme accomplishment,
one should first undertake the knowledge observance.
Each day, as much as one is able,
one should perform the rite in three sessions,
or this procedure in four sessions,
as is fitting and appropriate.
One should confess all misdeeds,
rejoice in all merit,
make supplication to all the buddhas,
and dedicate that merit to awakening.
Likewise, to all the unsurpassed buddhas
of the worlds in the ten directions,
making supplication and bowing low,
one should prostrate at their feet in equipoise.
As is taught in the vast scripture,
through the application of the four mudrās
one should recite the vajra mantras
of all the other tantras.

Here the heart mantra is this: oṃ sarva tathāgata pūjopasthānāyā ātmānaṃ niryātayāmi sarva tathāgata vajra satva adhitiṣṭha svamāṃ. oṃ sarva tathāgata pūjā abhiṣekāya ātmanaṃ niryātayāmi sarva tathāgata vajra ratna abhiṣiñca māṃ. oṃ sarva tathāgata pūjā pravartanāya ātmānaṃ niryātayāmi sarva tathāgata vajra dharma pravartaya māṃ. oṃ sarva tathāgata pūjā kermaṇe ātmānaṃ niryātayāmi sarva tathāgata vajra karma kuru māṃ.

Together with the mudrās and endowed with the rite,
I shall explain it correctly.
Joining all the fingers together
is declared to be the vajra palms.
Seated in a clean place,
the wise one should prostrate with all the limbs.
Extending the vajra palms,
one should make offering to the Victors.
Having done likewise with the head,
the wise one should prostrate with the forehead.
Having lowered them from the head,
one should likewise prostrate with the mouth.
Likewise, having placed them at the heart,
one should prostrate from the crown of the head.
By this application of the rite
the vajra yogin should prostrate.
Then, abiding again in the rite,
endowed with the rite of offering,
with incense, flowers, and lamps,
scents, garlands, and ointments,
having recollected the Buddha,
one gives fearlessness to all sentient beings.
Through the application of the four kinds of giving,
the wise one should offer as much as one is able.
Clean, and wearing clean clothes,
together with pure companions,
one should take up the bases of training
as the unsurpassed vajra vow.
Through the application of the four kinds of giving,
one should have faith and be fully devoted to them:
the Buddha, the Dharma, and the Saṅgha,
and the master too, who is the last.
Each day, as much as one is able,
one should perform the four ablutions.
How the yogin is to bathe
I shall explain fully, in order.
Abiding fully in the three vows
is said to be the first ablution.
The second, as taught in the tantra,
is confession, supplication, and so on.
The binding of mudrās is the third,
and the fourth is water, which is the last.
By this rite of ablution
the wise one should bathe here.
The practitioner should perform the rite
of changing clothes three times.
Being well established in the vows
is the first change of clothes;
the second is accomplishing the rite;
the third is having a sense of shame and conscience.
These three clean garments
are to be changed three times.
Whatever is blameless
may all be eaten and made use of.
Meat, alcohol, garlic, and so on,
which are not to be eaten, should be avoided entirely.
Foul-smelling and impure
food and drink should be avoided entirely.
What involves harm to others
is not to be done even for the sake of one's body.
Sexual intercourse, together with its seven forms,
should be given up by meditating on the unattractive.
Conscientious, learned, and clear-minded,
reflecting on the meaning of the Great Vehicle,
on the vast way of the bodhisattvas,
the Perfection of Wisdom and the rest,
reading them and contemplating them,
one should make supplication again and again.
Toward enemies, friends, and those in between,
with an even mind, one should give fearlessness completely.
Speaking pleasantly and truthfully,
one should cultivate loving-kindness, which destroys aversion.
Phenomena and persons are without self,
and conditioned things are mere delusion:
so should the intelligent and steadfast examine.
One should not be deluded about any conditioned thing.
Even for the sake of one's life and living,
one should not abandon the mind of awakening.
The vajra, the bell, and the mudrās
should never be abandoned.

Whatever is the mind of awakening, that is the vajra;
the other, called the bell, is wisdom.
One should not despise the master;
the guru is equal to all the buddhas.
One should not be lax in application,
and should take up the rite that is taught as one's chief concern.
In practicing the six perfections
one should train in their proper order.
Endowed with the aim of benefiting sentient beings,
one practices the conduct of a bodhisattva.
Even toward a slight fault,
though it cost one's life, one should not be indifferent and let it pass.
Whether one is a householder or has gone forth,
one should give up all intimate familiarity.
Even from children and wife
one should free oneself from the bonds of existence.
Not greedy, attached, or hostile,
nor deluded or possessed of laziness,
with conscientiousness and great diligence
one should accomplish the rite of yoga.
Fully liberated from all views,
one sees well by means of reality itself.
Knowing the meaning of the explanation of the virtuous intent,
one will come to realize the meaning.
Knowing the rite, what is fitting and what is not,
highly skilled in the yoga of reality,
loving, endowed with the conduct of awakening,
highly skilled in the practices:
by such various ways
and by such a practice,
one attains accomplishment and liberation;
there is no doubt about any accomplishment.
One should give up all excess
in dwelling, clothing, and food.
One should not despise the gods,
and should give up all that is not to be done.
Without laziness, every day,
one should perform the rite of protection.
Where there are no obstructors, one should not be afraid;
it is this mind of one's own that is hard to tame.
Regarding beings as father and mother,
and brahmins and monks likewise,
pure, disciplined, and peaceful,
restrained, and knowing liberation,
not for kingship, heaven, or enjoyments,
not to increase the enjoyment of sense pleasures,
but rather for the sake of sentient beings,
one who has resolved on perfect awakening
should know moderation in food
and should wake from sleep at night as well.
Other faults, such as attachment,
are not to be entertained by the mind.
Having made the mind utterly clear,
unwavering and resolved,
speaking well and discerning fully,
one truly seeks the meaning of the Dharma.
As one would in the actions of fierce rites,
one should not injure or harm sentient beings.
Wicked and malicious beings
should not be admitted into the maṇḍala.
The bodies of the deities and their hand emblems,
the mounts of the deities,
a woman, the shadow of a deity,
all the signs of the deities,
the various kinds of food,
and signs made into images:
one should not step over them, nor eat them,
and should never tread upon them.
Food connected with the Three Jewels
should never be made use of.
Not even the scent of offerings or of a woman
should be smelled.
Within the mountains or the forest,
in a place clean, pleasing, and secluded,
or on a riverbank, or in a park,
or in a very pleasing meadow,
or in a clean and delightful temple,
or in a clean and secluded house,
wherever there is no crowd,
with mind focused on the Buddha,
together with pure companions,
having settled in such a place,
taught to be supreme for liberation,
one should properly begin the knowledge observance.
Through the application in four sessions,
one should prostrate to the supreme Protector,
and, meditating on the recollection of the Buddha,
should perform confession and the rest.
One should also make offering as much as one can.
Further, binding the mudrās properly,
one emanates all the articles of offering
throughout all the directions.
The entire ground, without exception,
filled everywhere with jewel trees,
and furnished with supreme things of every kind,
should be offered to the Victors.
Flowering trees and incense,
pleasing masses of scent,
and an ocean whose nature is jewels
are offered to the Lords of Sages.
Then in all the directions
one should imagine clouds of flowers, incense, and so on,
pleasing hosts of music,
and clouds of offerings.
These great knowledge mantras, which perform all offerings,
and the mudrās as well,
should be grasped with a fully lucid intention.

oṃ sarva tathāgata puṣpra pūjā megha samudra spharaṇa samaye hūṃ. oṃ sarva tathāgata vṛkṣa pūjā megha samudra spharaṇa samaye hūṃ. oṃ sarva tathāgata gandha pūjā megha samudra spharaṇa samaye hūṃ.

Wherever there are, for the great Victors, flowers,
incense, and masses of scent,
one should likewise arrange them properly everywhere,
even the strewn ornaments.
One should train in the great way of the offering mudrās
and in the great mudrā,
just as it is taught at length
in the compendium of the Great Vehicle.
In order to protect all sentient beings,
as a cause of great bliss,
and to free them from the three destinies,
one should hold such an intention.
Here, so that the heedlessness of all the gods may be pacified,
and the various sufferings of humans
brought fully to an end, and likewise,
so that those in the assembly who wish for the Lesser Vehicle
may be wholly turned toward it,
the great-souled vajra yogin
should contemplate this:
"By the power of my merit,
may all sentient beings, without exception,
come to possess great bliss,
the supreme gathering of all happiness!
May all sentient beings be happy!
May there be loving-kindness toward all creatures!
May all animals be free of fear!
May all the lower realms be pacified!"
Having contemplated this great bliss,
one should bind the mudrā that rends misdeeds.
Having rent apart all misdeeds,
and likewise all the lower realms,

Recollecting again and again
all the buddhas of all the directions,
one should read again and again
the secret sūtras without exception.
One should act with a mind that wishes
to become, single-mindedly, an irreversible bodhisattva.
One should accomplish the unsurpassed vajra yoga,
the great yoga.
For a month, or else for six months,
for a year, or for some other period,
one should perform the knowledge observance in this way.
Binding all the mudrās properly,
every day, to the buddhas,
one must offer the food offering.
The food offering bestows illumination;
likewise, at the food offering of the knowledge mantra,
one should recollect at all times
Vajrayakṣa, the great wrathful one.
The rite of the application of binding
should be made firm with Vajramuṣṭi.
Through the application of the vajra armor
one should protect the whole maṇḍala.
By such a rite
one should accomplish the work of yoga.
Having made the protection and set about diligence,
generating great strength and enthusiasm,
each day, having examined,
one should contemplate the maṇḍala of the procedure.
With the body colors as they are in the maṇḍala,
as is fitting and appropriate,
the actions, such as summoning,
should all be performed in meditative equipoise.
As much as one can, each day,
through this vajra application,
even one who has committed the five acts of immediate retribution
will fully succeed within one year.
In the assembly of the Great Vehicle
he becomes supreme; he becomes Vajrasattva.
In accordance with the rites of the other tantras,
together with the sūtras, just as they are,
one should then take up the knowledge observance,
and should not abandon the vows either.
Even for a fault the size of a particle of dust,
one should feel remorse.
One should properly examine the site,
free of thorns and the like:
a mountain or a hermitage,
a temple or a park,
the bank of a river, or a remote pasture,
wherever the area is clean.
Gravel, potsherds, clods,
and stones should be removed.
It should be purified very well,
because the deities take it fully as their own.
If one becomes lax in the application,
one should not be afraid in any way.
All the buddhas, the great kings,
the lords of the Dharma, full of loving intent,
possessed of compassion as their very essence:
the buddhas will not hold it against one as a fault.
The site should be purified throughout,
and the maṇḍala, as far as one is able,
should be made a league in breadth,
and not smaller than six cubits.
First one should drive in the pegs
and bind with the vajra and the rest.
Then all the requisites
should be taken up by the supreme rite.
Here the vajra master himself
should accomplish the maṇḍalas.
With ash and so on, according to the time,
the wise one should examine the maṇḍala.
As far as one is able and has the enthusiasm,
and as far as one's substances allow,
so should the great yogin do it;
or else one should examine it with the mind.
If the practitioner is a king,
the intelligent one, by another rite,
if he desires the supreme accomplishment,
should purify the maṇḍalas,
furnished with the five colored powders.
Best is one a thousand cubits in size;
or else, on a portion of ground
the size of an earshot, one should snap the lines.
If he does not possess the wealth,
it should be reduced to four cubits in brief,
or else made with eight cubits
in all directions.
Of deep blue sapphire, indranīla,
the blue powder should be made.
Ruby, padmarāga, deep red,
is said to be the red powder.
Vajra beak and great beak,
which also possess yellow,
together with pure and clear gold,
should be made to provide the yellow color.
With utterly pure crystal
the white powder should be made.
Because a king possesses great wealth,
these powders have been properly explained.
If he is rich and possessed of wealth,
and is a householder,
the maṇḍala should be a full hundred cubits,
and not made smaller than sixteen.
With powders endowed with scent,
mixed with saffron and the like,
furnished with the five colored powders,
the wise one should make the maṇḍala.
For one rich and possessed of wealth,
a merchant or a caravan leader,
or a householder possessed of means,
or if others come gladly,
one should make great powders
mixed with jewel dust.
Indigo, orpiment, and the like
are furnished as the five colored powders.
Whether the person is of small or great means,
his wealth should be examined,
and the powders applied as is fitting.
The measure explained for a king
holds likewise for a householder.
For a merchant and a caravan leader
the cubits are fifty.
If others come gladly,
it is made of twenty cubits.
If one wishes to accomplish siddhi,
it should not be smaller than six cubits.
Whether the materials are many or few,
they should be fully endowed with scent.
Always, with various scents,
