one should first purify the ground.
At all four corners
of the outer sides of the maṇḍala
the wise one should burn incense,
because the vajra bearers come.
Then, at that site of the maṇḍala,
with urine purified by mantra
one should always sprinkle it well,
because it then becomes vajra in nature.
For six, seven, or eight days,
butter, milk, and rice:
the master should eat the three white substances as food,
or else ordinary food.
If he has endurance, then day and night
he should practice without eating,
because food that is the nature of the Dharma
will without doubt make him strong.
The supreme maṇḍala, the great king,
whatever it is, is to be made by intention.

Beginning on the fifteenth,
it should be completed on the second full moon.
Or else, in the dark fortnight,
one should properly begin on the fourteenth,
carry it through to the fifteenth,
and complete it well.
Or else, beginning on the eighth day,
draw it all by the final full moon.
Or else, beginning on the fourteenth,
draw it properly over two days.
Or else, on the fifteenth,
begin it properly in the morning.
The wise one should draw the maṇḍala
in keeping with the great mode.
From the time one begins
until it has been completed,
if one is to go wholly without food,
one should undertake great diligence;
or else one should eat the three white substances,
fragrant and well prepared,
because the Vajradhara of the Victors
will then bestow his blessing.
On whatever pure day
is of great merit and auspicious,
on that day the vajra master,
of great fortune, should begin the maṇḍala.
Having assumed the sattva cross-legged posture,
he should sit in the central part.
Focusing on all the buddhas,
he should meditate on the recollection of the Buddha.
Holding the mind fully,
having made the body straight,
the actions such as confession
are all to be done first.
Diligent in the mind of awakening,
through practicing the knowledge observance,
having bowed to the ten directions,
he should generate enthusiasm in himself:
"Today my birth has borne fruit;
my life too has its fruit.
Through the pledge with the buddhas,
today I become their equal in this existence.
With the one-pointed mind of a bodhisattva
I shall become irreversible.
There is no doubt that I shall become
one born into the family of the Tathāgatas.
Today is my excellent day;
today my offering is unsurpassed.
Through inviting all the buddhas,
today I am supreme among the assembled."
Thus, endowed with joy,
then, seated in the center,
holding a stick of incense in the hand,
he should invite all the buddhas.
With a mind joyful and pleased,
he should speak these words.
Bowing low with all the limbs,
he should make supplication to all the buddhas:
"Buddhas dwelling in all directions without exception,
please consider me.
I, the vajra, am named so-and-so.
I wish to draw the maṇḍala.
Buddhas and all the rest, please come,
and grant me this accomplishment."
Having spoken these words,
he should assume the sattva cross-legged posture.
Having entered absorption in the great Dharma,
he should make himself possessed of samādhi.
If he is able, his fortune is great;
or else he should accomplish it with the mind.
In the supreme king of vajra maṇḍalas,
whatever the samādhi so called may be,
endowed with the vajra form,
the yogin should enter absorption.
Or else in whichever yoga it may be,
if able, he should enter absorption.
How the mind comes to be placed in equipoise
I shall explain in order.
Just as the Blessed One formerly
became a bodhisattva,
in just the same way one oneself
should contemplate the meaning of yoga.
One should think that one performs
various austerities and vows.
One should imagine the great assembly of buddhas
as the trichiliocosm.
The intelligent one should imagine the exhortation,
the unsurpassed exhortation by the buddhas.
Bowing to all the buddhas,
one should receive the command as one wishes.
Without fail, in a moon disc,
one should examine one's own mind closely.
Having properly generated the mind of awakening,
one should visualize a moon in the center.
Then one should visualize one's own wisdom
as a vajra in the center of the moon,
and one's own body in the form of a vajra,
the very nature of a buddha's form.
Then, having realized buddhahood,
one should confer empowerment on oneself.
One should view oneself
as all the buddhas, as vajra yoga.
On the summit of Mount Meru,
having descended from the Densely Arrayed abode,
and being seated on the vajra seat,
one should perform the magical display of a buddha.
Having brought forth the four sattvas,
one emanates them fully in all directions.
Having made the dharmadhātu firm,
one should set them in the four directions.
In the intermediate directions are all the perfect buddhas;
one blesses oneself properly.
In the four directions there arises
the great maṇḍala of the buddha's wheel.
Together with the wheel of the retinue,
one should view all the maṇḍalas.
Together with the sattvas and the secret goddesses,
one should imagine the gatekeepers.
The pure maṇḍala of the wheel,
the great essence, should be visualized.
One's own wheel is properly established,
endowed with the five wheels.
One meditates on the recollection of the marks
of all the buddhas of great means.
Even after rising from samādhi,
one should look with the vajra gaze.
Through the samādhi of Vajrasattva
one should enter absorption once again.
In the center of Vairocana's heart
one should meditate on the mind of awakening.
The form of the moon disc
should be taken from the Protector's heart.
In the presence of all the sattvas
one should receive the command in order.
Through the empowerment of all the buddhas
one should confer empowerment on oneself.
By supplicating, one should let them hear:
"I am the supreme maṇḍala of the king."
Through this application of the Dharma,
the utterly unsurpassed samādhi,
all the buddhas together with Vajradhara
will bestow their blessing.
Even after rising from samādhi,
one should receive the command,
and one should never abandon
oneself as Vajrasattva.
Breaking the cross-legged posture and rising,
one should bow at the feet of one's own guide.
One should view the four directions
as filled with an ocean of buddhas.
Having realized the Great Vehicle,
one should speak the vajra words:
"I myself am the Vajra Bearer;
I myself am Vajrasattva;
I am the Buddha, the great king;
I am the Vajra Bearer of great power;
I am the king, lord of yogins;
I am supreme Vajrapāṇi."
oṃ vajra satva hūṃ.
This secret mantra of the vajra
should be recited just once.
"I am the chief, the great vajra;
I shall not abandon the blessing."

Everywhere filled with an ocean of buddhas,
one is properly established in the fifth by means of the wheel.
Having brandished the vajra with pride,
one should take on the manner of vajra pride.
Then, putting on one's armor once again,
one should confer empowerment on oneself.
With the mudrā of Vajrayakṣa
one should cut off all the obstructors.
With the mudrā of the kneading hands
one should utterly crush all the obstructors.
With the vajra pestle itself
one should pound all the obstructors.
With the razor-sharp vajra sword
one should chop up all the obstructors.
With the fierce vajra fang
one should pierce all the obstructors.
With the mudrā of the excellent vajra
one should vanquish all the obstructors.
With the mudrā of the vajra sword
one should demolish all the obstructors.
With the renowned vajra fang
one should devour all the obstructors.
With the mudrā of the Vajra Chain
one should bind all the obstructors.
With the mudrā of the Vajra Hook
one should summon all the obstructors.
With the mudrā of the Vajra Noose
one should draw in all the obstructors.
With the mudrā of the vajra spear
one should pierce all the obstructors.
With the mudrā of the falling vajra
one should topple all the obstructors.
In elaborate form, as far as one is able,
one should summon all the bodies.
Thinking, "There are no obstructors at all,"
the mind becomes pure.
One's own mind is said to be Māra;
the obstructors too are one's own mind.
Clear away all conceptual thought:
it is from conceptual thought that obstructors arise.
Taking hold of the mind that is hard to tame,
one should give up the five sense pleasures.
Beginning first with recollection,
or else one becomes Vajrasattva.
Having crushed them and cut them off decisively,
one should drive them out with the syllable hūṃ.
With the mudrā of Vajrakarma
one should bind the vajra fence.
Sealing it with the vajra of Hūṃkara,
one surrounds it with the vajra fence.
With the great mudrā of Vajrasattva,
having properly blessed the maṇḍala,
in elaborate form, as far as one is able,
one should bind the supreme fence.
Having applied it decisively to all conceptions,
the mind becomes pure.
The characteristics of the maṇḍala
should be grasped by the wise,
and one's companions and all else
should be guarded without negligence.
Leveled and well plastered,
of the right measure and beautiful,
fragrant and smooth to the touch,
well made and firm,
endowed with sweet scent,
pervaded all over with various colors,
avoiding colors that are impure:
so should the Dharma maṇḍala be laid out with lines.
Then it is properly explained.
The supreme maṇḍala is great,
the great secret of all buddhas,
the great secret of all maṇḍalas,
the Vajra Bearer, steadfast among buddhas.
These sentient beings are not yet fit to be trained:
they have not gathered at all the accumulations,
the great ocean of merit and wisdom;
they lack the accumulation of merit
and are without longing for the coming of the buddhas.
In order to inspire devotion in those beings,
the great maṇḍala is taught.
One who possesses the coming of the buddhas
is admitted to hold the maṇḍala;
or else the accumulations of merit
he will see for himself.
Because, together with the accumulations, the enjoyment body
and the dharma body are unsurpassed,
one should have him hold the coming of the buddhas
that arises from the sacred Dharma of the vajra.
If he has gathered the accumulations,
he will first see the buddhas in succession;
abiding in the yoga of the maṇḍala,
he will see all the buddhas.
Without having gathered the accumulations,
and without having seen the buddhas,
he will not even get to hear of it,
let alone enter here first.
For this is the incomparable secret
of all the buddhas;
this is the heart of the Great Vehicle,
the unsurpassed wheel of Dharma.
It arose over countless eons,
the supreme perfection of the grounds.
Because one obtains the lofty secret,
one will accomplish the Joyous ground.
If the yogin is diligent,
in this very life he passes into nirvāṇa;
he attains the ground of the Joyous
and vajra-hood itself, without doubt.
Or else, merely by seeing it,
he passes into nirvāṇa within sixteen births,
and attains the Joyous ground
or else vajra-hood itself.
He makes the sun and moon fall to the earth,
and becomes of the very nature of space.
Even ordinary people will attain
buddhahood itself, and not otherwise.
The supreme yoga is unsurpassed;
therefore apply oneself to the great yoga.
If one desires buddhahood itself,
one should not sit idle, but strive with great diligence.
With the measurements just as they have been explained,
following the scripture accordingly,
one should lay out the lines of the outer maṇḍala,
for it is the abode of Vajradhara.
Four-cornered, with four doors,
it should be beautified with four archways;
endowed with four lines,
it is made beautiful by equal proportions.
It is adorned with silk tassels,
ornamented with jeweled lattices,
furnished with nets of small bells,
splendid with the five colors,
richly decked with nets of bells,
beautiful with various yak-tail whisks,
and graced with garlands, whisks, canopies,
and the sun and moon.
All around, on the outer places,
vajra rims are properly set;
everywhere it is furnished with a rim,
but the doors are without a rim.
The pinnacles are of the nature of vajra stone;
others are golden in color,
others red like a lotus,
and others white like crystal.
Of the nature of all the supreme jewels,
the maṇḍala should be drawn beautifully.
Its various forms are supremely beautiful,
worshiped by the hosts of vajra goddesses.
It is endowed with doors in two tiers
and furnished with various archways,
white and clear, and adorned
with vajras and with sun and moon.
It is endowed with four petals,
and its four doors are supremely beautiful,
lofty, spacious and lovely,
furnished with vajra upon vajra.
The vajra doorkeeper of great fortune,
holding the Vajra Hook in person,
the fierce protector, the yogin,
is stationed at the eastern door.
In the south is the Vajra Noose,
in the west the Vajra Chain.

The Vajra Bell of great fortune
is stationed at the northern door.
Whether one is entering or coming out,
one should recite the vajra force:
if one treads upon the drawing, even its lesser lines,
one will not see the samaya itself.
oṃ vajra begākrama hūṃ
At the time that one lays out the lines,
one should recite the vajra line mantra;
then every portion of the ground without exception
becomes entirely of the nature of vajra.
oṃ vajra samaya sūtra mātikrama hūṃ
First, on the powdered colors,
one should look intently with a blazing gaze.
When one says vajra rāga,
everything becomes of the nature of vajra.
oṃ vajra rāga hūṃ
All the junctions of the sections
and the corners of the door recesses
are adorned with vajra jewels.
One should lay out the lines of the outer maṇḍala,
with sides and sections equal,
its sections divided on every side,
and the sections of the maṇḍala distinct.
One should lay out the lines of the wheel maṇḍala.
Entering the tier inside it,
one should draw a wheel like it,
with sections equal all around,
beautified by eight pillars.
In the positions in front of the vajra pillars
it is adorned with five maṇḍalas.
In all of the five places
one should set out five wheels.
In the center of the wheel maṇḍala
one should draw and arrange everything all around.
Having gazed with the manner of vajra pride,
one should take up Vajrasattva.
oṃ vajra satva hūṃ
There, in the center of the maṇḍala,
one should place the king of buddhas,
from whose heart there emerged
the dense and steadfast host of secrets:
Vairocana, the great yoga,
who illuminates all directions,
by the mere mention of whose name
one is fully freed from every sin.
The great host of the wheel-turning monarch,
the four sattvas, the great kings,
should be placed in all the directions
by those who hold the vajra force.
In the east is Vajrasattva,
in the south Vajraratna,
in the west the one called Vajradharma,
and Vajrakarma is in the north.
The five Vajrasattvas
all wear the crowns of buddhas,
are adorned with every ornament,
and are clad in white garments.
Their heart mantras are these:
oṃ sarva yogīśvaraya hrī hūṃ
oṃ vajra satva hūṃ
oṃ vajra ratna hūṃ
oṃ vajra dharma hūṃ
oṃ vajra karma hūṃ
For Vajrasattva, the great vajra:
one should set down the five-pronged one.
For Vajraratna, the jewel;
for the vajra lotus, the lotus;
for Vajrakarma, the vajra;
for Vajratīkṣṇa, the great weapon.
Vairocana's own mudrā
is to hold the wisdom fist.
Endowed with the disk of the moon,
he abides in the midst of light.
All four great beings
arose from the Blessed One.
In the four corners of the central wheel,
where there is no wheel,
the four great queens, the goddesses,
should be placed; reflect on their true nature.
Sattvavajrī, Vajradharma,
and likewise Ratnavajrī,
and Karmavajrī, the great queen,
should be drawn in the four corners of the maṇḍala.
Sattvavajrī, the bodhisattva lady,
is the great and supreme perfection;
the great goddess Vajradharma
is the supreme Perfection of Wisdom;
the greatly glorious Ratnavajrī
makes a treasure of the accumulation of merit;
Vajrakarma, called Sky Treasury,
is the supreme perfection of action.
Next, these are their secret names:
the Greatly Glorious Vajra White Lady,
Vajra Tārā, and Sky Vajra.
Of all the family maṇḍalas,
they are the unsurpassed supreme secret.
All have the light of the full moon,
and are playful, with proud waists;
like the four aspects of the Victor,
their hands are busy with their own emblems.
With the playful poise of a lion,
having gone forth with vajra force,
in the eastern maṇḍala of Akṣobhya,
in the center of the moon,
Akṣobhya, holding the mudrā of the Teacher,
should be properly drawn and placed by the wise.
He holds its directions completely;
he should be properly drawn and placed by the wise.
In all of its directions
one should draw the four samayas:
in front is Vajrapāṇi,
on the right the Vajra Hook,
likewise on the left Vajrarāga,
and behind one should properly draw Sādhu.
Adorned with every ornament,
they have the empowerment of the split vajra.
Having gone forth with vajra force,
in the southern jewel maṇḍala,
in the center of the moon, is Ratnasambhava,
holding the mudrā of the supreme;
he should be properly drawn and placed by the wise.
In all of its directions
are the four jewel sattvas:
in front is Vajraratna,
on the right Vajratejas,
on the left Vajraketu,
and behind one should properly draw Hāsa.
Adorned with every ornament,
they have the empowerment of the vajra jewel.
Having gone forth with vajra force,
in the western lotus maṇḍala,
holding the wheel of Dharma,
Amitābha is seated in the center,
the great being of great wisdom.
In the four directions one should place:
Vajradharma in front,
on the right Vajratīkṣṇa,
on the left Vajracakra,
and Vajrabhāṣa behind.
Having gone forth with vajra force,
in the northern action maṇḍala,
holding the mudrā of granting the supreme,
one should place the Sage Amoghasiddhi.
In all of its directions
one should properly draw the great action deities,
all around in the four directions,
showing their own emblems in their hands:
in front is Vajrakarma,
on the right Vajrarakṣa,
on the left Vajrayakṣa,
and Vajramuṣṭi behind.
Next I shall explain the sixteen emblems
and the twenty including the central ones;
the sixteen and the twenty with the central ones
I shall explain in their proper order,
as is fitting and appropriate.
The great five-pronged vajra,
the jewel of Vajraratna,
the great vajra lotus,
and Vajratīkṣṇa: these are the central ones.
For Vajrapāṇi, the great vajra;
likewise the Vajra Hook holds the hook;
for Vajrarāga, the bow itself.

For Vajrasādhu, the double vajra;
for Vajraratna, the great and mighty jewel;
for Vajratejas, the sun;
Vajraketu raises a banner;
for Vajrahāsa, great grinning teeth;
for Vajradharma, a great lotus;
for Vajratīkṣṇa, a fine sword;
for Vajrahetu, a great wheel;
for Vajrabhāṣa, the letters;
for the great action, a sharp vajra;
for Vajrarakṣa, armor;
for Vajrayakṣa, great fangs;
for Vajramuṣṭi, the clenched fist.
The hook, the noose, the chain,
and the bell are for the four doorkeepers.
For Sattvavajrī, the great vajra;
likewise for the vajra jewel lady, a gem;
the lotus for Dharmavajrī;
and the sharp vajra for the action lady.
In the four corners one should set out
these secret emblems.
In all four corners
outside the great wheel
one should properly draw the vajra goddesses,
the four kinds of secret offering:
Vajralāsyā, Vajramālā,
likewise Vajragītā herself:
with pride, with garland, with lute,
and with song and dance, the secret goddesses.
In the corners of the outer maṇḍala
one should properly draw the offering goddesses:
flower, incense, likewise scent,
and the lamp in the northwest corner.
In between, beginning with the lamp,
all through the outer maṇḍala,
one should definitely set out
the thousand great beings of the Fortunate Eon.
With their signs, as is fitting,
Maitreya and the rest without exception:
one should set out the great beings
according to one's wish.
By this ritual procedure
one should draw and set out everything.
So that all may descend through their own heart mantras,
each has his own heart mantra.
These are the heart mantras:
oṃ vajra pāṇi hūṃ vajra aṃkuśa jaḥ vajra rāga ho vajra sādhu sa
These are the heart mantras of the supreme samaya.
oṃ vajra maṇi oṃ vajra teja a vajra ketu trāṃ vajra hāsa hā vajra padma hrī vajra tikṣṇa dhaṃ vajra hetu maṃ vajra bhāṣa raṃ
These are the heart mantras of the great empowerment beings and wisdom beings.
vajra karma kaṃ vajra rakṣa haṃ vajra yakṣa hūṃ vajra muṣṭi baṃ
These are the heart mantras of the great action beings.
These are the heart mantras for consecration.
Next, the heart mantras of the buddhas are:
vajra hūṃ ratna trāṃ dharma hrīḥ karma ā
Those of the goddesses have already been explained, and likewise those of the doorkeepers.
By this ritual procedure
one should set out the whole maṇḍala.
Next I shall explain the activity of the vajra master.
Everything should be drawn
by the vajra yogin whose mind is set on Dharma.
One should bind the mudrā of the sattva
taught in the Śrī Paramādya.
Realizing the Great Vehicle,
one should recite it seven times.
Around the wheel of the outer maṇḍala
one should circumambulate seven times.
The wise one should examine everything
to see that the wheel maṇḍala is complete.
If anything is lacking or in excess,
one should purify it with vajra force.
Having purified the great wheel,
one should properly take up the yoga of the buddhas.
One should fully perform the act
of offering oneself to the Victors.
Then, with every kind of article,
one should make offerings to the maṇḍala.
This is done according to one's means;
even if it is modest, there is no fault.
All the wealth one has, however much,
one should offer in its proper order:
a hundred thousand full vases,
or a thousand, or even a hundred;
a hundred thousand or a thousand lamps,
or else a thousand, or even a hundred;
a hundred thousand vessels of scent and incense,
or else a thousand, or even a hundred.
Camphor and aloeswood,
turuṣka, sandalwood and the like,
blended with many and fragrant:
these incenses should be burned.
Vessels of gold or of silver,
or even made of clay, are no fault.
All the articles there are, however many,
should be placed in the maṇḍala.
There, a thousand lamp bowls
with brightly blazing lamp wicks,
or at the least a single vessel:
even if incomplete, there is no fault.
Silk banners are raised aloft and spread out by the millions,
adorned with yak-tail whisks and garlands,
decorated with nets and half nets,
and beautified with half moons.
Various mounts, a hundred,
or even a thousand, are no fault;
even if all are lacking but one,
one should gladly perform the finest music.
Thousands of wholesome foods,
and varieties in an ocean of kinds,
and various snacks as well:
if some are lacking, there is no fault.
All scents and fine garments,
fragrant unguents,
supreme activities beautifully arrayed,
canopies of various kinds,
very pleasing vases
filled with water of various scents,
and flowering trees, a hundred,
or even four, will be no fault.

All scents and flowers,
and every delightful incense:
if one has the means,
one should make offerings of every kind.
Just as the practitioner presents
the offerings and the torma,
so will he attain accomplishments
and miraculous powers in this very life.
Just as was proclaimed before,
one should set out everything according to the rite.
One should present them to all the buddhas
and perform the worship thoroughly.
Then, having bowed to the central wheel
of the wheel of the maṇḍala,
and sitting with one's knees planted on the ground,
one should properly summon them.
Binding the sattva Vajra Hook,
one should recite the Vajra Hook mantra;
by reciting it twenty-one times,
the tathāgatas come from everywhere.
Then, having bound the doorkeepers,
one should fully recite the syllable jaḥ.
The Vajra Bearer summons them all,
and without doubt they come.
Having bound the Vajra Noose,
one should make them enter, according to the rite.
Having bound the Vajra Chain,
one should keep them always under control.
With the mudrā of the Vajra Bell
one should make them descend.
With the mudrā of vajra abiding
one should set them all in their places.
One should proclaim praises as best one can,
and then thoroughly perform the worship.
One should apply the mudrās of incense,
flowers and so forth to the whole maṇḍala.
Offering oneself, worship,
confession, supplication and so on,
and having generated the mind of awakening,
one should properly practice the vows.
With the great mudrā of Vajrasattva
one should confer empowerment on oneself.
One should snap one's fingers
and gather all the buddhas.
Having summoned all the buddhas together with their heirs
and caused them to enter fully,
one should properly bring down the Vajra Chain.
With the great mudrā of Vajrasattva,
opening it, one should properly bring it down.
The unsurpassed vajra wisdom,
pure wisdom, will arise;
the wisdom of the sattva will descend.
One should properly recite the vajra descent.
If it does not descend,
one should ring the bell with the syllable a.
Even such a vajra master
who commits the five acts of immediate retribution
will have sublime wisdom arise,
and through this he will also see wonders.
A hundred and seven more,
and signs beyond even those will appear.
And any others at all,
if they have great merit and fortune,
will see all the buddhas,
or else bodhisattvas,
or else any vajra holder,
or will see them by their radiating light rays.
The sound of thunder roaring haṃ,
incense and flowers in between,
hair standing on end and the like will occur.
Then the vajra master,
having come outside the maṇḍala,
endowed with the mudrā of the sattva lady,
should hold a garland with two fingers.
Entering in the manner of a dancer,
whirling in the vajra dance,
and fully entering the central wheel,
one should throw the garland.
Wherever the garland falls,
that very one is accomplished in an instant.
Having sat in the sattva cross-legged posture,
if one binds all the mudrās,
all accomplishments will certainly
be achieved; of this there is no doubt.
Moreover, Vajradhara
will bestow his blessing on him.
Rising up there, the Blessed One,
or else Vajradhara,
may tremble or move,
likewise the limbs may contract,
or else words may come forth,
or one may even say hūṃ,
and certainly one says, "Excellent!"
This miracle of great bliss
is wondrous and inconceivable;
the vajra master of great fortune
will see it; of this there is no doubt.
Then, rising, one should recite
the sacred hundred and eight names.
Having bowed down to the Protector, the Sage,
one should ask permission for whatever one wishes:
"Great ascetic, vajra master,
I, who am named such and such,
for the sake of benefiting all sentient beings,
shall bring in the disciples."
Whatever happiness belongs to all the sentient beings
of the three realms, moving and unmoving,
does not equal even a sixteenth part
of that of the vajra master.
The activity of the vajra master is complete.

Next the disciples should be brought in. Into that great king of maṇḍalas, the secret of all the tathāgatas, every realm of sentient beings, all without exception and with none left out, is fit to enter. One should not examine whether or not they are suitable vessels; one should take up eagerness to bring them in. Why is that? Because merely by seeing it, all sentient beings at once become irreversible. Even though they may have much lack of faith, roots of virtue not accumulated, mental continua not fully ripened, and lack of devotion, nevertheless, merely by seeing it, they become so. For this great secret teaches the method of training through the secret of the Dharma maṇḍala of all the tathāgatas, the mode of the wheel, the conduct of the perfections, and the magical displays of the bodhisattvas. Therefore, merely by seeing it, all the lower realms are at once cut off.

The heirs of the Victor are without fear;
the doors to the lower realms are closed,
all are inclined toward liberation,
and they become irreversible.
Having them go for refuge to the Three Jewels,
one should have them generate the mind of awakening.
With the welfare of all sentient beings in mind,
one should also confer the vows.

From then on, killing, taking what is not given, sexual misconduct, lying, grain liquor, brewed liquor, and whatever intoxicates should be given up.

First, these vows
should be given by the wise
according to the wish and inclination of each;
they should not be given against one's will.
If he has faith,
one should give him the vows.
Having established him in the three vows,
one should then show him the maṇḍala.
If he wishes for the supreme accomplishment,
he goes for refuge to the Three Jewels,
abides in the five precepts,
and strives supremely for the mind of awakening.
Those sentient beings who are very hard to tame,
who do not take up the vows
and who are also without faith:
even their entry should not be prevented.
Just as the compassionate buddhas
become embodied for them,
so, merely by seeing it,
the vows are accomplished in purity.
Those sentient beings who are wicked by nature,
who are angry and grossly abusive,
or who laugh and mock:
that itself becomes the cause.
Next I shall properly explain
the elaborate rite of entry.
As is fitting and appropriate,
one should bring in the vajra disciple.
He should prostrate with the four applications,
and also meditate on the vajra mantra.
According to the rite explained before,
the four prostrations are also to be done.
His upper garment should be red,
and his eyes too should be covered with red.
Having had him put on red cloth,
because it holds the body of the buddhas,
one should have him bind, very clearly,
the great mudrā of Vajrasattva.
The heart mantra is recited once,
and one says samaya staṃ.
With joy, delight and satisfaction,
he takes a garland with two fingers.
With these very heart mantras
the great master should bring him in.
samaya hūṃ
Inside the eastern door
one should properly recite the syllable jaḥ;
in the south, the syllable of the noose, hūṃ;
in the west, the chain, together with baṃ;
in the north, the bell, for awakening:
one should recite the syllable a.
Afterward, with playful music,
having summoned him, one has him bow.
Confession, supplication, worship,
full dedication and rejoicing,
and the offering of himself as well:
one has him do these in their proper order.
"Today I shall without doubt
generate the wisdom vajra.
You must not show it contempt,
or you will surely be ruined."
Having said this, one has him throw the garland,
and one should give him the vajra garland.
Wherever that garland falls,
it will be accomplished, without doubt.
oṃ vajra māle
Fully proclaiming the vajra words,
one should now remove the face covering.
One should give him certain confidence,
and say, "Look at the maṇḍala!"
"oṃ I am Vajrasattva himself,
arisen from the heart of the buddhas.
I shall open the vajra eye.
O, behold the vajra maṇḍala!"
One should give him the samaya itself
and bind the mudrā of the sattva lady upon him.
"This crushes your samaya":
one should speak these vajra words.
oṃ udakaṭha
Through this vajra secret mantra
Vajrapāṇi protects him.
"If the samaya is impaired,
this fruit will not be obtained.
This is your hell water:
if you transgress the samaya, it burns;
by guarding the samaya, you will succeed.
Drink! Drink the vajra nectar!"
With this heart mantra
of the oath of Vajrasattva, the oath is sworn.
"Vajrasattva himself
has today entered your heart.
If you speak of this way,
in that instant he will tear it open and depart."
Whoever does not take the vows,
is very hard to tame and without faith,
should be given entry only;
for him the rites are not to be performed.
One who has faith in the Three Jewels,
fears wrongdoing and is well restrained,
and is perfect in faith:
to him one should give the samaya.
What, then, is the samaya?
The Buddha, the Dharma and the Saṅgha:
these chief ones should be taken to heart.
Even for the sake of one's life,
one must never abandon the mind of awakening.
One should not disparage the deities,
nor ever the mantras and mudrās.
One should not despise the vajra master:
the guru is equal to all the buddhas.
Mounts, weapons, emblems,
the lesser lines, and the shadows of bodies
should not be trodden on carelessly,
and should never be eaten.
How I saw it before
should not be told to other beings.
The training in mantras and mudrās
should never be abandoned.
One should not kill sentient beings,
and should not delight in other vehicles.
Of the welfare of sentient beings and of saṃsāra
one should never grow weary.
The practice of the six perfections
should never be abandoned.

What should not be done, always abandon:
this is your pledge (samaya) and your vow.
If he is a supreme and worthy vessel,
pledges such as these should be declared to him.
If he is not, one should remain in equanimity,
or else let him be at ease, as he wishes.
Through all these applications,
in the compendium of all the maṇḍalas,
the supreme pledge of all the tantras
should be applied in every case.
By this ritual procedure
the vajra disciple should be brought in.
Through accomplishing Vajradharma
the supreme accomplishment should also be bestowed.
If the Vajra Bearer is himself accomplished,
the wise one should train others as well.
Wisdom having truly arisen in himself,
he should likewise produce it in others.
One who has seen the maṇḍala and knows the rite,
endowed with the pledges and accomplishment,
and endowed with faith as well, will succeed.
Here he becomes a master.
When he has made offerings,
he will succeed without any doubt.
Even one who commits the five acts of immediate retribution
will succeed within seven times.
To the vajra disciple one should say:
"From here you go to no other world.
You are fully freed from all suffering.
Here you become a Vajra Bearer.
Emanating the conduct of the buddhas,
you are born into the family of the tathāgatas.
In the prison that is the abode of saṃsāra
you will never again suffer.
Today your birth bears its fruit;
equal to all the buddhas
you have today become.
Touch your crown to me with reverence.
Your body has crossed beyond the three vehicles;
you will not be born into unfavorable states.
Through the conduct of awakening you are without fault;
you will attain the ground Perfect Joy.
Vajra Bearer, strive!
What others attain in sixteen lifetimes
you will attain in this very life.
Though the heirs of the Victor attain the grounds,
they have not seen the secret maṇḍala.
As you have entered into it,
not so is it with trust in the other vehicles.
In each and every birth
the Vajra Bearer will be diligent in teaching.
At the end of sixteen births at most
he will attain peaceful buddhahood.
The state of the Victors is attained
through hardships over two countless eons;
without entering the secret vehicle,
it comes about through sixteen.
He will not be seen by any bhūtas.
The moving and unmoving beings of the three realms
and all the hosts of malevolent gods
will not overpower the Vajra Bearer.
Fevers, grahas, poisons and mudrās,
ḍākinīs and others with terrifying faces,
and other beings full of pride
will never again overpower you.
Even over a hundred eons
the qualities of the vajra would still be told.
You will see the supreme secret essence;
you will never be afraid."
Bind the mudrā that is the nature of the sattva,
and with great diligence make it firm.
What should be said is this,
the unsurpassed descent:
"This pledge vajra
is declared to be called Vajrasattva."
The unsurpassed vajra wisdom:
by that very thing one should make it descend.
vajra āveśa aḥ
Then, as soon as it has descended,
one gains the six superknowledges,
and through sublime wisdom, in an instant,
joy and bliss never known before,
supreme delight and contentment,
and all the knowledges become manifest.
The miraculous powers are brought to perfection.
With these very heart mantras
one then releases the mudrā at the heart.
Vajrasattva, the great king,
will dwell in the heart forever.
oṃ tiṣṭha vajra dṛḍho me bhava śāśvato me bhava hṛdayaṃ me adhitiṣtha sarva siddhi ma me prayaccha hūṃ ha ha ha ha hoḥ
Then, with this heart mantra,
the garland should be cast.
pratīccha ho
Wherever the garland falls,
the deity of that place is his family.
In due sequence, the great secret
of the maṇḍala should be revealed.
Even by merely seeing the maṇḍala,
the ground of buddhahood is beyond doubt.
Taking the water of the vase,
fragrant and pleasing,
one should empower with this heart mantra,
the supreme washing away of suffering.
oṃ vajra abhiṣiñca
Then, with whichever mudrā is suitable,
one should give the garland and empower.
In the empowerment of the vajra name,
the sound of the name should be properly joined.
oṃ vajrasatva abhiṣiñcami vajranāma abhiṣekata he vajranāma
This is the extensive rite for bringing in the disciple.
The unsurpassed vajra wisdom:
in that wisdom he should be trained.
Whichever of the four it may be
should be asked according to his fortune.
If he speaks of the accomplishment of wealth,
it should be explained in four ways.
The four great wisdoms of wealth,
the accomplishments, should be taught.
Where one hopes a treasure lies,
there one sits cross-legged,
and if one meditates on a vajra at the heart,
one will clearly see where the treasure is.
vajranidhi
One should meditate on a vajra in the sky
and gaze carefully into a mirror.
Where it is seen to fall,
there, it is shown, a treasure lies.
ratnanidhi
Meditate on a vajra on your own tongue,
and you yourself will say, "It is here."
dharmanidhi
Vajrakarma truly descends;
where it descends, there it is shown.

karmanidhi
If he speaks of the vajra accomplishment,
the four kinds should be taught.
Seeing water as having the nature of vajra,
one will delight in deep meditative equipoise.
Seeing water as having a solid nature,
one will walk upon the water.
vajra jala
Oneself endowed with samādhi,
one should rejoice again and again.
If one meditates on oneself in a pleasing form,
one will become like it.
vajra rūpa
Oneself equal to the nature of space,
meditating on the very absence of intrinsic nature,
if one brings about the vajra descent,
one will quickly become invisible.
vajra ākāśa
If one meditates on a vajra ladder in the sky
and climbs upward on it,
as far as the place one climbs to,
so far will one travel through the sky.
vajra mahā
If he says he wishes to be a vidyādhara,
the four kinds of wisdom should be given.
Dwelling in the center of a moon disc,
oneself abiding in the sky,
one should meditate fully on oneself
as Vajrapāṇi, holding the vajra in hand.
One will become a vajra vidyādhara.
If one wishes for the Dharma seal,
whether seated or otherwise,
if one meditates on oneself as abiding there,
and, abiding thus, meditates well
on a vajra lotus in the hand,
oneself as Vajradharma,
one will become a lotus vidyādhara.
vajradhara padmadhara
Seated on a moon disc,
oneself fully endowed with action,
oneself abiding in the sky,
one will become an action vidyādhara.
Oneself as Vajraratna,
and likewise oneself abiding there,
if one meditates on jewels in the hand,
one will become a jewel vidyādhara.
karmadhara ratnadhara
If he speaks of the buddha accomplishment,
the four kinds of wisdom should be declared.
If one has meditated constantly in the sky,
the body will become light,
and one will attain the state of miraculous power;
the vajra self swiftly flies up.
vajra ṛdhi
This whole world of a trichiliocosm
is shown to be entirely of vajra nature.
As far as the vajra self wishes,
so far will it fly in an instant.
vajra vajra
Pure, of the nature of the dharmadhātu,
with limbs endowed with lotuses,
if one meditates well on oneself as pure,
one will attain the five superknowledges.
śuddha śuddha
If, with diligence day and night,
one imagines Vajrasattva
dwelling in the expanse of space,
one will oneself become Vajradhara.
If one conceives of all these three realms
as the forms of buddhas,
and accomplishes steady recollection of them,
one will become a buddha.
satva satva buddha buddha
When she becomes the secret goddess,
she becomes the deity of his family.
The pure wisdom should be declared,
and she should also be made to swear an oath.
As taught in the Lord of Secret Sūtras,
so one should enter the bhaga.
Having caused all to enter by the mind,
one emanates them together with her body.
Emerging from the heart of Akṣobhya
and from the heart of Vairocana,
and having entered her heart,
all the buddhas are emanated together.
Abiding in the yoga of samādhi,
in equipoise, one should clap.
By clapping the vajra palms,
all accomplishments come under one's power.
vajra pāśa
Emerging from the heart of the Jewel Lord
and from the heart of Vairocana,
and having caused all the buddhas to enter,
oneself as the vajra goddess,
forming the vajra palms,
one should clap the palms of the vajra binding.
One should accomplish the vajra yoga
and truly strike the vajra wisdom.
Having performed the vajra descent,
one likewise enters the bhaga.
Forming the vajra palms,
one should clap with the fingertips together.
Desire, aversion and delusion,
ever bound up with the afflictions,
the obscurations called afflictions,
of a hundred kinds, are destroyed in an instant.
vajra hana
Likewise, abiding in the bhaga,
one emanates the bodies of the buddhas together.
Oneself as the vajra goddess
abides in the heart of Vairocana.
Having bound the vajra goddess,
one should say vajra vajra twice.
If one then releases the vajra binding,
it is the supreme destroyer of all suffering.
vajra hara
Next, the wisdom mudrā for accomplishing Vairocana:
Just as in the earlier samādhi,
one should fully enact the vajradhātu.
Abiding well in the five yogas as well,
even buddhahood is not hard to find.
oṃ citta pratibodhaṃ karomi oṃ bodhicitta utpādayāmi oṃ tiṣṭha vajra oṃ vajra ātmako haṃ oṃ yathā sarva tathāgatā stathā ahaṃ
Through these five yogas
one truly accomplishes the five mudrās.
Oneself as Vairocana,
one will attain all accomplishments.
These are the mudrās of the perfect awakening of all the tathāgatas.

Seated in the sattva cross-legged posture,
one should hold the vajra, brandishing it.
Through the mudrās of body, speech and mind,
one becomes Vajrasattva oneself.
vajra satvā haṃ
The great and supreme among the practices
I shall now correctly explain.
Proclaiming it oneself with vajra speech,
one should accomplish the vajra yoga.
Brandishing the vajra with pride,
one should truly assume the bearing of vajra pride.
Having oneself become the vajra descent,
one should meditate on oneself as the one called Vajra.
vajrasatvā haṃ
Endowed with all the supreme aspects,
revealing the maṇḍala without remainder,
in the form of a garland, radiant,
endowed with ornaments, garments and colors:
one should recite ḍa ba ta
and recollect the Great Sattva.
The wisdom sattva is unstained,
until one sees the lord of joy.
vajrasatva dṛśya
Having gazed at the sattva before one,
one should truly cause him to descend into oneself.
oṃ vajra satva aḥ
That body, beautiful and great,
one should truly draw in with the Vajra Hook.
jaḥ hūṃ vaṃ hoḥ
With this vajra mantra
the great-souled one should gaze.
Into the heart of Vajrasattva,
one should cause it to enter his heart once more.
samaya aḥ
Having said this, the yogin,
abiding upon the moon,
performs whatever acts he wishes,
and should accomplish even buddhahood,
just like this Vajrasattva.
Then, by this application,
one should meditate on all the Vajra Bearers
holding their own mudrās.
To whichever one one is devoted,
one should likewise recite that heart mantra.
By this vajra application
one becomes equal to the Vajra Bearer.
Having fully accomplished the recollection of the Buddha,
one should turn the wheel of Dharma.
Holding one's own mudrā,
through accomplishing the mind of Vajrasattva,
one will attain the state of a universal monarch.
With royal acts going before,
one should please and empower.
The sessions are brought to completion,
and there will be the laughter and joy of Dharma.
The wheel of samādhi and dhāraṇī,
the unsurpassed secret treasury,
ripening and liberation itself,
the supreme steadfastness of the buddhas' awakening:
from this great secret rite
countless mudrās have truly arisen,
fully endowed with the divisions of the families,
and they have been proclaimed at great length.
One who has not donned the armor does not become a vessel.
Those with laziness, anger and distraction
are surely unable to hold it;
therefore it has been proclaimed in brief.
The Lord of Sūtras, the Secret King:
then the mudrās are explained.
The realization of the Great Vehicle
should be regarded as a precious vessel.
The Realization of Wisdom is complete.

The Essence of the Vajradharas.
That from which the wheel truly arose,
the source of all the yogas,
the supreme secret, I shall explain.
What is explained as the central wheel
is proclaimed as the Display,
the display of the bodhisattvas,
fully revealing the wheel of means.
It is explained as five wheels,
and the means too are of five kinds.
The divisions of the secret abide there,
revealing the display of the wheel.
The central one is the mind of awakening.
The east is the four ways of gathering.
What is explained as the assembly of the south
is endowed with the accumulation of merit.
The samādhis and the dhāraṇīs,
free of grasping at dharmas,
are what is declared for the west.
The north is the ripening of sentient beings.
The four outer corners
are with the four immeasurables:
loving-kindness, compassion, equanimity,
and what is called joy.
What is explained as the measuring cords
arose from the path of the stream of Dharma:
this purity of all views
is free of the ground of the vehicles.
What is called the rim
is samādhi, the pure nature of reality,
explained as the vajra goddesses,
and called the doors of dhāraṇī.
What are explained as the gates
are the four doors of liberation.
The archways and the nets
are the city whose nature is the treasury of Dharma.
The nets, half-nets and canopies
are the display of various miraculous powers.
What is explained as the colored powders
is the accumulation of awakening of that kind.
What is explained as the lamps:
there is the supreme light of Dharma.
What is explained as the scents
is the unsurpassed scent of renown.
What is the ointment of miraculous power
is anointing with the scent of moral discipline.
The various foods
are the unsurpassed food of Dharma.
Joy of various kinds is explained;
all conceptual thought is abandoned.
What is explained as the city
is the supreme city of liberation.
What is explained as the maṇḍala
is the city whose nature is the essence of Dharma;
it is said to be called "free of sickness,"
and that is called the maṇḍala.
In the center, the one called Vairocana
is all dharmas gathered into one:
the Buddha-king, the great chief,
fully illuminating all the directions.
He is explained as Vajrasattva,
the supreme nature of the mind of awakening.
Therefore the king is the mind of awakening.
From the mind of awakening there truly arose
Vajrarāja.
The mind is said to be of four kinds:
ferrying across, liberating, giving relief,
and the hook toward nirvāṇa.
It is explained as Vajrarāga:
applying oneself to the conduct of awakening.
It is explained as Vajrasādhu:
revealing thorough application.
It is explained as Vajraratna:
fulfilling the wishes of sentient beings.
It is explained as Vajratejas:
making the application of Dharma shine.
It is explained as Vajraketu:
freeing sentient beings from saṃsāra.
It is explained as Vajrahāsa:
cutting the doubts of sentient beings.
It is explained as Vajradharma:
the unsurpassed wisdom of Dharma.
It is explained as Vajratīkṣṇa:
cutting and destroying the afflictions.
It is explained as Vajrahetu:
revealing the display of the wheel.
It is explained as Vajrabhāṣa:
teaching the vajra Dharma.
It is explained as Vajrakarma:
supreme in taming sentient beings.
It is explained as Vajrarakṣa:
supreme in freeing sentient beings.
It is explained as Vajrayakṣa:
exhausting the noose of saṃsāra.
It is explained as Vajramuṣṭi:
making supreme awakening firm.
Sattvavajrī and Vajradharma,
the vajra ladies of jewel and of action:
the vajra jewel, the great Dharma,
arisen from the secret of action.
The vajra goddesses, eight more;
the supreme perfections, the remaining eight.
The Dharma lady is the Perfection of Wisdom;
the action lady is the supreme of means.

The ten sublime perfections
are truly explained as the ten grounds.
One who guards the city of the sublime Dharma
is explained as the one called a bodhisattva.
How wonderful the wisdom! How wonderful the Dharma!
How wonderful the supreme city of liberation!
How wonderful the wheel of display
taught by the Lords of Sages!
Through this application of Dharma
the Protector establishes beings in liberation.
Where the buddhas have crossed over,
they free sentient beings as well.
This is accomplished as the nature of Dharma;
this is the chariot of the nature of Dharma;
this is the essence of the nature of Dharma;
this is the city of the nature of Dharma.

From the Vajraśekhara, the great secret yoga, the great king of rites of the supremely secret reality, the exposition called the Lord of Secret Sūtras of All the Tathāgatas, the first chapter, is complete.

Unsurpassed among all accomplishments,
the secret I shall now explain.
The vajra vidyādhara
should be applied by oneself.
In accordance with the rite,
I shall explain the binding of the mudrās.
They should be bound as is fitting;
otherwise they will not succeed quickly.
One should bind the joined palms:
first, one should join the palms evenly.
Then one binds the vajra palms,
and should bind the vajra binding.
Having bound the fingers in the vajra binding,
reciting the heart mantra, one should tear it open.
Then the mudrās come under one's power;
they will succeed, of this there is no doubt.
vajra bandha traṭ
This should be recited first,
and then the mudrā truly bound.
Before whomever it is bound,
one truly blesses oneself.
mahā samaya satvā haṃ
The wise one should contemplate the descent
and cause the sattva to descend upon himself.
Having bound the samaya mudrā,
one should also recite the heart mantra.
a
By merely binding the mudrā,
it fully abides in all the limbs.
The heart mantra that should be recited:
these are the heart mantras.
samayo haṃ
Then, having blessed oneself,
one truly recollects one's own deity.
With these very heart mantras
the heart mantra should be recited.
samaya satva adhitiṣṭha sva māṃ
Devoted to accomplishing the mudrās,
the wise one then accomplishes them.
The great samaya sattvas,
of four kinds, should be accomplished.
Here, the secret heart mantras of these are:
artha siddhi vajra siddhi vajra vīrya buddha siddhi
Then, the making firm of the mudrā:
oṃ vajra satva samaya manupālaya vajva satva tveno patiṣṭha dṛḍho me bhava sutoṣyo me bhava supoṣyo me bhava anurakto me bhava sarva siddhi mme prayaccha sarva karma su ca me cittaṃ śreyaḥ kuru hūṃ ha ha ha ha hoḥ bhagavān sarva tathāgato vajra ma me muñca vajrī bhava mahā samaya satva āḥ
Even one who commits the five acts of immediate retribution
becomes like Vajrasattva.
All the mudrās will succeed,
and there is no doubt that all will be accomplished.
Next, the releasing of all the mudrās is common to all.
Having bound all the mudrās,
at the end one should empower oneself.
With the fingertips, the great garland,
in four ways, or else in three.
oṃ vajra ratna abhiṣiñca
The wise one should bind on the armor
and empower as far as one is able.
With these very heart mantras
all the mudrās should be made firm.
vajra ka pa ce na vaṃ
They are released where they arose:
with these very heart mantras
one should release them in that place;
the act is not performed elsewhere.
oṃ vajra muḥ
One should please the protectors with the palms,
and should bind the vajra palms.
With these very heart mantras
one should please all the Vajra Bearers.
vajra tuṣya ho
One should release by this rite;
it is the supreme releasing of the mudrās.
If all the mudrās become firm,
the Vajra Bearer will be pleased.
Every day, in the morning,
well restrained and keeping it secret,
one should recite Vajrasattva as much as one wishes;
by mere recitation one will succeed.
vajrasatva
By reciting the secret syllable hūṃ
one will see the five aspects.
Through the rite of secret application
one will accomplish Vajrasattva.
In the center of a moon disc
one should meditate fully on a second moon.
Oneself endowed with the Buddha,
one should meditate in the center of the vajra.
In short, in the center of the vajra
one should place the syllable hūṃ.
Through this vajra application
one should accomplish the syllable hūṃ.
hūṃ
Thus, in sixteen ways,
one should meditate well on all the hand emblems.
By placing them well in the center of the moon,
oneself accomplishes all in an instant.
Or else, by applying the syllables,
one should accomplish by placing them in the center.
Through the application of the moon
the syllables ja and the rest will not succeed.
By applying well the syllables ho and sa
and the syllable oṃ and the rest,
on the moon oṃ a traṃ,
one should fully accomplish the syllable haṃ.
hrīḥ and dhīḥ and maṃ paṃ raṃ,
kaṃ haṃ hūṃ vaṃ should be accomplished.
Through these vajra words
one should accomplish by vajra recitation.
Oneself as Vajrasattva,
or by fully applying the sixteen as oneself,
one should accomplish according to the yoga tantra
and meditate in the center of the moon.
Next, the rite of the maṇḍala in accordance with reality:
Beginning from sunrise,
offering oneself and so forth,
one should make offerings to all the buddhas.
One should beg the Vajra Bearers for forbearance.
One should bind all the mudrās
and please them with the vajra palms.
The sublime hundred and eight names,
having done all this, one should recite.
Three times and seven times
one should also circumambulate.
The great mudrā of Vajrasattva
the wise one should tear open.
The great mudrā of Vajrasattva
one binds above, in the center of the wheel.
That by which the mind has been purified:
one truly binds all the mudrās.
oṃ vajra muḥ
Having recited this heart mantra,
one binds all the mudrās above.
For the gates and the outer offerings
one should recite the vajra mu.
This secret is the great secret.
The vajra vidyādhara,
the wise one does not teach to all;
one gives it to the supreme one who keeps the vows,
to another who is a truly worthy vessel.
That is the vow of the vidyā:
the vajra in the right hand,
and in the left the bell, should be given.
The vajra vow, the great secret:
this heart mantra should be given.
Having given it, one has him hold it at the heart.
It is the supreme accomplisher of all.

This is that of all the buddhas:
Vajrasattva abiding in the hand.
You too must always hold it,
firm in the vow of Vajrapāṇi.
oṃ sarva tathāgata siddhi vajra samaya tiṣṭha eṣa tvaṃ dhārayāmi vajrasatva hi hi hi hi hūṃ
Then, with all made equal,
one should release the maṇḍala with the mudrā.
vajra satva muḥ
The activities of the vajra master are complete.

Just as it is fully endowed with the rite,
the secret vajra maṇḍala,
Vajradhara, the great king,
should likewise be accomplished in every way.
Vajradhara, the great king,
the supreme of all, the sublime lord,
spoke the compendium of the yoga tantras,
the supreme secret.
How wonderful! The Buddha, the great protector,
appeared in order to practice the conduct:
from the protector of Dharma, the great yoga,
all the tantras truly arose.
There is no buddha and there is no sattva,
no yoga and no accomplishing of yoga,
no grounds and no supreme secret;
and yet they arose for the sake of those to be tamed.
The supreme Buddha and the supreme samaya,
the accomplishment of the supreme sattva,
the supreme secret, the supreme conduct of the sattva:
the vajra yoga truly arose.
Recollecting supreme qualities, supreme among the samayas;
recollecting supreme qualities, the sublime crown ornament;
greatly renowned, holding the supreme conduct;
the supreme conduct of those endowed with supreme qualities.
Great vajra, wholly restrained,
great yoga, practicing the great conduct,
holding the qualities of the great secret,
bestowing the true arising of great bliss.
Endowed with recollection of supreme qualities, the supreme diadem,
adorned by the arising of the supreme assembly of samayas,
unmoving, utterly supreme, granting the supreme, stainless,
supreme conduct, supreme of conduct, stainless.
Encircled by hosts, laughing greatly,
destroying the harm-doers, proclaiming traṭa traṭa,
laughing and laughing a laughter that makes things tremble,
supreme fierce qualities, cala cala pa,
daha daha paya, destroying the harmful ones,
hara hara hare hare, destroying with supreme action,
accomplishing the great abode by power,
liberating and liberating by the power of the supreme family.
ha ha hā ha ha hā ha ha hā
hi hi hī hi hi hī hi hi hī hi hi hī
hu hu hu hu hu hu hu hu hu hu
The supremely excellent self, led forth by the wise:
ṭa ṭa ṭī ṭa ṭa ṭī ṭa ṭa ṭī ṭa ṭa ṭī ṭa ṭa ṭī ṭa ṭa ṭī ṭa ṭa ṭī
ṭu ṭu ṭya ṭya
nya nya ṭya ṭya ṭya ṭya ṭya ṭya ṭya ṭya
The conduct of ornament, trembling and making tremble,
has become the supreme of the conducts of conduct.
Practicing the conduct, the supremely excellent self,
the samaya ornament, honored by the six classes of beings,
the great lord, destroyer of the ravishers,
bringing to accomplishment the practices of the ravishers,
granting the supreme, the self of the supreme, truly arisen,
ta te ṭa te, possessing the eye of the teaching.
Supreme, arisen from the syllable ja; hūṃ is the first application of action.
ho ho, endowed with supreme passion, revealing the woman; sa bestows the taking.
oṃ is the first, unsurpassed; ṭa ṭa ṭa ṭi sa; a is splendor blazing.
traṃ traṃ perfects the yoga of passion; ha joins in laughter.
hrī makes one a holder of Dharma, sharp and stainless; hi is the great wheel of cause.
haṃ and raṃ bind the application of the speech that delights the sky; a makes the manifold.
haṃ is protection; tra ta splits apart; ta ṭa ta ṭi sa; hūṃ eats and chews.
vaṃ gives joy and is joy; cha ta cha ti ṭa gives utter joy.
haṃ, with the teaching of the vajra, firm bliss, together with passion, gives joy.
ho, the protector, has become the cause of supreme birth; hri is the very nature of joy.
hi establishes the conduct, the supreme casting off of dust, encircled by the protectors of the three times.
Secret and secret, supreme of all qualities, stainless; ho is the supreme conduct of awakening.
Supreme of the supreme, granting the supreme, attaining the supreme state; the syllable hūṃ is the supreme granting.
Destroying all the stains of the afflictions and radiating out, armed with the weapons of the fangs of hūṃ;
beautifully adorned with supreme and manifold joy, truly interwoven with the syllable oṃ;
he ho haṃ is pleasing; with hūṃ, utterly destroying, surely destroying the harmful ones, the very self.
Supreme bliss, with song and music,
honored by goddesses of beautiful form,
with a radiant face white as the moon,
marked by the heaped qualities of the vajra secret;
adorned with every jewel, delightful,
beautified by canopies of jewel netting,
honored and beautified by the assembly of vajra goddesses;
the abode of bodhisattvas, without fear,
stainless, purified through all the grounds,
whose very nature is the full realization of the conduct of awakening,
endowed with the food of the fully realized practice of Dharma,
the great joy produced by that merit;
abiding truly in the fourfold wealth of the abodes of Brahmā.
With the lines laid out in the order of the supreme Dharma,
the wheel and its rim drawn most beautifully,
the Chief resides in the vajra maṇḍala.
Beautified by the rim of the supreme samādhis,
with assemblies of offerings by the hosts of dhāraṇī deities,
adorned by the vehicle of liberation and the gate of loving-kindness,
the Guide resides in the vajra maṇḍala.
The perfections, the queens, perform the dance,
delighting in the display that ripens beings;
endowed with the food of meditative concentration, adorned with the supreme superknowledges,
the Guide resides in the vajra maṇḍala.
That maṇḍala has the nature of reality of the Chief;
all the essences that reveal the nature of reality have entered it;
through the beautiful gathering of the hosts of all the buddhas,
it is explained as the one called the Secret of the Sugatas.
Vajrasattva, the great and sublime lord,
source of all secrets, abides truly in samādhi.
He beholds the nature of reality of Vairocana, the Sage,
the supreme true source of dhāraṇī and samaya.
In accordance with the Dharma of joy, the vajra truly abides.
He, Vairocana, chief Lord of Sages,
definitively brought forth the great maṇḍala of the families.
hūṃ
It arose like the utterly pure nature of reality,
supreme of all the sublime lords, the buddhas,
with one-pointedness: how wonderful, how greatly joyful!
The body of the Buddha too has the form of a woman,
abiding truly in order to tame the realm of beings.
The form of a woman too is the pure nature of reality,
which has become the abode of the lineage of all the buddhas.
Arising from your own great family, come forth!
oṃ vajra dhātvīśvari hūṃ oṃ vajra vajrīṇi hūṃ oṃ ratna vajrīṇi hūṃ oṃ dharma vajrīṇi hūṃ oṃ karma vajrīṇi hūṃ
The supreme first, the five marvelous secrets,
made by the five buddhas, have the form of women.

together with the marvelous ones, they abide as sublime sovereigns.
The vajra secret, the supreme secret of the nature of reality,
having come to dwell through the union of the wheel,
dwells in the east through vajra yoga.
This is declared the maṇḍala of the wheel of the family.
Each one, too, is endowed with supreme yoga;
supreme is what is fully explained as the wheel of yoga.
By it the maṇḍala of the supreme secret,
which arose supremely as Akṣobhya, was taught:
oṃ vajra satva guhya samaye hūṃ oṃ guhya vajra satva aṃ kuśa hūṃ oṃ vajra guhya rāge rāgaya hūṃ oṃ vajra guhya sādhīśvari hūṃ
The maṇḍala of Vajraratna having been shown,
by him, again, four heart mantras were spoken:
oṃ vajra guhya ratna samayaṃ hūṃ oṃ vajra guhya prabha hūṃ oṃ vajra dhvajāgra guhya hūṃ oṃ vajra guhya hāsa hūṃ
Vajradharma is the great sublime sovereign;
by him, again, four heart mantras were spoken:
oṃ vajra dharma guhya samaye hūṃ oṃ vajra ghoṣa guhya hūṃ oṃ vajra guhya maṇḍale hūṃ oṃ vajra guhya vāca samaye hūṃ
Vajrakarma worships through complete purity,
beautiful, abiding fully in all the wheels;
having become supreme in the Dharma, the sublime, supreme sovereign,
by him the four heart mantras are spoken:
oṃ vajra guhya karma samaye hūṃ oṃ vajra guhya kavace hūṃ oṃ vajra guhya daṃṣṭra dhāraṇi hūṃ oṃ vajra guhya muṣṭi hūṃ
As before, abiding fully in the city of the wheel,
beautiful, abiding fully in vajra form,
these very ones are the sons of the buddhas, together with the vajra;
yet now they are emanated in the form of women.
They blaze intensely, purifying existence,
purifying attachment in the city of liberation,
pure through reversing thought and conceptuality,
delighting in the Dharma, from which the supreme body arises.
Truth and untruth, the twenty that are wholly pure,
Dharma and non-Dharma: delighting, a vessel of joy.
Doing and not doing: the vajra, distinct and one.
The vajra teaching thoroughly purifies and practices by means of ho.
Secret yoga, jewel, action performed, city of Dharma:
this wheel of magical display is the meaning of the unconquered,
adorned with the ornament of the vajra canopy.
By the great vajra, the lord of secrets,
the city whose nature is liberation was made.
For the sake of benefiting all sentient beings,
the five wheels truly arose:
that called the Tathāgata family,
that which makes indivisible and firm,
the supreme practice of the conduct of awakening,
and the supreme of the way of the Great Vehicle.
Like the maṇḍala of the wheel, suited to those to be trained,
they practice together, just as the principal deity practices.
To beings who are not to be trained, who are not supreme vessels,
the greatly powerful ones do not grant accomplishments.
There, of the very nature of vajra,
many good qualities truly arise.
For the sake of training sentient beings,
the vajra consorts truly arose.
That very one, together with the sons of the buddhas,
that very one, the great city of the wheel,
together with the offerings and the gatekeepers:
the secret maṇḍala truly arose.
The Protector, the Vajra Lord of Secrets,
is declared to be the Sovereign Lady of the Vajra Sphere (Vajradhātvīśvarī).
Through the union of Vajradharma
she is the supreme Vajra Perfection.
As it was before, so too afterwards;
likewise she abides firmly in the center.
This is the means of all the buddhas;
the secret union truly arose.
Just as the Vajra Sphere was described,
that very thing is called the secret.
In the Compendium of the Great Vehicle,
extensively, together with the mudrās,
the supreme maṇḍala of the five wheels
is truly explained in its extended form.
The king, the Lord of Secrets of the Sūtra Collection,
from the Compendium of the Great Vehicle,
the secret tantra and the mudrā tantra,
the maṇḍalas, the wheels and the moons
of the Tathāgata family:
these are explained as fivefold.
The supreme heart of the Vajra Sphere
is, in brief, explained as sixfold.
Endowed with the practice of the sublime Dharma,
next, the Tathāgata family,
according to ability and as is fitting,
the supreme maṇḍala as it is practiced,
according to the rite, as appropriate,
the supremely marvelous, I shall explain.
First, of the Tathāgata family,
I shall distinguish the sattvas.
I have explained the Lord of Secrets of the Sūtra Collection,
about which the childish are bewildered.
The vajra master of great fortune,
abiding in the secret maṇḍala,
should train with certainty in the secret,
the sixteen secret mudrās.
In the center of the moon disc,
with feet and hands joined in añjali,
one should visualize the vajra opened wide,
and make the Vajra Bearer enamored too.
This world of the trichiliocosm
one should gather completely into a single moon.
Me, the goddess, the Vajra Sovereign Lady,
one should hold fully in the center.
One should move with coquettish grace,
and beguile with one's countenance through magical display.
Through this union of meditation
the Vajra Bearer too is to be pleased.
Having sat in the cross-legged posture of the sattva,
one should join the palms at the crown of the head.
Having bound this mudrā,
one truly blesses oneself.
Through this mudrā of meditation
one binds the mudrā of the body.
Bending the forearm into a hook,
one should accomplish oneself completely.
Likewise, through the union of meditation,
one should draw the vajra arrow fully.
With the hand one says, "Excellent!"
and with the shoulders one arms oneself in armor.
Again, what is to be done in secret:
in the center of the moon disc
oneself abides as the single vajra,
the secret, the supreme sovereign of goddesses.
Likewise one should bend the forearm
and imagine a vajra on the crown of the head.
I sit to one side,
and one makes the armor of the two vajras.
All is the vajra body;
the jewel eyes are completely clear;
face and speech are the lotus;
and one should visualize Puṣpā, trembling all over.
Moving the eyes like a child,
one truly accomplishes the signs of a woman.
Moving the eyebrows, baring the teeth in a wrathful frown,
the eyes are opened wide with the wrathful gaze;
the gaze of the wrathful lady is of great passion;
with a smile one should close the eyes.
Through the method of opening the eyes,
one should stare wide with the vajra gaze.
Again, speech is of four kinds:
ho at the palate and the throat,
ja at the middle and the tip of the tongue,
oṃ at the throat and the lips,
vaṃ at the lips and the edges of the lips.
These are the secret, fully extended.
Expelling, reviling, cutting,
and binding are to be done with vajra words.
Likewise, in just the same way, in the center of the moon,
one should meditate on the very self of Vajrapāṇi,
and on jewel, Dharma, and likewise karma,
as the secret self.
In all the rites, in their extended form,
one guards the vajra secret thereafter.
Just as one abides truly in yoga,

so too one abides as the holder of the supreme secret,
the unsurpassed vajra yoga.
Next I shall explain it correctly.
Abiding truly in vajra yoga,
one should meditate on the subtle vajra.
The hero Vajrasattva,
freed from what is outside, abides truly.
Whether standing or else sitting,
one truly undertakes vajra yoga.
The pure way of the Great Vehicle,
the great conduct, the great way:
if one meditates thoroughly on oneself as the great Dharma,
one will attain the vajra accomplishment.
Out of the great suffering of formations,
one should radiate through the conduct of joy.
Having focused with great compassion,
one should not abandon sentient beings to mere indifference.
One should join the tongue to the palate,
and meditate at the tip of the nose.
Oneself as Vajradhara, the king,
abides likewise at the tip of the nose.
From the heart to the crown, as far as one is able,
one radiates the entire body.
Through touching the bliss of the subtle vajra,
the mind becomes settled in equipoise.
Before the vajra is the vajra;
one should meditate in the center of the vajra.
Through the union of meditating on the vajra,
one radiates the subtle vajra fully.
In front of one's own body
one should meditate on Vajrasattva.
In the center of Vajrasattva's heart
is the maṇḍala of all the vajras.
In the center of the vajra maṇḍala,
at its hub, is the Buddha, the Tathāgata.
One should meditate on the subtle vajra.
Whatever mind has meditated on the vajra,
formless and indemonstrable,
without delusion and without fear,
is here called the subtle vajra.
Because it is without entity and without substance,
one meditates on it thoroughly as without appearance.
In the absence of self and the absence of sentient beings,
the yogin meditates on yoga.
The body has the nature of the five substances;
it rests truly on the four elements,
on the twelve sense sources,
and further on the eighteen elements.
It is pervaded throughout by flesh, bone and blood,
by marrow and brain;
it is pervaded throughout by various substances,
by phlegm, bile and the rest, an aggregate;
it is pervaded by various kinds of worms.
As a source of foul smell and impurity,
one should examine one's own body individually.
With the three kinds of suffering as its origin,
by day and by night, constantly,
one meditates on mindfulness of the body.
One should examine one's own mind individually:
whatever has truly arisen there
has no going and no having gone;
in it there is no transference.
If in it there is no arising,
from what has the mind truly arisen?
This unreal false imagining
deludes the mind like a lie.
There is not even the mere absence of mind;
like a lie, arising appears.
In this there is no abode, no characteristic,
no stability and no arising.
Delight in grasping is great delusion.
It is to be declared the subtle vajra.
Through yoga of this kind,
in the manner of meditating on the mind,
body, mind and feelings
one should meditate on in the subtle mode.
sūkṣma vajra
The place of the sign of vajra touch
one should come to know correctly.
When it has arisen one should radiate it,
radiating it fully throughout the trichiliocosm.
spharaṇa vajra
Through touch one should withdraw the union,
and should not become frightened or bewildered.
One should also withdraw it into the three places,
and examine it individually with the intellect.
saṃharaṇa vajra
The very mind that focuses on the Dharma
should not be bewildered or destroyed.
One should make that vajra firm,
and should touch the subtle vajra.
vajra dṛḍha tiṣṭha
Fearless Vajrasattva,
the king with the crest of Brahmā:
one should meditate on the sattva at the crown of the head,
and radiate the entire body fully.
vajra ātmaka
At the forehead, Vajraratna
one should meditate on in equipoise.
One should radiate the signs,
and radiate all the sattvas too.
vajra ratna ātmaka
Vajradharma, utterly pure,
one should meditate on at the tip of the nose.
One should radiate his signs,
and radiate all bodies too.
vajra padma ātmaka
Pervading the whole body completely,
one meditates fully on the sphere of reality.
One should radiate its signs,
and radiate all the sattvas too.
sarva vajra ātmaka
The sattva, likewise the jewel, the karma,
and the Dharma one should meditate on at the crown of the head.
One should radiate their signs,
and radiate the entire body fully.
sarva vajra
A hook abides truly at the nose:
so the intelligent person should imagine.
One should draw in all the buddhas
and make one's own body firm.
At the heart, the place of passion,
one should imagine passion completely purified.
One should please all the buddhas,
and the sphere of reality will become firm.
One should take up the gesture called "Excellent!",
supreme as abiding truly at the crown.
One should please all the buddhas
and radiate the sphere of reality fully.
hṛda vajra aṃkuśa tiṣṭha vajra rāga hṛdaye ādi vajra duṣṭi
At the heart, a vajra sun
the intelligent person should meditate on.
One should radiate the splendor of its light,
and radiate all the worlds too.
At the crown, a creeper together with light
one should imagine at the top of a banner.
One should radiate showers of jewels,
and truly radiate clouds of vessels.
Having imagined bliss through laughter,
one should definitely purify intense suffering.
One should radiate a shower of Dharma too,
and radiate clouds of light as well.
hṛdaya vajra sūrya tiṣṭa vajra dhājāgre hṛdaya vajra hāsa
Beginning with recollection at the crown,
one should imagine the sword as principal.
One should cut the suffering of saṃsāra,
and radiate all the sattvas.
In the place of the wheel at the heart
one should meditate on what accords with the Dharma.
One should radiate it throughout all of space,
and radiate the sphere of reality fully.
The sound that arose from its family
the wise one imagines on the tip of the tongue.
One should radiate the words of the Dharma
and radiate them fully throughout all directions.
hṛda vajra ghoṣa tiṣṭha vajra hṛdayaṃ phra veśa vajra vāktra
Beginning with recollection at the crown,
one imagines the act of offering.
For the sake of offering one radiates vajras,
radiating them throughout all directions.
At the heart, the vajra armor
one imagines in deep equipoise.
That which frees from all suffering
one should radiate in the form of armor.
From the crown of the head to the soles of the feet
one should imagine being bound with vajras.
One should draw in the vajra essence
and radiate all that has the nature of vajra.
hṛda vajra śavace tiṣṭha vajra yakṣa vajra vajra muṣṭi hṛdayaṃ
Toward friends and non-friends without exception,
toward all bhūtas, loving-kindness
one should radiate, pervading equally,
without exception, leaving none out.
Alas, these sentient beings suffer,
wandering everywhere among the five destinies.

Through thorough application by day and by night,
one should radiate compassion itself.
Day and night, virtuous and free of aversion,
whatever sentient beings strive after virtue,
rejoicing in them, great bliss
one radiates fully to all sentient beings.
Having entered the path that is hard to cross,
abiding in the awakening of the buddhas,
in order to accomplish the welfare of all sentient beings,
the one endowed with great intelligence rests in equanimity.
mahā maitri sāraṇa mahā kāruṇi kāya spharaṇa sarva buddha pramoda spharaṇa mahopekṣa spharaṇa
In the sky, or else in the other directions,
one should imagine the form of the vajra.
Through focusing on sentient beings equally,
one radiates it fully throughout all of space.
vajra muṣṭi bhava
Vajrasattva, the great king,
at the heart, the vajra, firm in the nature of reality:
one should meditate on all things,
and radiate the sphere of reality fully.
Beginning with recollection of the Buddha,
in every aspect, in all its variety,
one meditates on that as one's own self,
and radiates the sphere of reality fully.
Endowed with the other subtle vajra,
meditating on all phenomena,
one swiftly radiates them fully in all directions,
and should meditate on the vajra at the heart.
mahā bodhisatva viśva buddha anusmṛti viśva vajra pāṇi rśaya sarūpaṃ
In the center of the moon disc
one should meditate on the mind of awakening.
samanta bhadra viśva
Abiding in the center of the moon disc,
one meditates on oneself as the vajra.
vajra satva
Abiding in the center of the moon disc,
with the vajra pride, and with play,
vajra satva samaya samādhi jñāna piśva
abiding in the center of the moon disc,
one's own self in the form of the Buddha.
tathāgata samohaṃ
The union of the two faculties
is to be performed by vajra masters.
If one desires the supreme accomplishment,
the wise one should certainly do it.
With a mind in meditative equipoise,
one should contemplate recollection of the Buddha.
Adorned with the marks, just as he is,
one should always meditate on oneself.
As the Buddha is, so am I:
one should always meditate on oneself as the Buddha.
Whoever is the Buddha, that is oneself;
one abides truly as many buddhas.
I am equal to the Tathāgata:
so one truly speaks the vajra words.
Through the union of the two faculties
one should please all the buddhas too.
oṃ sarva tathāgata samohaṃ
Vajrasattva, the great king:
just as his bodily color is, according to the rite,
the two faculties abide truly;
likewise I should always meditate.
oṃ vajra satva samohaṃ
I am equal to Vajrasattva:
so one truly speaks the vajra words.
Through the union of the two faculties
one will certainly conquer the world.
Whatever is the essence of the elements
is free of dust and stainless.
I should always be meditated upon
as the vajra nature of reality.
vajra dharma samohaṃ
I myself am equal to Vajradharma:
so one truly speaks the vajra words.
Through the union of the two faculties
one will attain the vajra Dharma.
Vajrakarma is oneself,
without distinction, with a single sign.
Worshiping all the buddhas thoroughly,
one should always meditate on oneself.
oṃ vajra karma samohaṃ
I am equal to Vajrakarma:
so one speaks the vajra words again and again.
Through the union of the two faculties
one becomes equal to Vajrakarma.
I am Vajraratna himself,
the empowerment of all the buddhas.
One should always meditate on oneself,
and one becomes equal to Vajraratna.
oṃ vajra ratna samohūṃ
I am equal to Vajraratna:
so one speaks the vajra words again and again.
Through the union of Vajraratna
one will attain the empowerment of the buddhas.

The connection of the two forms is called the two faculties. When whatever is one's own body and whatever is the body of the Buddha have become mingled as one, that is called the union of the two faculties.

This world of the trichiliocosm
one imagines as a single vajra,
and in the center, equal to the form of the Buddha,
one should meditate on oneself.
One meditates on the vajra in the sky;
in a single vajra, fully clear,
through the concentration of Vajrasattva,
one should meditate on oneself in the center.
This world of the trichiliocosm
being fully clear as a single vajra,
through the concentration of Vajraratna,
one should meditate on oneself in the center.
As far as one is able, one blesses it;
one meditates on the single vajra.
Through the concentration of Vajradharma
I should always be meditated upon.
One should meditate on the form of the Buddha,
and meditate on the jewel self in the center.
Everywhere, in the center of the vajra,
one always meditates on oneself as karma.
Whichever is the principal deity, together with the principals,
just as their bodily color and attire are,
one should always meditate on oneself;
then one attains that very sublime being.
Speaking the vajra words of oneself as the principal,
one should imagine oneself as the vajra mind.
Through the union of the two faculties
one will attain Vajradhara.
Further, the method of meditation
I shall explain thoroughly in its own right.
Just as it arises from the mind,
one meditates on vajra yoga.
Relying on the tip of the nose,
the stream of mind abides truly.
For a hundred and seven more,
one should meditate through the vajra union.
If one desires accomplishment through meditation,
one should accomplish the vajra meditation.
Through the union of recollecting the Buddha
the yoga of accomplishment abides truly.
