Through the union of recollecting the sattva
one should ascertain it without doubt.
Whatever one desires,
one should meditate on oneself as the vajra.
Just as is the yoga of bodily color,
as is fitting, oneself in various forms;
or else, the vajra meditation being principal,
one should also meditate on the accomplishment.
How wonderful! Marvelous! The wheel of magical display has arisen!
How wonderful! Marvelous! The supreme sublime conduct is accomplished!
Oh, marvelous! The state of the supreme secret has been attained!
I am supreme in meditating on vajra yoga.
This is the accomplishment of those skilled in yoga,
which accomplishes the knowledge of yoga.
Through the mudrā that accomplishes offering,
I shall next thoroughly explain offering.
Four sessions and four colors:
through thorough application at the four times,
proclaiming the yoga of the buddhas,
one should make offerings to the buddhas.
Radiating fully throughout all the directions,
radiating the offerings fully,
through thoroughly accomplishing the awakening of the buddhas,
one always truly meditates on yoga.
One truly applies radiation to the offering,
and so the Dharma is radiated fully.

Abiding in the union of concentration,
one should thoroughly perform the offering of the Dharma.
oṃ sarva tathāgata vajra satva anuttara pūjā spharaṇa samaye hūṃ
This secret mantra of the vajra
one should recite again and again.
Through the union of the Vajra Sphere
one should thoroughly perform the five mudrās:
individual realization and full realization,
the vajra, and likewise the vajra self,
as explained before in extended form;
one thoroughly generates the Vajra Sphere.
Having meditated on all the magical displays of the buddhas
without exception as one's own self,
through the union of the Vajra Sphere
one offers them to the Lord of Sages.
Through the concentration of Vajrasattva,
having made offerings to the Sage,
I am the Protector Vairocana;
I have radiated the elements.
Having radiated the mind of awakening,
one offers it to the Lord of Sages.
Through this vajra secret mantra
one thoroughly accomplishes all the offerings.
oṃ sarva tathāgata vajra satva anuttara pūjā spharaṇa samaye hūṃ
Having radiated Vajraratna,
the empowerment of all the buddhas,
I am the Protector Vairocana;
I have radiated the elements.
It will be accomplished through the vajra mantra.
One offers to the Lord of Sages,
radiating the jewel offerings thoroughly,
and thoroughly accomplishes the union of binding.
oṃ sarva tathāgata pajra ratna anuttara pūjā spharaṇa samaye hūṃ
Just as before,
through the concentration of Vairocana,
just as the sattva was brought forth,
so one should meditate on oneself.
Having meditated on one's own self,
radiating the great marvel fully,
one should offer it to all the buddhas;
it will be accomplished through the vajra union.
Having entered into absorption in this way,
Vajradharma thoroughly arises.
One should radiate it throughout all the directions,
and offer it to the buddhas.
oṃ sarva tathāgata vajra dharma anuttara pūjā spharaṇa samaye hūṃ
Abiding in the union of concentration,
having radiated the vajra actions,
one should offer them to all the buddhas,
and radiate them everywhere.
oṃ sarva tathāgata vajra karma anuttara pūjā spharaṇa samaye hūṃ
With this king of offerings
one should meditate in the four sessions.
Through the rite of offering one will be accomplished;
even the sinful will be accomplished.
The act of offering is unsurpassed;
I shall explain it thoroughly as is fitting.
Endowed with the king of Dharma mudrās,
one will be accomplished; of this there is no doubt.
oṃ sarva tathāgata sarva ātma niryātana pūjā sāraṇa karma vajri a
One should meditate on one's own self as Vajrasattva
in meditative equipoise,
and again and again
offer oneself to all the buddhas.
oṃ sarva tathāgata sarva ātma niryātana pūjā spharaṇa karmāgre ja
One's own self as the Vajra Hook
one should radiate in all directions,
summon all the buddhas,
and offer to the Sages.
oṃ sarva tathāgata sarva ātma niryātana anurāga pūjā spharaṇa karma vāṇi hūṃ ho
As the passionate love of the vajra family
one should always meditate on oneself.
Having pleased all phenomena,
having spoken this, one should radiate.
oṃ sarva tathāgata sarva ātma niryātana sādhukāra pūjā spharaṇa karma tuṣṭi maṃ
Vajraratna, the great yoga:
one should meditate fully on oneself,
and radiate the unsurpassed jewel offering
throughout all the directions.
oṃ namaḥ sarva tathāgata kāya abhiṣeka rahnebhyo vajra maṇi oṃ
The great king of all the buddhas,
empowered at the seat of awakening:
having contemplated the mind in this way too,
one will attain Vajraratna himself.
Oneself as Vajratejas,
illuminating all the directions,
should urge on all the buddhas,
and radiate the splendor of offering fully.
oṃ sarva tathāgata sūryebhyo vajra tejoni jvāla hrīḥ
Vajraketu, seen everywhere:
the wise one raises the banner.
One should make offerings to all the buddhas
and urge them on with the vajra mantra.
oṃ namaḥ sarva tathāgata āśā paripūraṇi cintāmaṇi dhvaja agre trāṃ
The maṇḍala of all the buddhas of great renown
the wise one beholds.
One should always delight oneself,
and urge them on thoroughly with the vajra mantra.
oṃ namaḥ sarva tathāgata mahā prīti bramodyakrebhyo vajra hāse hā
All sentient beings are the great Dharma;
I am the great Dharma, pure.
One should urge on all the buddhas thoroughly.
All the buddhas are all of oneself.
One should lay out all the maṇḍala cities;
the wise one contemplates oneself,
and urges them on thoroughly with the vajra mantra.
oṃ sarva tathāgata vajra dharma samādhibhi stunomi mahā dharma agre hrīḥ
I am the sharp sword of wisdom,
the way of the Perfection of Wisdom.
I thoroughly turn the syllables of the wheel;
I am the one who holds all the sūtras.
oṃ sarva tathāgata prajñā pāramitā nirahare tunomi mahā ghoṣanuge dhvaṃ
The speech of all sentient beings:
I am the sattva, Vajrabhāṣa, the steadfast,
Vajrabhāṣa, lord of secrets.
One should make offerings to all the buddhas thoroughly.
oṃ sarva tathāgata sandhi bhāṣa buddha saṃgīti bhirgaya stunomi vajra vāce rbaṃ
I turn the wheel of Dharma,
the one who bestows the maṇḍala of the wheel.
I fully dedicate the vajra wheel;
one should radiate all the offerings fully.
oṃ sarva tathāgata cakra akṣara parivartīdhana sarva mutrāṃ tenaya stunomi sarva manḍale hūṃ
I am Vajrakarma, the king,
making offerings thoroughly in all directions:
flowers, incense and other fragrances.
One should urge them on with the vajra mantra.
oṃ sarva tathāgata dhupa megha spharaṇa pūjā karma kara kara
Endowed with the apportioned rite,
the one who presents the offering of flowers,
oneself, one's very self, is the king.
One should urge them on thoroughly with the vajra mantra.
oṃ sarva tathāgata puṣpa prasara spharaṇa pūjā karme kiri kiri
I am Vajrayakṣa,
devourer of afflictions, the blazing lamp of Dharma.
One should radiate throughout all the directions,
and truly radiate blazing light.
oṃ sarva tathāgata ālokajval spharaṇa pūjā karme bhara bhara
Vajramuṣṭi himself, truly,
truly endowed with a vessel of fragrance,
one should radiate throughout all the directions,
making offerings thoroughly with every kind of shower.
oṃ sarva tathāgata gandha samudra spharaṇa pūjākarma kuru kuru
Truly endowed with the welfare of all sentient beings,
having generated the mind of awakening,
one should offer it to all the buddhas,
thoroughly accomplishing the supreme offering.

oṃ sarva tathāgata vajra ātma niryātana pūjā megha samudra spharaṇa samaye hūṃ
The wise one should draw the maṇḍala,
and bow down thoroughly with all the limbs.
One should offer to all the buddhas,
and radiate throughout all the directions.
oṃ sarva tathāgata kāya niryātana pūjā megha samudra spharaṇa samaye hūṃ
Through the gathering of Vajrasattva,
Vajraratna, the unsurpassed;
through singing Vajradharma as a song,
one comes to perform the vajra actions.
With this song of the vajra
one should delight one's own self.
One should offer to all the buddhas,
and thoroughly recite the vajra mantra too.
oṃ sarva tathāgata citta ninyātana pūjā meghasamudra spharaṇa samaye hūṃ
All the offerings in all the directions
the learned one should imagine.
One should offer them to all the buddhas,
and thoroughly urge them on with the vajra mantra.
oṃ sarva tathāgata citta ninyātana pūjā megha samudra spharaṇa samaye hūṃ
Having offered, the wise one
contemplates the concentration of the vajra consort.
Endowed with supreme substances, one should radiate them,
and offer them fully to all the buddhas.
oṃ sarva tathāgata anuttara harṣa rati prīti sukha anuttara prajñā pūjā megha samudra spharaṇa samaye hūṃ
Through this application of the rite
one should make offerings thoroughly to all the buddhas.
The vajra master of great austerity
will attain the unsurpassed accomplishment.
All the maṇḍalas gathered together:
the wise one contemplates the maṇḍala.
With unsurpassed masses of offerings
one should make offerings to all the buddhas.
Through this vajra union
one obtains long life, good complexion, freedom from illness,
and every perfect happiness;
it thoroughly destroys all evil.
Whatever vajra yogin
makes offerings day after day,
without doubt that vajra yogin
will attain every accomplishment.
With the vajra mantra one should train in the precepts,
and contemplate all the mudrās.
In the Compendium of the Great Vehicle
the mudrās are explained at great length.
Following the sequence of the vajra actions,
the wise one trains in the maṇḍala.
Fully equal to the Vajra Sphere,
it is explained once more to the yogin.
The Protector of the World, of great miraculous power,
is always to be worshiped in that way.
O yogins of the supreme secret,
I shall explain still more beyond that.
When one has first generated the mind,
the unsurpassed vajra mind,
having firmly generated the mind of awakening,
one should train in the activity of offering.
In the union performed by the vajra master
one should contemplate vajra joy.
Having generated the joy of passion,
one makes the offering of joy thoroughly.
One should meditate on oneself as the Buddha,
and having meditated, perform the offering thoroughly.
For the sake of the bliss of great joy,
through thorough union with the knowledge goddess:
buddha ātmakohaṃ
Having thoroughly generated the mind of awakening,
one should think, "I myself am the Buddha."
With the powers, the fearlessnesses and the rest
one should empower one's own self.
buddha abhiṣiñcāmi
Having thoroughly generated the mind of awakening,
one should think, "I am the Buddha."
Singing the song of the vajra,
one should worship oneself directly.
buddha siddhi karomi
Having firmly generated the mind of awakening,
one should think, "I myself am the Buddha."
Dancing with the vajra dance,
the learned one should worship oneself.
buddha pājā
Body, speech and mind, the supreme vajra:
through union one likewise pays homage,
and having worshiped all the buddhas,
one will certainly have paid homage.
praṇamāmi
Arisen from vajra body, speech and mind,
all the great merit of the buddhas:
by my supreme rejoicing in it,
I will swiftly attain buddhahood itself.
anumode
Offering oneself and the rest:
with all the offerings of vajra body, speech and mind,
the very Buddha is worshiped
by one whose nature is offering.
buddha pūjā
Arisen from vajra body, speech and mind,
all the accumulations of virtue, every one:
through the offering of full dedication
one becomes equal to all the buddhas.
bariṇāmaḥ
The sphere of reality is the great essence,
unsoiled by the mire of desire,
unborn because it is pure from the very beginning,
like a lotus in the ocean of saṃsāra.
sarva śuddha
All phenomena are unborn,
peaceful from the very beginning, without defilement,
not defiled by adventitious stains,
purified by the letter a.
samanta bhadra
How wonderful! The wheel whose nature is bliss,
the unsurpassed wheel of Dharma!
Of all phenomena themselves,
here the Tathāgata is the cause.
dharma cakra
A sound like an echo
does not exist, nor is there anything else;
in the nature of all phenomena
there is nothing whatever called a self.
nisprapañca
First the intelligent one should contemplate,
and meditate in equipoise;
after that one recites the various vajras,
and then one will attain accomplishment.
Of vajra body, speech and mind,
one's own body, the subtlest particles,
one should meditate on in vajra form,
and meditate on oneself as the vajra.
vajra kāya
Of vajra body, speech and mind,
however many aspects of the limbs there are,
one should meditate on the form of the Buddha,
and radiate them all fully.
dharma kāya
However many subtlest particles
abide in one's own body,
one should meditate on them as Vajrasattva,
and worship thoroughly with unfailing offerings.
satva kāya
Likewise, of all bodies,
their own subtlest particles
one meditates on as arrayed everywhere;
that is declared the offering to the buddhas.
buddha kāya
All the buddhas rejoice.
Whoever is a bodhisattva in training,
the wise one, trains in the supreme secret
and will attain the ground of a buddha.
Vajrasattva, the great king,
binds all bodies fully.
Through passion for all the limbs
together with concentration,
one is fully enamored with every body.
The self-arisen offering of bliss:
how wonderful, I am the offering of passionate love!
One should speak the vajra words:
How wonderful, delightful! How wonderful, lovely!
How wonderful, joy, thoroughly beautiful!
Attachment to the Dharma is great passion,
that in which one delights thoroughly and becomes attached.
The selflessness of phenomena and of persons,
emptiness, should be declared passion.
One should abandon the suffering of saṃsāra,
yet should not abandon sentient beings.
Craving, attachment and enjoyment,
all conceptual thought, one abandons completely.
One should know name and form,
and realize the three characteristics.
What is called the bliss of the Great Vehicle
is thoroughly declared to be passionate attachment;
not grasping at any views,

one completely abandons the suffering of existence.
There is neither thought nor conceptual thought;
all suffering is decisively cut off.
In the aggregates, the sense sources and the elements
there is not so much as an atom of thought.
In that there is nothing conditioned,
nor is there even the unconditioned.
There is neither male nor female,
no conceptual thought of a self or of a sentient being,
no desire, no aversion, no delusion;
afflictions and secondary afflictions are abandoned.
Through attachment of this kind,
in which the Vajra Bearer becomes attached,
through union of this kind,
one should be passionately devoted to Vajrasattva.
The kiss of the mouth, supreme and pure,
should be thoroughly accomplished through secret union.
oṃ mahāsukha vajrasatva
The rite of passionate devotion has been taught.
One should clasp the upper arms tightly,
and through what is taught as passionate attachment
Vajrasattva will be fully accomplished.
Being freed from the five attachments,
one should not be attached to sense pleasures;
through the joy of the rite, the intelligent one
will accomplish all accomplishments.
Through the rite of Vajrasattva
all the secrets should be fully revealed.
All the peaceful ones should be pleased,
and Vajrasattva and the others should be pleased.
Buddhas or bodhisattvas,
the Vajra Bearer and great joy,
are accomplished through great passion such as this;
of this there is no doubt.
oṃ sarva rāg rati sukha yāmi hūṃ
Supremely secret, holy and supreme:
having done this, all the secrets,
once examined, come to be accomplished.
The wise one especially should train in it.
Having oneself accomplished the sign as firm,
one should abide in the center of the lotus;
having blessed the sign, the stūpa,
one's own self becomes equal to the vajra.
From the King of Sūtras, the Lord of Secrets,
he spoke words that are exceedingly secret.
They were spoken with a hidden intention,
and should be understood as intentional.
"Sign" is said to mean the hand emblem;
"firm" means preeminent.
Whatever the hand emblem of the bodhisattva,
the learned one should hold it preeminently.
Practicing the six perfections
is proclaimed to be its sign.
Because it should be taught as preeminent,
the intelligent wish the family to arise.
Those who have themselves become firm in the sign
are proclaimed to be exactly these.
Practicing the six perfections
should be declared the vajra family.
Whatever is the mind of awakening, that is the vajra;
this should be declared the vajra sign.
Through thorough application of the mind of awakening
the sign should be declared firm.
The lotus is the Perfection of Wisdom;
"one should abide in the center of the lotus" means
that in the yoga of the Perfection of Wisdom
one should enter absorption again and again.
The union of bhaga and liṅga,
when relied upon again and again,
free of wavering and of fault,
truly arises without doubt.
Wisdom and the mind of awakening,
when relied upon again and again,
in the way of the Perfection of Wisdom,
awakening comes without doubt.
This Dharma that is to be practiced
is hard for any embodied being to find.
With the mind of awakening, faith and great wisdom,
one should be devoted to the stūpa, the sign.
By the yogin's meditation on this,
by practice of this kind,
and by this secret conduct,
the vajra secret itself is attained.
As is taught at length in the sūtra
that compiles the secret by its intention,
through this secret yoga
one should meditate on all sentient beings.
Through this yoga of practice
of the Vajra Compendium of the Lord of Secrets,
if one practices the great secret,
one is accomplished in that very instant.
Some, on that very day,
are accomplished again and again;
in two, three, four or five days,
or in six or seven, without doubt.
Even one who commits the five acts of immediate retribution,
by the rite as is fitting,
applying himself thoroughly for one year,
will be accomplished; of this there is no doubt.

The Rite of the Vajra Secret is complete.

The supreme maṇḍala of the secret
and the rite of the vajra master,
as is fitting and appropriate,
I shall now correctly explain.
In brief, the observance of the vidyā,
as explained above in the maṇḍala,
as far as one is inclined and as far as one is able,
should first be explained by the learned one.
Vajrasattva, of great fortune,
the king, light pervading everywhere:
without doubt, a hundred thousand times,
one should constantly recite the vajra words.
One should cultivate the being vajra;
having cultivated vajra yoga,
one should cultivate the purity of phenomena.
Through mere cultivation one will be accomplished.
Thoroughly abandoning afflictions, one should turn afflictions back;
through cultivating yoga one accomplishes yoga.
Through mere mantra recitation great joy is accomplished,
and through the rite of yoga the practice is accomplished.
Or else, for mere recitation,
one should recite with the supreme recitation.
In order to accomplish the yoga of vidyā,
one should recite according to the rite of accomplishment.
Through that rite of yoga
buddhahood is attained in an instant.
Without the rite of yoga
there is no accomplishment; of this there is no doubt.
The four secrets of all the vidyādharas
are correctly explained.
The great, immeasurable secret
is the compendium of all four.
The rites of recitation and fire offering,
devotion to binding mudrās,
the rite of the supreme vidyā:
this is the first compendium of secrets.
Killing and desiccating, striking with the ritual dagger,
beating, and fierce rites,
and this yantra for expelling obstructors:
this is the second compendium of secrets.
Binding on the unchanging armor,
conduct and likewise setting in motion,
the very protection of oneself,
dispelling the obstructors,
seizing substances and destroying,
guarding against all the malicious:
this is the third compendium of secrets
of all the vidyādharas.
Worship with mantra and mudrā,
likewise the accomplishment of vidyā itself,
the obstructors, and the gathering of merit,
are held to be the fourth secret.
All the four secrets,
comprising every nature and miraculous power,
the great tantra of the supreme secret,
are fully explained in that tantra.
The rite of recitation and fire offering
shall be explained from the supreme tantra,
because all vidyādharas
should perform recitation and fire offering.
Without the practice of recitation
the accomplishment of vidyā will not arise;
without so much as a fire offering
the work of vidyā will not be done.
That by which one becomes diligent in yoga,
the stages of recitation, I shall explain.
Of all the vidyādharas,
this essence is the supreme secret.
In this secret family, recitation
has arisen as twenty yogas.
The twenty kinds of recitation
are twenty-one in the secret tantra.
One should realize the habitual tendencies of negative states;
thought is called a habitual tendency.

Through mere recitation it dissolves;
one should speak through mere recitation.
It abandons the wrongs one has committed,
and likewise purifies existence,
and various wonders will be seen;
therefore one should recite.
The conduct of awakening will arise,
and the supreme way will be seen;
therefore the rite of recitation
must certainly be done by the yogin.
In this tantra of the great secret
hūṃ is proclaimed as first.
All the secrets arise from it;
one should recite with hūṃ.
The chief of all the buddhas
is likewise the supreme syllable hūṃ.
All the secrets, together with the wheel,
and together with the five families, arise.
Here, how the syllable hūṃ arises
the wise one should first examine.
The tongue and all the rest as hollow,
one should examine the whole body.
It does not arise from the ten points of articulation;
not in any way from the teeth,
nor from the tongue and its root,
not in any way from the palate.
From what place does articulation arise?
It does not arise from the heart itself.
In all hollow things
arising itself does not appear.
Through the application of the syllable hūṃ
one joins it properly to one's own expanse;
that itself does not become afflicted.
The ground of all, which holds all seeds:
from the effort of the palate and the throat
and the assemblage of the tongue, sound arises.
The sound of the syllable hūṃ that has arisen
arises as nonconceptual.
From an articulation that arises in this way
the abode does not become afflicted.
Sound just as it is, together with words,
and all conditioned things, should be cast aside.
Just as with the application of hūṃ,
all phenomena are unborn.
What arises from conceptual thought
arises as nonconceptual.
The beginning of saṃsāra
cannot be known in any way.
Here arising does not appear,
and conditioned things do not perish.
Therefore through the mere syllable hūṃ
one should examine conditioned things one by one.
The pure and ultimate abode
should also be joined with hūṃ.
This sound without intrinsic nature
arises from the syllable hūṃ.
In it there is no entity;
it is without intrinsic nature and selfless.
Likewise, of all phenomena
there is no intrinsic nature.
There is no self, sentient being or nourisher;
grasped and grasper are abandoned.
Undefiled and unconditioned,
the pure abode should be made firm.
Through the wisdom of the union of accomplishment
the awakening of a buddha will be attained.
Through application of this kind
the syllable hūṃ arises,
pure wisdom arises,
and it should be made firm through the dharmadhātu.
Even the great miraculous power of the buddhas
is not apprehended here,
because the essence of those gone in the three times,
the buddha essence, is taught as an assembly.
All the buddhas together with their heirs,
and the Vajra Bearer with the Victor's heirs,
through the application of mere hūṃ
should all be properly summoned.
Through the application of a single syllable
the essence of all the buddhas is made firm,
because Vajradhara, the king,
abides in this through his own union.
Without realizing the mind of awakening,
the habitual tendencies that accord with the Dharma
bring no emergence whatsoever
from beginningless saṃsāra.
Of the śrāvakas, the pratyekabuddhas
and the emanations of the bodhisattvas,
without the mind of awakening
not one can pass into nirvāṇa.
If buddhas did not appear,
from where would the śrāvakas come?
If there were no appearing of buddhas,
the pratyekabuddhas would not arise.
If there were no bodhisattvas,
from what would the buddhas arise?
If there were no mind of awakening...
Therefore this entire wheel
arises from the mind of awakening.
The mind of awakening, the great secret,
arises from the syllable hūṃ.
The arising of all the buddhas
becomes the appearance of the syllable hūṃ.
Because those with form, endowed with the marks,
the buddhas, constantly arise,
the application of the syllable hūṃ is hūṃ;
that itself arises as the vajra.
The vajra fully joined with the dharmadhātu
arises from that.
From that comes the great vajra yoga,
the unsurpassed lord of yoga.
Without doubt, all yogas
are accomplished through the application of the syllable hūṃ.
Through recitation of this kind,
just as the Blessed One
made dedication in the past,
one dedicates the virtue produced by yoga.
Through the power of the bodhisattvas
one should dedicate even one's own mind.
The great vajra brings to completion;
one should deliver beings through the mind of awakening.
The great essence of the dharmadhātu:
all sentient beings are unsullied.
All are the essence of the Tathāgata,
unsullied by the mire of afflictions.
Pure just like the moon,
luminous as the full moon,
always abiding perfectly complete,
utter clarity will be accomplished.
This is the primordial wisdom of the Dharma,
the essence of the Tathāgata (tathāgatagarbha).
It always abides in purity,
yet is obscured by one's own afflictions.
Therefore the aspect of one's own mind
should be known as a full moon.
There is no self at all;
this nature of reality is known by the Victor.
Wisdom is not a full moon,
because there is no self;
it is fitting to liken it to the full moon
because it is pure and unsullied.
The flawless wisdom of the buddhas
should be meditated upon through the application of the moon.
Having seen it, the wise one should reflect:
"By whom is this realized?"
The Tathāgata, the Blessed One,
who suffered for a long time,
one should think of as likewise purifying
all the habitual tendencies of the afflictions.
The mind of awakening is the great mind,
destroyer of all afflictions,
which has arisen as the antidote
from the habitual tendencies that accord with the Dharma.
Therefore, through its power,
one engages in the wisdom of the buddhas,
unsullied by negative states with their habitual tendencies
or by the mire of desire.
The great mind that is like the sun
burns up the roots of the afflictions.
The great mind that is like the moon
makes the medicinal herbs of virtue flourish.
Like the moon in the heat of summer:
the torment of the sun scorches,
but the moon, by rising, brings relief;
it pacifies all torment.
Untormented by the torment of afflictions,
to ignorance it is the very sun;
the mind of awakening is the great moon
in pacifying the torment of afflictions.
It is supreme in destroying thorough affliction
with its habitual tendencies, and negative states.
As a magnet draws iron,
it draws out the habitual tendencies of the afflictions.
The yogin whose nature is vajra
should meditate on that mind of awakening.
In that place, the moon disc,
one should meditate on the moon once again.
Through the power of the mind of awakening,

the wisdom that burns up all habitual tendencies
will arise there.
Grasped and grasper abandoned,
nonconceptual and incomparable,
selflessness and likewise emptiness
destroy the mountain of ignorance.
Like a vajra, without hollowness,
of vajra nature, of great essence,
the firm mountain of the view of a perishable aggregate
is destroyed by that vajra;
therefore it is taught as having the nature of a vajra.
Therefore the vajra yogin,
in the center of the moon, an equal moon;
between the two moons, with a vajra,
should make firm the vajra wisdom.
Emptiness, suchness, reality,
the limit of reality and the unconditioned,
unborn and unceasing:
the syllable hūṃ is taught as their meditation.
The dharmadhātu, supremely firm,
equal to the expanse of space,
should be meditated upon in the center of the moon.
Through that application of hūṃ
one should meditate on the supreme vajra.
One should radiate it into the whole body,
radiate it as equal to emptiness.
This very form is a mere name;
there is no entity whatsoever.
If it is analyzed into its parts,
there is no such thing as form.
Even when they are examined down to the subtlest particles,
there is no such thing as a subtlest particle.
It is called the conventional truth of the world;
in the ultimate sense it does not exist.
In a nature of this kind
there is no such thing as form.
If that form does not exist,
how can it be said to exist?
If that form does not exist,
there is no name for it either.
From what does a name arise?
Whatever a name may be like,
what is called an entity is the same.
When it is examined one by one through that name,
in what place does it abide?
When a single entity is analyzed,
many names arise;
through the division of each one,
the earlier name is lost.
Where there is no name for it,
no name arises.
For that which has no name,
how can it be spoken of by a name?
That in which the name abides
is not apprehended in any way.
Form is not apprehended;
how then does form exist in it?
This is the true nonconceptual,
entwined all around by habitual tendencies,
where the childish are utterly deluded
and do not attain nirvāṇa.
Just as one meditates on form,
so with feeling and perception:
when each is analyzed separately,
no core is found in it.
Where there is no core,
how can there be an entity?
Where no entity is apprehended,
how can there be ultimate truth?
What is not apprehended when taught
does not exist; it is not an entity.
Since all phenomena do not exist,
they should be declared unborn.
What is unborn does not exist;
what does not exist, how can it exist?
The learned declare
all phenomena to be unceasing.
Unborn and unceasing,
they are held to be at peace from the very beginning;
therefore they are by nature in nirvāṇa.
This is declared to be emptiness.
This is the great true reality;
it should be declared the limit of reality.
The dharmadhātu, of great essence,
should be declared the Great Vehicle.
It is an essence without hollowness,
the unsurpassed teaching of the vajra.
One should radiate it into the whole body:
my own body is like this,
that which is called the vajra self.
One radiates the syllable hūṃ throughout.
Through this application of meditation
buddhahood itself will be accomplished.
Such a phenomenon is unborn;
here too no arising appears.
Through the application called hūṃ
it does not appear as born either.
The nature of reality, pure, true,
the limit of reality, undefiled,
the dharmadhātu, firm and holy,
is here proclaimed as the syllable hūṃ.
That vajra syllable hūṃ itself
is the teaching of the dense dharmadhātu.
Whatever is the mind of awakening, that is the vajra;
whatever is the vajra, that itself is hūṃ.
The nature of reality, pure like this,
the delightful vajra wisdom,
the great essence of all the buddhas,
abides in my hand as the vajra.
Therefore all the secret yogas
of all the Vajra Bearers,
through application to the syllable hūṃ,
one should contemplate as the yoga free of elaboration.
The holy moon disc
should be meditated upon in the center of the heart.
On that moon, two moons;
in their center one should meditate well on a moon.
In the center of the moon, a vajra
the learned one should meditate upon.
In the center of the vajra, the syllable hūṃ:
one should meditate well on the awakening of a buddha.
Through application of this kind
one should meditate fully on the whole body.
Through the recitation of Vajrahūṃkāra
one swiftly attains the vajra self.

The Meditation of hūṃ, Vajrahūṃkāra, is complete.

Just as with the application of hūṃ,
one should meditate on all the syllables.
Through the application of the great being,
the sixteen syllables are pure.
hūṃ is the syllable of all the buddhas,
because the buddha family arose from it.
hūṃ of all the Vajra Bearers:
one should meditate fully on it as cutting off all that is fierce.
oṃ is properly endowed with the jewel;
hrī is properly endowed with the lotus;
the syllable a is said to be the various;
one should meditate on them in this way too.
hūṃ is the buddha vajra;
trāṃ is the vajra essence;
the syllable hrīḥ is taught as the vajra;
the various vajra is the syllable a.
hūṃ is Vajrasattva;
Vajrarāja is the syllable ja;
ho is Vajrarāga;
Vajrasādhu is the syllable sa.
oṃ is Vajraratna;
Vajratejas is the syllable a;
trāṃ is Vajraketu;
Vajrahāsa is the syllable ha.
The syllable hrīḥ is Vajranetra;
Vajratīkṣṇa is the syllable dhoṃ;
oṃ is Vajracakra;
Vajrabhāṣa is the syllable sa.
The syllable kaṃ is Vajrakarma;
Vajrarakṣa is the syllable haṃ;
hūṃ is Vajrayakṣa;
Vajramuṣṭi is the syllable paṃ.
Whichever one desires,
one should meditate on it likewise at the heart.
The yogin of the syllable hūṃ
will swiftly be accomplished.
Through reciting it twenty-one times
one should emanate a single form.
Through merely reciting the syllable hūṃ
the awakening of a buddha will be attained.
Abiding in the center of the moon disc,
one should meditate on one's own self.
One's self in the form of a vajra:
one should meditate on a single vajra.
At the hub in the center of the vajra,
there one should meditate on the syllable hūṃ.
Following the Great Vehicle, fully compiled,
just as in the sūtra,
just as the king with his men,
with the syllable hūṃ placed first,
so with every mind, the yogin
should meditate at the heart.
One's self in the form of a vajra,
one should meditate on the syllable hūṃ in the center.
Likewise all the syllables too
the yogin meditates upon at the heart.
Abiding properly from the form of the moon,
one should meditate fully on oneself,
and through the application of the deity of the family
one should meditate on one's own self.
Whoever Vajrasattva may be,
whatever one may truly desire,
one should meditate on that family at the heart,
joining the syllable hūṃ first,
because realization arises from it.
So with every mind, the yogin,
having meditated at the heart, should meditate.
Or else, through the application of space,
one joins the syllable hūṃ first;
one should meditate on the nature of space
and join all the syllables.
Or else, in the center of a moon in space,
one should meditate on the syllable hūṃ abiding there,
and think of oneself as the goddess of the family:
one is fully accomplished in that very instant.
As hūṃ has been taught,
one should meditate fully on all the mantras.
The secret of all the vidyās
is the compendium of all the tantras.
Apart from that secret of recitation,
this secret is of six kinds:
the essence of the vidyādharas,
the compendium of the yoga tantras,
the meaningful, the exceedingly wondrous,
and what is taught as the supreme secret, beyond even the secret.
Through the application of mere recitation
the mountain of views will be destroyed.
In the center of one's own heart
one should meditate on a moon disc.
In the center of that moon disc
one should meditate on a second moon.
Whatever breath goes out and is drawn in,
one meditates on it as the vajra syllable hūṃ.
Through the application of churning fire
one should make it move upward and downward.
Abiding properly in the moon disc,
one should meditate on the syllable hrīḥ in the center.
One should meditate on it moving upward and downward,
having joined the syllable hūṃ with it.
Just as with a dry base board for kindling fire,
when the fire drill is properly placed upon it,
by merely making the effort,
fire arises in an instant,
so the syllable a is the base board
and the syllable hūṃ is the fire drill;
through the yogin who makes them move,
wisdom is born in an instant.
The wisdom of the Perfection of Wisdom
should here be declared the syllable hrīḥ.
The mind of awakening is the syllable hūṃ;
one should churn it through the application of fire.
Through this application of churning
holy wisdom will be born.
That vajra is to be burned:
the unsurpassed vajra wisdom.
The bhaga is the wisdom of all the buddhas;
the liṅga should be declared the vajra.
Whatever is the mind of awakening, that is the vajra;
one practices fully in bhaga and liṅga.
Having inserted the liṅga into the bhaga:
thus it is taught in the Sūtra of the Lord of Secrets.
Through this application of the bhaga
the learned one should insert the liṅga.
This secret of great splendor:
if someone practices it
through the application of not churning,
wisdom is born in an instant.

The Seven Kinds of Secret Recitation are complete.

The great tantra, hidden and secret:
this very one is gathered into all the yogas.
This very great compendium
I shall further explain in full.
Abiding properly on the full moon,
one should meditate on oneself as a vajra.
In its center is the vajra,
and one should perform the recitation of the vajra family.
Abiding on the full moon,
one should meditate on oneself as a lotus.
In its center is that lotus,
and one should recite all of the lotus family.
Abiding on the full moon,
one should meditate on oneself as a gem.
In its center is the jewel,
and one should recite the jewel family.
Oneself as a single vajra
one should meditate on fully as a sharp vajra;
on that supreme karma family
one should recite all of the karma family.
Beginning with the recollection of the Buddha,
one should meditate on the form of the Buddha.
Having entered his heart,
one should recite all of the buddha family.
The great form of Vajrasattva,
the single being, one should meditate upon.
Having entered his heart,
one should recite all of the vajra family.
The form of the great lotus being,
Lokeśvara, one should meditate upon.
Having entered his heart,
one should recite all of the lotus family.
The gem, the jewel, of great fortune,
Vajraratna, one should meditate upon.
Having entered his heart,
one should recite all of the jewel family.
The great being Vajrakarma,
the form of that being, one should meditate upon.
Having entered his heart,
one should recite all of the karma family.
Truth, become the supreme pledge,
fully liberates all the buddhas.
By merely hearing it, the great means.
Or else, at the heart, of vajra nature,
the Lord of the Various, equal to the recitation:
whatever the wise one has thought of,
all excellences are attained with ease.
I shall correctly explain the secret.
One should meditate on the form of the Buddha,
and enter his heart;
having made all enter by means of the mind,
one radiates his body as an equal.
In the center of his heart
the vajra family is fully taught.
Following just as before,
one radiates all the Tathāgatas.
Through application of this kind,
if one recites the Tathāgata family,
without doubt all the buddhas
will be accomplished in that very instant.
This vajra arrow is peaceful;
one should meditate on a form like this.
Having entered its heart,
one should meditate on it as equal to the vajra dance.
Through application of this kind,
for all those of the vajra family,
without doubt all accomplishments
are accomplished through the recitation of wisdom.
One should meditate on the lotus being
and enter its heart as before.
If one meditates fully on the various means,
those of the lotus family will be accomplished.
One should meditate on Vajraratna
and enter his heart as before;
having entered the treasury of jewels,
one should radiate again and again.
Through application of this kind,
Vajraratna, the great joy:
without doubt all accomplishments
are accomplished through mere recitation.
Here in this world of the trichiliocosm,
one should think of it as Vajrakarma.
Having entered his heart,
one should radiate various emanations.
Through application of this kind,
having fully radiated the various treasuries,
without doubt all accomplishments
are accomplished through mere recitation.
These five, exceedingly secret,
are the unsurpassed secret of recitation.
Through application of this kind
the awakening of a buddha will be attained.
This application of the rite of recitation
gathers the essence of all the tantras
and utterly destroys all afflictions;
the vidyādhara should recite it.
Through this application of recitation
one must certainly take up the observance of vidyā.
If one desires accomplishment from this,
the yogin should keep the observance.
In the rite of recitation and fire offering
one should exert oneself in the supreme yoga.
The Compendium of the Great Vehicle
one should recite in full thirty million times.
Having recited it thirty million times,
one should begin the practices.
The mind of awakening is the great joy;
the wise one should draw the maṇḍala.
Vajrasattva, the great king,
should be drawn properly by a single person,
because the supreme king, the Lord of Secrets,
bestows the supreme vajra.
Through the application of the vajra realm,
that should properly be begun first.
The wise one should draw the maṇḍala;
one should draw it properly ten cubits across,
square, with four doors,
adorned with porticoes,
properly furnished with eight pillars,
adorned with tassels of silk,
properly furnished with four lines,
beautifully decorated with ornamental borders,
draped all around with nets of jewels,
adorned with golden threads, bells and small bells.
At the door frames and the corners
it is bound firmly with vajra bonds,
and vajra jewels are fully arranged.
The lines are laid with the vajra cord,
with the rite of applying perfume;
the obstructors should be dispelled with ritual daggers,
and the cord of five colors
should be protected by the vajra secret.
Through the application of the vajra realm
one should lay the lines of the outer maṇḍala.
Just as was explained before,
the vajra secret is fully arranged.
Having fully arranged the vajra secret,
one should lay the lines of the outer maṇḍala.
The beautiful Vajracakra
one should place in the center of the wheel,
and place Vajrarāga all around.
One should fully distinguish the five colors,
just as is taught in the secret tantra:
white, blazing with various lights,
blue and yellow, brightly blazing,
fully furnished with madder and the rest,
endowed with various red rays of light.
One should draw with various colors,
blazing with various red rays of light.
Having fully arranged the Vajracakra,
it is fully arranged upon the full moon.

Taking up all the wheels,
with their colors just as is fitting,
beautiful and exceedingly white,
just like the moon when it is full,
one should draw the full moon.
In the center of the full moon
one should draw Vajrasattva,
by holding whose name
one is freed from evil in an instant:
smiling, the color of the moon,
intensely passionate, in great joy.
Swiftly one should utter the vajra words;
this is the heart of the vajra itself:
oṃ mahāsukha vajrasatva hūṃ jaḥ hūṃ vaṃ hoḥ
One should sing the vajra song:
"Great conduct of the bodhisattva,
bestower of the awakening of a buddha,
fully mindful of the passion of desire,
loving all, possessed of the supreme mind of bliss,
Vajrasattva, supreme in perfect joy,
O you who bring increase by teaching great bliss,
please bring the practice to accomplishment most swiftly!"
Singing such a vajra song,
one should invite the wheel.
Vajrasattva, the great king,
will be loving and grant full accomplishment.
Seated in the posture of the sattva,
supreme in bearing the vajra air of pride,
his eyes moving playfully,
of great proud bearing and great joy,
with his right hand the vajra
he supremely holds at his heart;
the left is furnished with a bell,
and again he has a proud bearing.
"Bodhisattva, great vajra,
great king, great sage,
great conduct, great vehicle,
Vajrasattva, grant me accomplishment!
Great jewel, great vajra,
great banner, great mindfulness,
mindfulness of the buddhas, great vajra,
Vajrasattva, grant me accomplishment!
Great passion, great bliss,
unsurpassed great bliss,
blissful in the grounds and the perfections,
Vajrasattva, grant me accomplishment!
Great wisdom, great vajra,
arisen from Vajrahūṃkāra,
mind of awakening, great vajra,
vajra mind, great vajra,
Vajrasattva, grant me accomplishment!
Great vajra, great passion,
great king, great sage,
Amogharāja, supreme vajra,
Vajrasattva, grant me accomplishment!
Existence of the Dharma, great wheel,
unsurpassed act of speech,
protecting, great fierce protection,
Vajramuṣṭi, grant me accomplishment!"
When with this vajra song
one has supremely urged him,
Vajrasattva, the great king,
will be accomplished; of this there is no doubt.
Great passion, great joy:
one should meditate on Vajrasattva,
and the wise one should lay out
the maṇḍala of Vajrasattva, the great king.
In the same way as the previous maṇḍala,
one should draw it in the maṇḍala.
Then, gazing at the vajra,
one should recite the hundred and eight names.
The holder of the knowledge of the Dharma
should perform all the rites,
causing descent, entering, and the rest,
by the method of the Vajradhātu.
The vase and the other implements,
supreme incense, flowers, and so on:
by the method of the Vajradhātu
one should perform all the rites.
If one recites the vidyā of Vajrasattva
over the vase a hundred and eight times
and places it at the feet,
it becomes the water of vajra nectar.
By the method of the Vajradhātu
one should enter according to the rite,
and with the great mudrā of Vajrasattva
one should empower oneself.
Having fully entered with hūṃ,
one should worship with vajra offerings.
Through the samādhi of Vajrasattva
one should accomplish as is fitting.
Abiding in the full disc of the moon,
one should take up the great mudrā;
through the samādhi of Vajrasattva
one should accomplish one's very self.
Then the secret great mudrā
one should accomplish instant by instant.
Vajrasattva, the great king,
will become stable; of this there is no doubt.
Having accomplished the secret yoga,
the unsurpassed, thoroughly taught,
the loving great sattva
one should accomplish in an instant.
Such is the supremely secret and consummate,
the supreme complete purity of the sattva, the great king;
this mode of purity has nothing in the least to equal it,
the supreme complete purity of loving passion.
The supreme vajra king, with stainless enclosures,
holds the sublime wheel arisen from the secret;
this way of the secret mantra of knowledge brings awakening,
practicing the conduct endowed with compassion.
Likewise it has gone beyond great and terrifying suffering;
supremely skilled and subtle, arisen from tantra upon tantra,
there it cuts the great net of existences.
Thus is explained the secret arisen from the wheel of secret mantra.
It is the wheel arisen supremely from the secret family,
the single intent of all the Tathāgatas.
The chief of the sons, the sovereign king,
has thereby fully taught the city of liberation.

From the Vajraśekhara, the king of ritual of the secret great yoga, the supremely secret, sublime reality: the explanation called The Tathāgata Family, Lord of the Secret Sūtras of All the Tathāgatas, the second chapter, is complete.

Then the great secret yoga,
the gathering together of all the buddhas,
the supreme stability of the dharmadhātu,
endowed with the various substances of space,
the great king, the lord of mountains,
emanated the emanations of the buddhas.
In the abode of the great Vajra Lord
all the buddhas fully gathered.
At the summit of Vajrarāja
the secret yoga fully arose.
Vajradhara, the great king,
equal to the Dharma and to perfect enjoyment,
made utterly beautiful by all the vajras,
in that abode that had become supreme,
Vajradhara, the great king,
the yogin, remained in silence.
He sat in the posture of the sattva;
by the Chief all dharmas come under control.
Playful, with the vajra air of pride,
Vajrasattva of great joy,
the single abode of all the buddhas,
the supreme yoga of the great Dharma.
All the buddhas, having gathered,
supplicated the Lord of Sages:
"Vajra yoga, great yoga,
great joy of the bodhisattvas,
sattva arisen from the mind of awakening,
please explain the arising of your own family!
Vajradhara, the king,
the great secret, the heart of the tantra,
the great secret, the lord of the family,
the sūtras' Lord of Secrets, the king,
the Compendium of the Great Vehicle:
through peace awakening will be accomplished,
yet that does not fully gather all.
Utterly conquering all the fierce ones,
please always explain your own family!"
Vajradhara, the king,
remained entirely silent.
Silent, he did not speak the secret;
he remained in the bliss of peace.
The secret, supreme Tathāgata,
the great sattva, entered into equipoise;
neither conceiving nor looking,
he remained in the secret yoga.
All the buddhas, having gathered,
made the dharmadhātu stable;
they truly became like a single buddha,
the unsurpassed Amoghasiddhi.
The great yoga of all the buddhas
arose fully from all the secrets.
Through the samādhi of vajra union
they spoke an exhortation to the Vajra Bearer:
oṃ vajrasatva uttiṣṭha
When the vajra exhortation had been spoken,
the dharmadhātu, the one and only buddha,
through the samādhi of the secret yoga,
Vajrasattva, rose up.
The Sage bowed to the protectors
and spoke these words:
"What shall I do in every way?
Let all the buddhas tell me."
Then to steadfast Vajrapāṇi
all the buddhas spoke:
"Please explain the maṇḍala of the family
called the secret vajra family."
Having heard the speech of all the buddhas,
he who illumines the abode of the Vajra Lord
bowed deeply and spoke these words:
"Protectors, please behold with yoga!
Among these there is no sentient being at all
who is a supreme and fit vessel for me.
For they are attached to the non-Buddhists (tīrthikas),
beings pervaded by the stains of views.
These sentient beings are not to be trained;
they are not vessels for the Dharma of the buddhas.
They have fully entered wrong paths,
bent upon thick darkness.
Some are attached to regarding gods and humans;
some dwell in views of ascetic vows;
some are deluded, tormented by austerities;
their minds are agitated by afflictions.
Attached to the five qualities of desire,
they do not even become weary;
to this secret of the vajra family
they will never be joined.
They are attached to great desire,
overcome by the passions of desire;
free of attachment to the awakening of the buddhas,
they will not be joined to this secret.
They are overcome by attachment to views;
through the passion of desire they hold views;
they are tightly bound by the noose of existence;
they have no attachment to the Buddha's teaching.
Bound by pride and by arrogance beyond pride,
vicious, fierce, and extremely wrathful,
they become without compassion and without love:
for them the secret is not fitting.
For the deluded who think they are omniscient,
who think themselves skilled in great wisdom,
this secret is not fitting.
Guide, how shall I explain it?
Yakṣas and rākṣasas,
piśācas, bhūtas, guhyakas,
gods and the rest, the greatly vicious,
will not enter the teaching.
I am a buddha, utterly at peace,
the mind of awakening, great joy;
these sentient beings are extremely vicious:
my words will not be accomplished.
I, the great victor, in the Great Vehicle
am vast as the expanse of space;
alas, these sentient beings are inferior:
my words will not be accomplished.
I am the pure Great Vehicle,
unsullied by the mire of afflictions;
alas, these sentient beings are afflicted:
these words of mine will not be accomplished.
I am great wisdom and gnosis,
thoroughly taught in the Great Vehicle;
alas, sentient beings have poor wisdom:
these words of mine will not be accomplished.
Great merit, great gnosis,
I am a host of great offerings;
alas, sentient beings have little merit:
these words of mine will not be accomplished.
These sentient beings do harm,
everywhere pervaded by various fears,
stirred by concepts, poor in wisdom;
they are not vessels for the Dharma of the buddhas.
In this world of the trichiliocosm,
the great lord Īśvara, too,
has become the very dispeller of all delusion,
of great power and great magical might.
He has a king who acts as his ally,
who wholly holds the mantras of illusion;
he is called Human Lineage,
devoted to deluding sentient beings.
He is the king, the Great God (Śiva),
equal to the chief, the great king;
he is called Maheśvara (Śiva),
destroyer of vicious beings.
The great king who has his form,
the generator, the maker, the lord,
the great Brahmā, the great king,
bestows the various views.
The great king of the desire realm
is called Indra, lord of Śacī.
The vigorous son of Maheśvara,
Ṣaṇmukha (Kārttikeya), of great magical power,
has the great name Mahāsena,
and the names Kumāra, the Invincible, and Five Lights.
These five great kings
are declared to be the lords of the three realms.
The five kings of great splendor,
of the three bodies, of great wonder:
the instigator Maheśvara,
Brahmā, Viṣṇu, and Maheśvara;
the great king who has his body
is renowned as Maheśvara,
a being vicious and extremely fierce.
Those five kings,
with their mantras and their retinues,
utterly delude sentient beings.
They hold great views
and have come under the power of Māra;
they are utterly afflicted by great afflictions,
and they also thoroughly afflict others.
They destroy sentient beings:
these words of mine will not be accomplished.
Maheśvara, the great self,
the great mind of the sublime self,

Through conceptual thought such as this
they utterly delude sentient beings.
Brahmā, the producer, lord of creatures,
the maker, the self, the creator:
through great views such as this
they utterly delude sentient beings.
Viṣṇu, the all-going, the all-pervader,
all cities having the nature of Viṣṇu:
not grasping things correctly in this way,
they bestow the bondage of views.
These kings, far and wide,
surely teach the doctrines of views;
deluded, they think themselves wise,
and they bind sentient beings.
Those sentient beings, urged on by others,
surely rely on the proponents of views.
Viṣṇu, Brahmā, and the rest
pay homage to Maheśvara.
So in every way, O gnosis of the buddhas,
these words of mine do not succeed.
These sentient beings have become deluded;
for them the secret is not fitting."
In a manner such as this
Vajrasattva of great joy,
the yogin, remained in silence,
all the buddhas having understood.
The great protectors, all the buddhas,
the sages, the suns, every one of them,
with a single voice spoke
the great secret, the vajra words:
"Alas, these sentient beings are hard to tame,
vicious, fierce, and extremely savage;
they are not tamed by all the buddhas;
therefore we supplicate you.
Protector, you are great wisdom;
you must speak the great means.
The mind of awakening is the great means;
it surely conquers all afflictions.
You who thoroughly tame all the vicious,
Protector, we supplicate you!
Magical display who tames the vicious,
you who fully proclaim the great means:
these sentient beings, who are not vessels,
will not be tamed by all the buddhas;
so the great means, the great wrath,
please, Great Sage, teach it!"
Having heard the words of all the buddhas,
Vajrapāṇi, of great magical power,
requested with these words,
spoke thus to all the buddhas:
"How wonderful! The great secret wheel,
the great means, the great self,
the very substance of the teaching of the dharmadhātu,
fully showing the wheel of means:
just as you have commanded,
so I shall do.
For the benefit of all sentient beings
I shall display fierceness itself."
Having spoken thus, the great sattva,
the bodhisattva, the great self,
remaining silent, through yoga
entered into equipoise in fierceness itself.
The samādhi called
"Supreme Vajra, the Fierce Maṇḍala":
in such a samādhi the great fierce one entered equipoise.
Then from his heart there came forth
the fierce and terrifying syllable hūṃ,
which uttered the great wrathful one.
Vajrahūṃkāra, the wrathful king,
came forth from the Protector's heart.
The whole surface of the earth shook,
and these grounds resounded with sound;
the whole of this trichiliocosm
uttered the words "hūṃ hūṃ."
When the measureless, immeasurable
kings of wrath had come forth,
the greatly vicious in every direction
in that instant turned savage.
Then, becoming one once again,
massed into a single lump and tightly compressed,
the king of wrath, of great body,
became one terrifying being.
He, the great king of wrath,
the great renown of all the buddhas,
the wrathful king, sitting before them,
once again fully uttered his heart mantra:
hūṃ tya hūṃ tya
Making the hair stand on end with terror,
the yakṣa, lord of all yakṣas,
Vajrayakṣa, of great magical power:
this is the great wrathful one who was uttered.
This world of the trichiliocosm
was pervaded by the light of blazing garlands;
the vajra fire, greatly fierce,
shone everywhere and burst into flame.
The vicious ones outside, every one,
by the vajra fire arisen from the maṇḍala
fully blazing,
became motionless and died.
The vicious, the Great God and the rest,
whatever maṇḍalas of the families there were,
tormented by Vajrayakṣa,
became motionless and burned.
He, the great Vajrayakṣa,
of fierce form, utterly terrifying,
with a face wrinkled in a wrathful frown,
turned black and blue.
He has a mouth with bared fangs,
his hair stands on end, most frightening;
he has red eyes,
and likewise a face displaying the vajra.
The fierce one holds the vajra and the fierce hosts;
alone, he is fully ablaze;
lord of noose, hook, and vajra,
terrifying all the buddhas.
He makes all the grounds shake;
he makes this earth, too, shake.
The great yakṣas, bhūtas, and the rest,
motionless, fell down beneath.
He, the fierce great yakṣa,
terrifies all the buddhas.
Remaining before him,
they fully uttered this inspired utterance:
"How wonderful! The mind of awakening,
Samantabhadra, wholly virtuous,
as a means to tame sentient beings
has become a pitiless wrathful one.
He who formerly was Vajrasattva,
abiding in a peaceful form,
having taken up Vajrayakṣa,
is renowned as Fierce Vajra."
He, the fierce Vajradhara,
terrifying, making the hair stand on end,
also spoke the savage heart mantra
of the mudrā of the four syllables hūṃ:
oṃ sumbhani sumbha hūṃ gṛhṇa gṛhṇa hūṃ gṛhṇāpaya gṛhṇāpaya hūṃ ānaya ho bhagavāna vajra hūṃ phaṭ
Extremely savage and greatly renowned,
there likewise came forth from his heart
one four-faced and eight-armed,
fierce like the fire at the end of the eon.
He came forth like the vajra fire of time,
fully dispelling the side of the afflictions,
his brows knitted, his mouth with bared fangs,
and likewise his face utterly terrifying.
The samaya that burns the three worlds,
he burns all the vajra bearers;
with the sound of blazing garlands massing everywhere,
he blazes like the vajra fire of the eon.
The mudrā of Vajrahūṃkāra
he holds fully with his two hands.
The king holding the vajra bond,
adorned with hook and noose,
with a vajra chain
bound in two hands, his face terrifying,
threatening all the buddhas,
Vajracaṇḍa fully came forth.
He receives the fierce command:
"Give your command swiftly!
Otherwise I shall without doubt
devour all of you buddhas."
Then all the buddhas spoke:
"ṭakki jaḥ hūṃ, utterly fierce one,
with your great power summon
the Great God together with his family!"
Then the supreme king of knowledge,
laughing with an air of pride,
the fierce wrathful one, roaring like a drum,
is to exhort Vajra Noose:
"Vajra Noose, great fierce one,

together with his army and his allies,
and likewise with his family and his maṇḍala,
go and summon Maheśvara!"
vajrapāja hūṃ
The mighty great king
dwelt amid his various retinues;
together with the Terrifying Lady,
the lustful one was supremely sporting.
Vajra Noose went there
and threatened him with a wrathful sound.
He said to the mighty king,
"The Teacher invites you."
Frightened and enraged, the Great God
uttered terrifying words:
"I am the king of the three worlds;
is there any teacher other than me?"
Vajra Noose, the great wrathful one,
spoke the words three times:
"The vajra wrathful one, the great king,
earnestly invites you."
The Great God, Maheśvara,
again spoke three times:
"I alone am the king himself;
who is there to invite me?"
Then the Noose did as he was commanded.
The fierce wrathful one, utterly terrifying,
binding all his limbs,
summoned Maheśvara.
Fearful and frightened, the Great God
lamented loudly and wept:
"There is no refuge at all;
great terror has arisen everywhere."
With strength and likewise with pride,
displaying his strength and vigor,
he cut the noose and fled.
Having gone to his own abode,
he called the gods of the three worlds.
Gods and asuras, together with gandharvas,
gathered in an instant,
and the retinues wept:
"Such a thing as was never done before
we have truly seen.
Who is this extremely fierce being?"
Vajra Noose went before
the supreme Vajra Knowledge and reported:
"Maheśvara cut me loose,
and Rudra and Brahmā have fled."
The supreme king of vajra knowledge,
the wrathful, savage, extremely fierce one,
having heard words such as these,
blazed up on every side.
To the wrathful Vajra Chain
the fierce one gave his command:
"Go swiftly and summon the Great God
together with his great family!"
The king of wrath, Vajra Chain,
having heard, went to Maheśvara's abode;
binding all his limbs,
he summoned him wholly, together with the goddess.
In an instant they wept with terror,
and all the gods were utterly afraid;
in the suffering of fear they lamented:
"Today the Great God is no guide!"
With all their strength and vigor
they fought against all the vajras;
by might, strength, and vigor
they broke free, and all of them fled.
The Great God and all the rest
fled in every direction.
Everything was thrown into turmoil by darkness;
in every direction they were terrified.
The wrathful Vajra Chain came back
and told him everything:
"Into all directions without exception
they have fled from our abode."
The wrathful one, the supreme Vajra Knowledge,
once again became extremely enraged;
fully ablaze with blazing garlands,
he became Fierce Vajra himself.
Fiercely laughing "ha ha,"
he devours his own limbs,
he strikes his own head,
and he blazes, whirling and brandishing the vajra.
With distinctive features such as these
the wrathful one fully arose.
Again recollecting his own self,
the king of wrath uttered:
oṃ sumbhani sumbha hūṃ gṛhṇa gṛhṇa hūṃ gṛhṇāpaya gṛhṇāpaya hūṃ ānaya ho bhagavān vajra hūṃ phaṭ
Joined with the king of wrath,
he fully uttered the syllable jaḥ
and fully dispatched Vajra Hook:
"Mighty one, go in fury!"
oṃ vajra jvala agni satriṣa aṃkuśa jaḥ jaḥ
The blazing Vajra Hook,
the great self, went in every direction,
and in an instant the mighty ones,
all the gods, were summoned.
The five kings, the lords,
and all the gods beyond counting,
together with their families and armies,
were gathered into one.
He fully dispatched Vajra Chain:
"Bind them, you of great strength!"
All the gods with their hosts
were bound with the five bonds.
The hosts with their inner retinues,
the hosts of gods, yakṣas, and rākṣasas,
asuras, and the hosts of wind spirits and bhūtas,
and the other hosts of the vicious,
were all of them bound.
He fully dispatched Vajra Noose:
"Together with Vajra Hook,
go and summon the maṇḍalas!"
From Vajra Noose, in passion,
nooses streamed forth from all his limbs;
the noose whose nature is vajra
became firm, without any doubt.
Ten countless ten millions
of vajra hooks fully came forth;
to each of the gods and the others
they fastened the summoning at the crown of the head.
Having fastened onto the crown of the head
of the Great God, Maheśvara,
by Vajra Hook, the king,
Maheśvara is summoned.
All the gods and the rest, with their retinues,
weep to one another;
heaving long sighs on every side,
they speak to one another:
"Alas, suffering! Alas, death!
Where is our host being carried off?"
Burned in that instant
by that great blazing vajra,
all the gods and the rest were summoned
to the summit of Mount Meru, the mountain.
There in that place they all
remained, surrounding it on every side.
Outside the vajra city
all those gods remained.
Outside the vajra fence
all the gods and the rest were summoned.
The outer vajra fence
is like a vajra whose nature is red-hot iron;
by the blazing vajras of the Vajra Bearer
they were intensely tormented from within.
Those hosts of gods, those beings, too,
cried out with terrifying wails;
all of this trichiliocosm, too,
fully uttered the sound "hūṃ hūṃ."
Crying out, they spoke:
"What are these evil destinies?
What is this, never seen before?
Here there will be Vajradhara!"
In Vajradhara, that king,
compassion arose once again on every side.
He released them all from their bonds,
beautiful with the vajra fire.
Vajrayakṣa, the great fierce one,
came out from his own maṇḍala
and called all the gods and the rest:
"Listen well to these words of mine!"
Then the kings of the gods and the rest
said, "Who are you?
We have never seen you before,
nor have we seen this supreme place before."
Vajrayakṣa, the great fierce one,
laughing "ha ha," said:
"I am Vajrayakṣa,
the devourer of all, hard to bear."
Then the Great God said:
"I do not know Vajrayakṣa;
I have never seen you before.
I alone am self-arisen."
Fierce Vajra declared:

"Abide swiftly by my command!
Otherwise, with this blazing vajra
I shall burn up your own family."
The Great God spoke these words:
"I do not abide by any teaching.
I am Īśvara, lord of the three worlds;
whose teaching should I abide by?
Whatever strength you have, with your retinue,
today I shall show you mine.
What strength do you have?
You who have great strength, show it!"
Maheśvara, the great king,
together with his hosts of bhūtas,
having spoken these words,
was about to display his fierceness.
This world of the trichiliocosm
was pervaded by garlands of blazing light rays,
and with an utterly fierce "ha ha"
Maheśvara set about displaying it:
oṃ śūla atriśūla mahāsahasraśūla āyudhe
The king who wields the trident
brought forth a thousand of them;
with their prongs blazing on every side
they fought against the Vajra Bearer.
Nārāyaṇa said:
oṃ mahāsahasracakra āyudhe
When Viṣṇu had spoken these words,
a single wheel became a thousand forms,
all of them pervaded by blazing rays of light,
and they fought against the Vajra Bearer.
Śakra said:
oṃ mahāsahasravajra āyudhe
A thousand vajras came forth,
the blazing vajras pervaded by heat;
it was Śakra who had spoken,
and they fought against the Vajra Bearer.
Kumāra said:
oṃ mahāsahasraśakṣya āyudhe
When Kumāra had spoken thus,
a thousand three-pointed spears came forth;
roaring with a fierce sound,
they fought against the Vajra Bearer.
Brahmā said:
oṃ mahāsahasradaṇḍa āyudhe
When Brahmā had spoken thus,
a thousand staffs came forth,
and with fierce and terrifying weapons
they fought against the Vajra Bearer.
And all the others who remained,
every one of them, their weapons too
each becoming a thousand,
fought against the Vajra Bearer.
Even whatever emanations there were,
and the outsiders bound by oath,
each surely becoming a thousand,
fought against the Vajra Bearer.
Fire too blazed on every side,
and a terrifying wind arose;
thunder roared and great lightning flashed,
pervading in particular the three realms of existence.
A great din of "hala hala"
resounded through the sky and the intermediate space.
The great host of Fierce Vajra
and all the gods were locked in battle.
Then the king, Vajradhara,
savage, fierce, and terrifying,
spoke the vajra heart mantras,
the mudrās that answer the gods:
oṃ mahāsahasraharavajracakre
oṃ mahāvajraśūle
oṃ dībtajvālagnivajra
oṃ vajraśakti
oṃ mahāvajranidhi
In this manner,
a thousand heart mantras
were brought forth by the great Vajradhara
in order to pacify the three realms of existence.
Savage, pervaded by blazing rays of light,
fully endowed with vajra fire,
they arose from the fierce heart mantras,
and Vajradhara sat in the center.
Those mudrās of the hosts of gods
were utterly demolished and broken into pieces;
crushed, compressed, and mixed together,
they dissolved in every direction.
Tightly bound by the vajra fire,
the kings of the gods burst into flames.
From beneath, the ground blazed fiercely;
from above, the fire tormented them intensely.
The great king, Vajra Wrath,
said to Maheśvara:
"You of putrid body, great fierce one,
listen well to my command!
You who delight in dwelling in the charnel ground,
whose body is smeared with ashes,
who eat human flesh,
listen well to my command!
You who are thoroughly smeared with fresh blood,
who wear a fresh hide as your garment,
whose retinue is all the gods,
listen well to my command!
Vicious and greatly fierce, night-roaming beggar,
wearing a hide soaked in blood, your body smeared with ashes,
hear my command, you of fierce mind and putrid body!
Otherwise I shall burn you like grass with the fire of the eon's end,
and you will become ash, like a heap of ashes from a burned corpse.
With your family, your army, and your maṇḍala, fierce yakṣa,
you will be utterly destroyed and demolished by Fierce Vajra."
Maheśvara said:
"I am Maheśvara, the wheel-turning monarch of all;
the hosts of the gods of the three worlds, every one, are my retinue.
Though you are extremely fierce, grimacing, of the world,
you should listen to my command.
The vicious and the retinues of many buddhas, the source of the buddhas' Dharma, I have emanated.
Peaceful, and in that the taming, the secret, stainless, gone beyond to the absence of affliction:
such a protector, joyful, is empowered by all who abide in the three times.
Again, listen to the words of my bhūtas."
Such were his words.
Then the king, Vajradhara,
supremely skilled in the aim of taming,
the wrathful, vicious, great savage one,
the fully blazing king of wrath,
from the mudrā of vajra blessing
entered into equipoise in a samādhi
of such activity; and the great sattva,
in order to tame the vicious,
taking up the way of the Dharma of wrath,
brought forth from his whole body,
starting from his feet up to the crown of his head,
wrathful ones arisen from his own family.
oṃ vajrakrodhauṣṇiṣa hūṃ
Vajroṣṇīṣa, the great wrathful one,
having come forth from his blazing crown,
makes all the grounds shake
and conquers all the kings of wrath.
oṃ vajrabhṛkuṭikrodha hūṃ
The king of wrath with the wrathful frown
came forth from the ūrṇā hair between the brows,
fully endowed with terrifying fierceness,
setting all bodies ablaze.
oṃ nisumbhavajra hūṃ
The supreme king of vajra knowledge
came forth from the mouth,
fully endowed with blazing vajras
from the crown of the head down to the soles of the feet.
ṭakki hūṃ jaḥ
The great king of wrath of the heart
came forth from the heart,
his body pervaded by blazing garlands,
terrifying, making the hair stand on end.
oṃ vajraaṃkuśa ākarṣaya hūṃ
Vajra Hook, the great wrathful one,
came forth from the right shoulder,
brandishing a vajra hook,
fully endowed with blazing vajras.
oṃ vajravāśakrodha ākadḍhya hūṃ phaṭ
Vajra Noose, the great wrathful one,
came forth from the left shoulder,
fully endowed with blazing vajras,
encircling with a vajra noose.
oṃ vajrakīlikrodha hūṃ phaṭ
The king of wrath bearing a hook
came forth from the center of the navel,
fully endowed with blazing on every side,
equal to Vajrayakṣa of supreme intelligence.
oṃ vajramekhalākrodha hūṃ phaṭ
The great wrathful one, the Girdle,
the vajra of the waist, came forth,
fully endowed with blazing on every side.

endowed together with the mudrā.
oṃ vajra krodha bala hūṃ
The great king of wrath, Strength,
came forth from the right upper arm,
blazing with blazing vajras,
exceedingly proud with fire.
oṃ vajra balāya krodha hūṃ
The king of wrath, the Bracelet,
came forth from the left upper arm,
secret, and pervaded by blazing vajras,
filled completely with vajra wrath.
oṃ samaya vajra krodha hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ
The king of wrath, Samaya,
likewise the wrathful harm-doer,
came forth from the right fist,
fully endowed with blazing on every side.
oṃ vajra muṣṭi krodha hūṃ phaṭ
The wrathful Fist, greatly fierce,
came forth from the left fist,
brilliant with blazing vajras,
richly adorned with vajra fire.
oṃ vajra bandha krodha hūṃ samaya hūṃ
oṃ vajra añjali bandha krodha hūṃ samaya hūṃ
Vajra Binding, the great king,
and likewise Vajra Fingers
came forth from the fingers
of the wrathful one's right and left hands.
oṃ vajra nakha hūṃ
oṃ vajra krodha aṃkuli mahā krodha hūṃ
Vajra Claw, the great wrathful one,
and likewise Vajra Claw Face
came forth from the fingers
of the left and right hands.
oṃ vajra kumāra krodha kaṭi rāga hūṃ
oṃ vajra kumāra krodha murdha ḍha ka hūṃ phaṭ
The wrathful king of the Waist,
and likewise the wrathful one of the Crown,
kings, came forth
from the left and right thighs.
oṃ śṛṃ kha li mahā krodha bandha hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ vajra krodha āśāni bandha hūṃ
Thunderbolt and Vajra Chain,
wrathful kings of great miraculous power,
kings pervaded by garlands of flame,
came forth from the kneecaps.
oṃ vajra bandha krodha hana hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ vajra krodha aṃkuśa hūṃ phaṭ
Vajra Staff, the great wrathful one,
and the Vajra Limb who draws in,
these two yakṣas, together with their blaze,
came forth from the shins.
oṃ nūpura krodha hūṃ
oṃ vajra krodha mogha bega ānaya hūṃ phaṭ
Then Austerity, the great wrathful one,
and likewise Vajra Amogha himself,
these two kings of great might,
came forth from the ankles.
oṃ vajra bandha hala krodha hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ vajra pātāla māraya krodha hūṃ phaṭ
The Foot, the wrathful one of great might,
and likewise the wrathful one of the Sole,
intent on radiating blazing flames,
both came forth from the soles of the feet.
These and other kings
came forth beyond measure,
countless as the sands of the Ganges,
as the atomic particles of the elements.
From all the spaces between the body hairs
and from all the crevices of the body,
the great kings, the vajra wrathful ones,
came forth from their own heart mantras.
Through the samādhi of Vajra Wrath
they emanated throughout all directions,
and becoming one once again,
they took their places in the maṇḍala.
Assembled before all the buddhas,
they proclaimed this solemn utterance:
"How wonderful, the great skillful means of the buddhas!
How wonderful, wisdom most excellent!
The samādhi of all the buddhas
is declared to be the Great Vehicle.
How wonderful, all the buddhas
have come to be called immeasurable!
These wrathful ones of the vajra family
are all declared to be of the vajra family.
For the sake of benefiting all sentient beings,
that itself is the great buddha family."
Then their secret names,
all declared to be of the vajra family:
arisen from the great wrathful one,
the lord of vajra wrath is proclaimed;
Vajra Uṣṇīṣa, Vajra Ūrṇā,
Sumbha, the mighty one among us,
the Wrathful One of the Heart, the great king,
the King Cakravartin,
Vajra Hook, Vajra Noose,
the mighty Wrathful One of the Waist,
the Girdle and the Mighty One,
the Bracelet, and likewise Samaya,
Vajramuṣṭi, Vajra Binding,
the extremely fierce Vajra Fingers,
Vajra Nail, Vajra Face,
likewise the wrathful king of the Waist,
the wrathful king of the Crown,
the Chain of great miraculous power,
Vajra Thunderbolt, Vajra Staff,
the vajra wrathful Fierce Limb,
Vajra Anklet, Vajra Amogha,
Vajra Foot, and likewise the Sole:
these kings of the samaya vajra
are declared to be of the vajra family.
These and other kings,
great kings of great miraculous power,
arose from the mind of awakening,
and ever more, countless others besides,
in their fury devouring their own flesh,
abide with garlands of flame pervading the ground,
blazing like the fire at the end of the eon.
Vajra Wrath, having brought forth
the maṇḍala, the vajra family,
commanded the extremely fierce ones:
"Tame the malevolent ones,
and heed my command!"
All the vajra bearers, having received
his command, were to uphold it,
and with one voice all the deities
recited this vidyā:
oṃ ni sumbha vajra hūṃ phaṭ
As the mighty vajra wrathful ones,
Uṣṇīṣa and all the rest, spoke it,
the malevolent ones, the Great God and the rest,
fell down face downward.
When the gods had been made to faint
by the mighty Vajra Wrath,
with "Vajra blaze, blaze!"
they proclaimed the heart mantra:
oṃ vajra anala daha paca matha bhañja raṇa hūṃ phaṭ
The great wrathful one, Vajra Fire,
came forth from that in an instant,
and having burned those gods,
likewise cast them face down.
Burned intensely by vajra fire,
set ablaze by vajra flames,
scorched and cooked,
the gods and the rest cried out in anguish.
Those great vajra wrathful ones,
likewise the great wrathful ones and the rest,
all speaking with one voice,
once again spoke a heart mantra:
oṃ vajra āveśa a
The great wrathful one of Vajra Possession
came forth from the heart of the Vajra Bearer,
went to the abodes of all the gods,
and likewise descended
upon all the gods, the Great God and the rest.
Made to suffer terribly
by the vajra possession,
they devoured their own limbs,
each one devouring the other.
All, together with the Vajra Bearer,
recited the Vajra Noose:
oṃ vajra jvala agni pradīpta mahā vajra aṃkuśa ākarṣaya hūṃ
Vajra Hook, the king, went forth
and in an instant summoned them;
having summoned all the gods,
he made them fall upon their heads.
Summoned by the Vajra Hook,
drawn in by the Vajra Noose,
burned intensely by vajra flames,
feet up and heads down,
crying out "Ha ha!",
they suffered, with great and terrible cries.

They made supplication to the Vajra Bearer:
"Mighty One, do not destroy us!"
Likewise, all the gods and the rest
were summoned into the maṇḍala.
All, together with the Vajra Bearer,
recited the Vajra Chain:
oṃ vajra sphoṭa bandha hūṃ phaṭ
Those malevolent hosts of gods
were bound with the five bonds.
Vajra Chain, the great wrathful one,
bound them with firm bonds.
The wrathful one, the great Vajrarāja,
exceedingly ferocious, of great strength,
once again called the heart mantra to mind
and proclaimed his own heart mantra:
hūṃ
Through the uttering of the fierce heart mantra
those malevolent ones fell flat;
they became motionless, as if dead,
and came close to the suffering of death.
Then King Vairocana,
lord of all dharmas, endowed with power,
called out to Vajradhara,
the great king, Vajra Wrath:
"Great being, listen!
These ones terrify sentient beings,
yet this circle of the three worlds,
do not destroy their lives!"
Then King Vajradhara,
the powerful lord of all vajras,
having heard the Buddha's words,
recited this vidyā:
oṃ vajra uttiṣṭha
Vajra Raising, of great miraculous power:
by the mere uttering of it,
all the gods and the rest
came back to life once again.
They were freed from all suffering,
and the suffering of death was stilled.
Through the loving-kindness of Vajra Raising,
since all the buddhas had urged it on,
all the gods and the rest
rose up through the Buddha's loving-kindness.
Joining their palms, with a single voice
they made this request to the Blessed One:
"Blessed One, for what fault
do you tame us in this way?
May all the compassionate buddhas
please protect us completely!"
The Blessed One said:
"I am not able to protect you completely
or to benefit you.
This is the fierce Vajradhara,
declared by all the buddhas to be the master."
They said:
"We do not understand how this
can be fitting for you, the Blessed One.
Who is it that has become master of the buddhas?
What, then, is a noble buddha like?"
The Blessed One said:
"The awakening of all the buddhas:
from that he became the Vajra Bearer.
Whatever is the mind of awakening, that is the vajra;
whatever is the vajra holds the fierce form."
They said:
"All the buddhas are utterly peaceful;
that is the same as awakening,
arisen from love and the mind of awakening.
From where did such a fierce one arise?"
The Blessed One said:
"This is taming by skillful means;
it thoroughly tames all the malevolent.
This glorious Vajradhara
arose from the mind of awakening."
Then King Vajradhara,
Vajra Wrath, the great being,
called out to all the hosts of gods
and spoke to Maheśvara (Śiva):
"O malevolent ones, listen
to the commands of my teaching!
Otherwise, with blazing vajras
